# Mural Quest — All Artists (156 murals)

**St. Petersburg, FL — compiled April 18, 2026**

This document contains every artist bio and mural description in the Mural Quest app.
Use this as source material for marketing copy, press materials, and brochure writing.
See the Marketing Brief (docs/marketing-brief.md) for the voice/style card.

---

## Aaron Tullo (2025)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 253 2nd Ave N
**Building:** Cordova Inn
**Instagram:** @aaron.tullo

### Bio
The Milwaukee Brewers drafted him twice. He picked up a paintbrush instead.

Aaron Tullo was born in St. Petersburg in 1988 and grew up throwing fastballs.
He played at St. Petersburg High School, made second-team All-County as a
sophomore, and tried out for the USA Baseball Youth National Team at 16. The
Brewers took him in the 17th round of the 2006 draft — he turned them down
for college. They took him again in 2007 out of the University of Tennessee.
This time he signed.

He pitched in the Brewers' minor league system for roughly six years before
injuries ended it. He was around 25.

What came next was a pivot, not a restart. Tullo studied at St. Petersburg
College and the University of Tennessee (arts and sciences), and his eye for
composition carried over. He started with nursery murals and pet portraits,
then connected with the Vitale Brothers in Gulfport — working on their jobs,
learning the trade, exhibiting alongside them. His first major public piece
was a towering vintage pocket watch on Old Northeast Jewelers at 1131 4th
St N — a mural he still considers his best work.

His style moves between figurative realism, bold graphic design, and sign
painting. He letters by hand. He collaborates frequently with Ty Maner.
Clients include Old Northeast Jewelers, Plotnick Law (where he painted
Hemingway and Dali side by side), Fortunato's, and St. Pete Fishing
Outfitters. For a Tampa Heights law office, he painted an Elliott Smith
Figure 8-inspired piece with an MLK quote and a child's face.

For SHINE Origins 2025, he painted a five-story white peacock on the Cordova
Inn — the festival's title sponsor. It fills the building's entire facade and
reads from blocks away.

searchBio: |
The Milwaukee Brewers drafted him twice. He picked up a paintbrush instead.

Aaron James Tullo was born March 9, 1988 in St. Petersburg, Florida. He
attended St. Petersburg High School under coach Stefan Futch, made second-team
All-County as a sophomore in 2004, and was selected to try out for the USA
Baseball Youth National Team at age 16. A throwing arm injury in the summer of
2005 complicated things, but the Milwaukee Brewers still drafted him in the
17th round (512th overall) of the 2006 MLB Draft out of high school. He turned
them down and went to college.

At the University of Tennessee he pitched as a right-hander — 10 appearances
as a freshman in 2007 with a 3.72 ERA and 56 strikeouts in 36.1 innings. The
Brewers drafted him again in 2007, 47th round. He signed and entered their
minor league system. He pitched professionally for roughly six years before
injuries ended his baseball career around age 25. He also attended St.
Petersburg College and is described as a USF graduate.

Tullo operates AJT Art + Design (also Aaron James Tullo Fine Art) out of a
studio at 2411 15th Avenue North in St. Petersburg. He works in brush, spray
paint, airbrush, and stencil across styles including realism, pop art,
abstract, and typography. He was mentored by the Vitale Brothers, well-known
St. Pete muralists — he worked on their jobs and exhibited at their Gulfport
studio, including Renaissance-inspired paintings on wood at their "Birds and
Bees" show.

His first major public piece was the vintage pocket watch mural at Old
Northeast Jewelers / Hess Fine Art (1131 4th St N, St. Pete) in 2018 — a
work he considers his best. Other confirmed murals include the
Hemingway-and-Dali piece at Plotnick Law (1515 4th St N), a collage-style
mural at St. Pete Fishing Outfitters, work at Fortunato's in Pinellas Park,
and an Elliott Smith Figure 8-inspired mural with an MLK quote at Josh
Sheridan's law office in Tampa Heights. He collaborates frequently with Ty
Maner.

Fox 13 News profiled him in September 2023 ("Retired minor league baseball
player creates custom murals in Tampa Bay"). He has been covered by the
Northeast Journal, Creative Loafing Tampa Bay, and The Gabber.

For SHINE Origins 2025 — the festival's 11th year, focused on local Tampa Bay
artists — Tullo was one of 17 selected artists. He painted a five-story white
peacock on the facade of the Cordova Inn at 253 2nd Ave N, the festival's
title sponsor. The bird's plumage fills the building's vertical expanse,
creating a landmark visible from blocks away in downtown St. Petersburg.

muralDescription: |
This five-story mural on the facade of the Cordova Inn depicts a large white
peacock rendered in Tullo's characteristic blend of figurative realism and bold
graphic composition. The bird's plumage fills the building's vertical expanse,
creating a striking visual landmark in downtown St. Petersburg.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
This five-story mural on the facade of the Cordova Inn depicts a large white
peacock rendered in Tullo's characteristic blend of figurative realism and bold
graphic composition. The bird's plumage fills the building's vertical expanse,
creating a striking visual landmark in downtown St. Petersburg.

## Amy Ilic-Volpe — Here & Now (2025)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 31st St & 7th Ave S
**Instagram:** @amy_ilic_volpe

### Bio
Before she picked up a brush, she spent nearly a decade on cruise ships
entertaining children around the world. That sense of play never left.

Amy Ilic-Volpe is a Florida native now based in St. Petersburg. Her work is
process-driven and spontaneous — fluid color, layered geometric shapes, bold
palettes of pink, green, yellow, and turquoise that land somewhere between
playful and contemplative. She cites Hilma af Klint as an influence and
listens to Ani DiFranco in the studio.

She works with children through youth education and art classes, and calls
teaching her greatest achievement — "sparking wonder." Kids are her primary
artistic inspiration. She views her practice through "the excitement and
vitality of young eyes" and prioritizes fun over seriousness. Her current
collection, Keys, explores the mystery found in isolation and going within.

Recognition includes the 2022 Gasparilla Festival of the Arts Emerging Artist
award, a feature in All She Makes magazine, and a SHINE 2022 "Bright Spot"
mural. For SHINE Origins 2025, she returned with "Here & Now" — a candy-bright
explosion of circles, stripes, polka dots, and stacked block letters on 31st
Street and 7th Avenue South.

searchBio: |
Before she picked up a brush, she spent nearly a decade on cruise ships
entertaining children around the world. That sense of play never left.

Amy Ilic-Volpe is a Florida native and multimedia artist based in St.
Petersburg. Her work is process-driven and spontaneous — fluid color, layered
geometric shapes, bold palettes that land somewhere between playful and
contemplative. She allows materials to "speak" to her, describing painting as
"a visceral and emotional practice." She cites Hilma af Klint as an influence
and listens to Ani DiFranco in the studio. She advocates against
pretentiousness in the art world, calling for artists to "lift each other up."

She works with children through youth education and art classes, and calls
teaching her greatest achievement — "sparking wonder." Kids are her primary
artistic inspiration. She views her practice through "the excitement and
vitality of young eyes." Her current collection, Keys, explores "the
excitement and mystery that can be found in isolation and going within
ourselves." She uses meditation and intentional thought in her creative
practice.

Recognition includes the 2022 Gasparilla Festival of the Arts Emerging Artist
Recipient award, a feature in All She Makes magazine, and a SHINE St.
Petersburg "Bright Spot" mural in 2022. She has been profiled by Not Real Art
and FloridaRAMA / Fairgrounds St. Pete. For SHINE Origins 2025, she painted
"Here & Now" at 31st Street and 7th Avenue South — a wide mural on a cinder
block wall bursting with overlapping geometric shapes in a candy-bright
palette. Instagram: @amy_ilic_volpe.

muralDescription: |
A wide, vibrant mural on a cinder block wall bursting with overlapping
geometric shapes — circles, stripes, polka dots, and waves — in a candy-
bright palette of pink, green, yellow, orange, purple, and turquoise. The
left side features the words "Here & Now" in stacked multicolor block
letters. Signed "Amy Ilic Volpe @amy_ilic_volpe" in pink in the lower
right corner.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A wide, vibrant mural on a cinder block wall bursting with overlapping
geometric shapes — circles, stripes, polka dots, and waves — in a candy-
bright palette of pink, green, yellow, orange, purple, and turquoise. The
left side features the words "Here & Now" in stacked multicolor block
letters. Signed "Amy Ilic Volpe @amy_ilic_volpe" in pink in the lower
right corner.

## Brain The Genius — Culture (2025)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 31st St & 7th Ave S
**Instagram:** @brainthegenius

### Bio
He built one of the rare Black-owned tattoo studios in St. Petersburg, then
kept going.

Chris Roberts — Brain The Genius, formerly Brain Storm — has spent over two
decades running Urban Ink Tattoo Company, a shop he turned into one of the
city's most respected studios. But tattooing is one lane. He is a painter,
designer, and cinematographer who founded Culture Creative, an art and event
space on 34th Street South that amplifies local talent.

His 2020 SHINE Bright Spot mural at the St. Pete Youth Farm, sponsored by
NOAA Fisheries, focused on diversity and inclusion in STEM professions — a
community project in the Innovation District. For SHINE Origins 2025, he
painted "Culture" at 31st Street and 7th Avenue South.

searchBio: |
He built one of the rare Black-owned tattoo studios in St. Petersburg, then
kept going.

Chris Roberts — professionally Brain The Genius, formerly Brain Storm — is a
St. Petersburg-based multidisciplinary artist, tattoo artist, and
entrepreneur. He has spent over two decades building Urban Ink Tattoo Company
into one of the city's most respected studios, recognized as one of the rare
Black-owned businesses in the body art community. Beyond tattooing, he is a
painter, designer, and cinematographer. He founded Culture Creative, a dynamic
art and event space on 34th Street South that amplifies local talent.

In 2020, he painted a SHINE Bright Spot mural at the St. Pete Youth Farm in
the Innovation District, a community project sponsored by NOAA Fisheries
focused on diversity and inclusion in STEM professions. For SHINE Origins
2025, he painted "Culture" at 31st Street and 7th Avenue South alongside
Karel Garcia, Amy Ilic-Volpe, Elizabeth Barenis, Jenipher Chandley, Zulu
Painter, and Rebekah Lazaridis. Instagram: @brainthegenius.

muralDescription: |
A bold mural on a cinder block wall featuring the word "Culture" in ornate
gold script, framed by oversized gold bamboo hoop earrings and topped with a
gold crown. Lush monstera leaves fill the background in deep greens against
black. The left side reads "Bamboo Earrings At Least Two Pair" in white
graffiti-style lettering — a nod to the LL Cool J lyric. Signed "BRAIN
@brainthegenius" in the lower right.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A bold mural on a cinder block wall featuring the word "Culture" in ornate

graffiti-style lettering — a nod to the LL Cool J lyric. Signed "BRAIN

## Cecilia Lueza (2025)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 251 10th St N
**Building:** Haddy
**Instagram:** @cecilialuezaap

### Bio
She painted an entire intersection. Fifty volunteers helped. Then a storm
washed it away, so they painted it again.

Cecilia Lueza was born in 1971 in Posadas, in Argentina's subtropical
northeast — a landscape of dense flora and fauna that still runs through
everything she makes. She earned her MA in Visual Arts from the National
University of La Plata in Buenos Aires, held her first solo exhibition there
in 1998, and moved to the United States shortly after. She settled in
Pinellas County in 2007.

Her work spans murals, monumental sculptures, and mixed-media installations,
all built on bold geometric patterns, clean lines, and jewel-tone color.
Birds, women, and children recur. She was originally a monochromatic painter
in Argentina — Florida's light changed that.

The public art resume is staggering: nearly 60 installations since 2013.
Starbucks, the North Carolina Museum of Art, the Atlanta BeltLine, the James
Museum, a kinetic angel sculpture in Chattanooga acquired for permanent public
display. Her work sits in the Bass Museum of Art, the Museum of Contemporary
Art Miami, and the Florida Department of State collection.

She is a four-time SHINE artist — "Common Ground" in 2016 (the intersection),
"Encounter" and "The Blue Hour" in 2018, "The Sky Is the Limit" in 2019, and
"Radiance" at the Haddy Factory for SHINE 2025 Origins.

searchBio: |
She painted an entire intersection. Fifty volunteers helped. Then a storm
washed it away, so they painted it again.

Cecilia Lueza was born in 1971 in Posadas, Misiones Province, Argentina —
subtropical northeast, dense flora and fauna, a landscape that still runs
through everything she makes. She earned her MA in Visual Arts (Painting) from
the National University of La Plata in Buenos Aires (1990-1994), held her
first solo exhibition in Buenos Aires in 1998, and moved to the United States
in 1999 after meeting Miami-based artists. She lived in Miami before settling
in Pinellas County, Florida in 2007. She cites Georgia O'Keeffe and Francis
Bacon as influences.

Her work spans murals, monumental sculptures, and mixed-media installations —
bold geometric patterns, clean lines, jewel-tone color gradations. Birds,
women, and children recur. She was originally a monochromatic painter in
Argentina; Florida's light and vibrancy changed that. Nature is her primary
subject. She calls her art an attempt to embody "universal meaning such as
innocence, beauty, freedom."

The public art resume is staggering: nearly 60 installations since 2013, over
20 years as a working public artist. Major murals include "I Am My Own Muse"
at the Marriott City Center in Durham NC (2019), "Exuberance" at HCC Dale
Mabry Campus in Tampa (2020), "Summertime" in Clearwater (2021, selected from
190+ nationwide applicants), "Chromascape" in Jersey City (2021), "The Legend
of Ondine" in Lakeland (2022), "The Starbucks Siren" at 3939 4th St N in St.
Petersburg (2023), "Life Force" at Arya Residences in St. Petersburg (2024),
and "Morning Ritual" at Joffrey's Coffee HQ in Tampa (2026).

Public sculptures include "Winds of Change" — a kinetic stainless steel angel
acquired by the Chattanooga Public Art Commission for permanent display —
"Luminarium" at the Arts and Innovation Center in Rockville MD (interactive
wall sculptures with fiber optics and LEDs activated by sound), "Time Flies
Sundial" at River Terrace Education Campus in Washington DC, multiple school
commissions for the DC Department of General Services, "Delta" and "Nova" in
Jacksonville, "Dual Nature" in Reno, "Journey of Hope" in Fort Myers, "Nexus"
in Tampa, "Caleidoscopio" in Toledo, "Sea Orb" in Jacksonville, and "Golden
Hour" at the Orange County Administration Center in Orlando (2026).

Clients and commissions include Starbucks, the North Carolina Museum of Art
(mural for a Frida Kahlo/Diego Rivera exhibition), the Atlanta BeltLine, the
City of Atlanta Mayor's Office of Cultural Affairs, the Chattanooga Public Art
Commission, Hillsborough Community College, and municipalities in
Indianapolis, Clearwater, Fort Myers, and Jacksonville. Her work is in the
collections of the Bass Museum of Art Miami, the Museum of Contemporary Art
Miami, the Florida Department of State, and 13+ institutional collections.
Gallery representation includes Kavachnina Contemporary, Onessimo Fine Art
(Palm Beach Gardens), Mary Martin Art Gallery (Naples), and Naomi Silva
Gallery (Atlanta).

Awards include a Tourist Development Council Grant (Miami-Dade, 2011),
Professional Development Individual Artist Grant (Pinellas County, 2008),
Florida Artist Enhancement Grant (2008), Puffin Foundation Grant (2006),
Karrvaz Artist-in-Residency in Spain (2006), and Sculpture Key West People's
Choice Award (2006).

She is a four-time SHINE artist: "Common Ground" (2016) — a street mural at
Central Ave and 5th Street outside Florida CraftArt, painted with 50+
volunteers, washed away by a storm, painted again; "Encounter" (2018) at the
ArtsXchange in the Warehouse Arts District; "The Blue Hour" (2018) at ONE St.
Petersburg; "The Sky Is the Limit" (2019) — a 164 x 11 foot ground mural at
North Shore Park pier; and "Radiance" (2025) at the Haddy Factory for SHINE
Origins. Instagram: @cecilialuezaap. Website: lueza.com.

muralDescription: |
A large-scale mural on the side of a commercial building depicting a
vividly colored hummingbird in flight, wings spread wide, rendered in
iridescent greens, blues, and oranges. The bird hovers against a dynamic
background of overlapping translucent circles in gradients of blue, teal,
green, and yellow — Lueza's signature geometric style. Signed "LUEZA" in
the upper right. The building number 255 is visible at the left edge.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A large-scale mural on the side of a commercial building depicting a
vividly colored hummingbird in flight, wings spread wide, rendered in
iridescent greens, blues, and oranges. The bird hovers against a dynamic
background of overlapping translucent circles in gradients of blue, teal,
green, and yellow — Lueza's signature geometric style. Signed "LUEZA" in
the upper right. The building number 255 is visible at the left edge.

## Derek Donnelly (2025)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 1847 1st Ave N
**Building:** instagram: saintpaintarts
**Instagram:** @saintpaintarts

### Bio
He painted a T-Rex in a Hawaiian shirt going fishing — for AARP. That tells
you most of what you need to know about Derek Donnelly.

A Pinellas County native, Donnelly grew up in Pinellas Park, graduated from
the Center for the Arts at Gibbs High School in 2001, and studied graphic
design and digital illustration at IADT Tampa. He went full-time on murals in
2010. A year later he opened Saint Paint Arts gallery on the 600 block of
Central Avenue — not as a vanity project, but as a launchpad. Several artists
got their first real exposure there before he closed it in 2014 to focus on
walls.

His style sits at the intersection of illustration and realism — playful
without being childish, bold without being loud. His son Syre shows up in
murals across the city. So does Florida wildlife. He doesn't separate the
personal from the public work. In 2012, when mentor Bill "Woo" Correira died,
Donnelly organized the Woomorial — a 40-artist tribute mural on the 600 block
that became a landmark. That instinct to build things for other people keeps
showing up. He founded COVE gallery in Pinellas Park, helped establish the
Pinellas Arts Village district, and started Public Art Project Inc., a
nonprofit that pushes public art into places it wouldn't otherwise reach and
raised pandemic relief funds for local artists.

His client list runs from Fender Guitars to the Tampa Bay Rays to Sirata Beach
Resort — the first major St. Pete Beach resort to commission a local artist.
Multiple Best of the Bay wins. Inaugural Artmaker 2026 at Ruth Eckerd Hall.
For SHINE 2025, he wasn't just painting — he ran operations and served as
artist liaison for the entire festival while producing two murals of his own.

searchBio: |
He painted a T-Rex in a Hawaiian shirt going fishing — for AARP. That tells
you most of what you need to know about Derek Donnelly.

A Pinellas County native who grew up in Pinellas Park, Donnelly graduated from
the Pinellas County Center for the Arts at Gibbs High School in 2001 and
studied graphic design and digital illustration at IADT Tampa. He has 20-plus
years of painting experience and went full-time on murals in 2010. Within a
year he opened Saint Paint Arts gallery on the 600 block of Central Avenue in
downtown St. Petersburg — not as a vanity project, but as a launchpad for
other artists. Several got their first real exposure there before he closed it
in 2014 to focus entirely on walls.

In the early 2010s, Donnelly and collaborator Sebastian Coolidge co-founded
the downtown St. Pete mural movement when the area was, by his own account,
"drab and virtually art-free." Together they painted "A Moment to Reflect" on
the Florida Craftsmen building — at the time the largest mural in St.
Petersburg, four stories tall, completed in ten days. It depicts a businessman
removing his shoes to embrace creativity, with a green tie nodding to sponsor
Regions Bank.

In November 2012, mentor Bill "Woo" Correira died from complications of brain
surgery. Correira was known for his fish paintings and his fierce support of
the 600 block arts community. Donnelly organized the Woomorial, a tribute
mural on the side of the Saint Paint gallery where 40-plus artists
contributed — Donnelly painted Woo's portrait while others added marine life.
It became a 600 block landmark.

His style blends illustration and realism — bold character work, signature
gradients, hand-drawn detail, playful without being childish. His son Syre
appears in murals across the city, including the Outshine project at Planet
Retro Records on Dr. MLK Jr. Street N, where he collaborated with Hinson the
Healer, R2 Romero, and Artist Slim. Florida wildlife is a constant. He has
painted portraits of Prince, Kurt Cobain, and Tupac Shakur. His canvases
featuring Syre were exhibited in the "Son-Shine" show at the Wayward Goose.

Donnelly's client list includes AARP, Hilton Hotels, the Tampa Bay Rays, MLB,
Fender Guitars, St. Petersburg College, Sirata Beach Resort (two murals as
part of a $15 million renovation — the first major St. Pete Beach resort to
commission a local artist), Rococo Steak (a ceiling mural), Station House, and
Smith & Associates Real Estate. His work spans Clearwater Beach, Gulfport,
Treasure Island, Madeira Beach, Largo, Dunedin, Tampa, Seminole, North Ft.
Myers, and Anna Maria Island.

That instinct to build things for other people keeps showing up. He founded
COVE gallery (Creative Opportunities & Visual Entertainment) in late 2016 in
the Pinellas Arts Village — a city-funded project using rehabbed shipping
containers at 5705 Park Blvd in Pinellas Park. He helped establish the
Pinellas Arts Village district from the beginning and hosts ArtWalk events
there every fourth Saturday. He started Public Art Project Inc., a nonprofit
that creates large-scale beautification projects, educates the public on the
social and economic impact of the arts, and raised pandemic relief funds for
local artists. He is a member of the Bloom Collective and a participant in the
Carmada art car movement alongside Sebastian Coolidge, James Oleson, Daniel
Barojas, and Zulu Painter.

He helped originate the SHINE Mural Festival and in 2018 curated five "Open
Call" walls for the festival. Multiple Best of the Bay wins from Creative
Loafing Tampa Bay. Selected as the inaugural Artmaker 2026 at Ruth Eckerd
Hall's Marcia P. Hoffman School of the Arts — a year-long exhibition in the
hall's galleries with a featured artwork on the annual holiday ornament. He
appeared on The Catalyst Sessions (May 2020) and RHStv's "Jabari's Beautiful
People: Featuring St. Pete Murals."

For SHINE 2025, executive director Helen French brought him on as Operations
Lead and artist liaison — the person with "the pulse" of the city. It was his
first time as a featured SHINE artist after a decade of painting city walls.
He produced two murals: a vintage postcard piece reading "Greetings from
Hollander Hotel" at 421 4th Ave N, and a panoramic ocean scene with his
signature pelican-riding boy at 1847 1st Ave N.

The dinosaur in the Hawaiian shirt fishing off the waterfront? That's his AARP
"Age Friendly St. Pete" series on the east wall of the Morean Arts Center —
the older generation, he says, enjoying life to the fullest.

muralDescription: |
A wide-format mural wrapping around two walls of a single-story building.
The central scene shows Donnelly's signature cartoon boy — in a red cap and
sunglasses — riding a pelican in flight over ocean waves. A dragonfly hovers
on the right side, and seagulls fill the sky. The left wall transitions into
an underwater scene in deep blue-green tones, with a diver amid coral and
sea life. Signed "Donnelly" in the upper right. The palette shifts from
cool oceanic blues on the left to bright sky blue and green on the right.

muralInspiration: |
Donnelly stated: 'My murals are a celebration of all things local, with my
son and our city's bird.'

### Mural Description
A wide-format mural wrapping around two walls of a single-story building.
The central scene shows Donnelly's signature cartoon boy — in a red cap and
sunglasses — riding a pelican in flight over ocean waves. A dragonfly hovers
on the right side, and seagulls fill the sky. The left wall transitions into
an underwater scene in deep blue-green tones, with a diver amid coral and
sea life. Signed "Donnelly" in the upper right. The palette shifts from
cool oceanic blues on the left to bright sky blue and green on the right.

muralInspiration: |
Donnelly stated: 'My murals are a celebration of all things local, with my
son and our city's bird.'

Donnelly's client list includes AARP, Hilton Hotels, the Tampa Bay Rays, MLB,
Fender Guitars, St. Petersburg College, Sirata Beach Resort (two murals as
part of a $15 million renovation — the first major St. Pete Beach resort to
commission a local artist), Rococo Steak (a ceiling mural), Station House, and
Smith & Associates Real Estate. His work spans Clearwater Beach, Gulfport,
Treasure Island, Madeira Beach, Largo, Dunedin, Tampa, Seminole, North Ft.
Myers, and Anna Maria Island.

That instinct to build things for other people keeps showing up. He founded
COVE gallery (Creative Opportunities & Visual Entertainment) in late 2016 in
the Pinellas Arts Village — a city-funded project using rehabbed shipping
containers at 5705 Park Blvd in Pinellas Park. He helped establish the
Pinellas Arts Village district from the beginning and hosts ArtWalk events
there every fourth Saturday. He started Public Art Project Inc., a nonprofit
that creates large-scale beautification projects, educates the public on the
social and economic impact of the arts, and raised pandemic relief funds for
local artists. He is a member of the Bloom Collective and a participant in the
Carmada art car movement alongside Sebastian Coolidge, James Oleson, Daniel
Barojas, and Zulu Painter.

He helped originate the SHINE Mural Festival and in 2018 curated five "Open
Call" walls for the festival. Multiple Best of the Bay wins from Creative
Loafing Tampa Bay. Selected as the inaugural Artmaker 2026 at Ruth Eckerd
Hall's Marcia P. Hoffman School of the Arts — a year-long exhibition in the
hall's galleries with a featured artwork on the annual holiday ornament. He
appeared on The Catalyst Sessions (May 2020) and RHStv's "Jabari's Beautiful
People: Featuring St. Pete Murals."

For SHINE 2025, executive director Helen French brought him on as Operations
Lead and artist liaison — the person with "the pulse" of the city. It was his
first time as a featured SHINE artist after a decade of painting city walls.
He produced two murals: a vintage postcard piece reading "Greetings from
Hollander Hotel" at 421 4th Ave N, and a panoramic ocean scene with his
signature pelican-riding boy at 1847 1st Ave N.

The dinosaur in the Hawaiian shirt fishing off the waterfront? That's his AARP
"Age Friendly St. Pete" series on the east wall of the Morean Arts Center —
the older generation, he says, enjoying life to the fullest.

muralDescription: |
A vintage-style postcard mural reading "Greetings from Hollander Hotel,
St. Pete, FL." A cartoon boy in a red cap rides a pelican wearing sunglasses
over turquoise waters, with dolphins leaping below, seagulls overhead, and a
palm-tree island with the Sunshine Skyway Bridge in the distance. Signed
"Donnelly @Saint Paint Arts" in the lower right. The retro color palette of
teals, creams, and warm tans gives it a classic Florida tourism feel.

muralInspiration: |
Donnelly stated: 'My murals are a celebration of all things local, with my
son and our city's bird.'

### Mural Description
A vintage-style postcard mural reading "Greetings from Hollander Hotel,
St. Pete, FL." A cartoon boy in a red cap rides a pelican wearing sunglasses
over turquoise waters, with dolphins leaping below, seagulls overhead, and a
palm-tree island with the Sunshine Skyway Bridge in the distance. Signed

son and our city's bird.'

## Dreamweaver (2025)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Tampa, FL
**Address:** 696 1st Ave N
**Building:** Vernis Bowling
**Instagram:** @dream.weavin

### Bio
She painted her first mural at seven, on her aunt's backyard shed in Waco,
Texas. She has not stopped.

Taylor Smith — Dreamweaver — grew up between Portland, Oregon and just south
of Atlanta, born in Dallas. She studied communications and fine art at Georgia
College and State University, with a minor in graphic design, graduating in
2017. Before that, she took private painting lessons as a kid and apprenticed
at a tattoo shop at 16 — too young to actually tattoo under Georgia law, but
old enough to learn.

She spent time doing live painting at music festivals — Electric Daisy
Carnival in Vegas, Okeechobee, Electric Forest — before relocating to Florida
around 2019. She works almost entirely in spray paint, blending expressive
abstraction with realism. Nature runs through everything: wildlife, water,
birds, flora. She is left-handed.

She has painted an estimated 120 to 150 murals, 15 to 20 in St. Pete alone.
Other projects include the Clearwater Municipal Parking Garage and a sea
turtle mural at Coachman Park for Art Oasis 2024, and "The Garden Within" in
downtown Kissimmee for Osceola Arts. She is a resident artist at AER Tampa
and a member of Few and Far, an international women's graffiti crew.

For SHINE she painted in 2022 at 1510 1st Ave N and returned for SHINE
Origins 2025 at the Vernis Bowling building on the 600 block — where the
SHINE movement started in 2012.

searchBio: |
She painted her first mural at seven, on her aunt Carolyn's backyard shed in
Waco, Texas. She has not stopped.

Taylor Smith — professionally Dreamweaver — was born in Dallas, Texas, grew
up between Portland, Oregon and just south of Atlanta, Georgia. She earned a
BA in Communications and Fine Art with a minor in Graphic Design from Georgia
College and State University in 2017. She took private painting lessons
throughout elementary and middle school and did a tattoo apprenticeship at
16 — eight months, but Georgia age restrictions meant she couldn't actually
tattoo.

After college she started live painting at concerts and festivals in Atlanta
(2017), worked in restaurant service, graphic design, and creative event
production, then relocated to Florida around 2018-2019. She went full-time as
an artist around 2020. She works primarily in spray paint and aerosol,
blending expressive abstraction with realism. Nature is the constant —
wildlife, water, birds, flora and fauna. She also paints on canvas, airbrush,
skateboard decks, surfboards, shoes, and backpacks. She is left-handed.

She has painted an estimated 120 to 150 murals, with 15 to 20 in St. Pete.
Festival credits include SHINE 2022 (mural at 1510 1st Ave N, Yacht Power
Products building), SHINE Origins 2025 (Vernis Bowling building at 696 1st
Ave N), Art Oasis Mural Festival 2024 (Clearwater Municipal Parking Garage
and a marine-themed sea turtle mural at Coachman Park Dockmaster's Office),
Wynwood Mural Festival, Paint Memphis 2019, and Art Basel. She has done live
painting at Electric Daisy Carnival (Las Vegas), Okeechobee Music Festival,
and Electric Forest Festival.

Other projects include "The Garden Within" at 219-231 Broadway in downtown
Kissimmee for Osceola Arts' ARTisNOW Public Murals Project (2025). She was
Artist in Residence at Parc at Wesley Chapel, Tampa (2023) and is a resident
artist at AER Tampa. She is a contributor to Few and Far, an international
women's graffiti crew, and collaborates with fellow artist "Slim Gee" under
KindMinds Art (@kindmindsart). She teaches spray painting and mural techniques
to youth communities and is listed on Creative Pinellas as "an avid supporter
of improving our communities through public art and collaboration."

Profiled in Voyage MIA Magazine (January 2020) and The Artisan Magazine.
Contact: Dreamweavin.murals@gmail.com. Website: weavindreams.com.
Instagram: @dream.weavin.

muralDescription: |
A multi-story mural wrapping around a lavender building, featuring a white
egret with delicate plumage nestled among enormous, lush flower petals in
deep pinks, corals, oranges, and purples. The bird's long neck rises
elegantly through the blooms on the left wall, while the right wall
continues with swirling petals and soft round forms in warm sunset tones.
The scale is dramatic — the egret spans several stories — and the
soft-focus, almost photorealistic rendering gives the whole piece a
dreamlike, ethereal quality.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A multi-story mural wrapping around a lavender building, featuring a white

deep pinks, corals, oranges, and purples. The bird's long neck rises
elegantly through the blooms on the left wall, while the right wall
continues with swirling petals and soft round forms in warm sunset tones.
The scale is dramatic — the egret spans several stories — and the
soft-focus, almost photorealistic rendering gives the whole piece a
dreamlike, ethereal quality.

## Elizabeth Barenis (2025)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Pinellas County, FL
**Address:** 31st St & 7th Ave S
**Instagram:** @elizabethbarenis

### Bio
In 2018, she was one of ten painters invited to the Mark Rothko Art Center in
Latvia. She grew up in the Mississippi Delta, where her parents owned a music
store.

Elizabeth Barenis studied piano with her mother, took private art lessons
throughout childhood, attended the Mississippi School for Math and Science,
and graduated from Sarah Lawrence College in 2004. A summer at the Chautauqua
Institute for Art that same year sealed it — she committed to painting
full-time.

She calls her approach "Abstract Precisionism" — generalized shapes rendered
with clean, precise lines that recall Cubism. Palm fronds, tropical flora,
seascapes, forests, and foggy scenes dominate. She works from her own
photography, particularly nighttime reference shots where she reconstructs
pattern and form. She also has a background in medical illustration, which
shows in the detail.

She moved to St. Petersburg in 2015 for family reasons and stayed for the
arts community. Since then: a 2017 Creative Pinellas Emerging Artist Grant, a
2023 Professional Artist Grant, solo shows at the Morean Arts Center
("Tropical Splendor") and Art Center Sarasota ("Palmistry"), and a mural on
the Bayfront Hospital ambulance bay — a diptych painting photographed and
enlarged for the louvered exterior wall. Her first public mural was "Super
Palm" in Dunedin in 2022, 300 square feet on a Parks and Recreation building.

For SHINE Origins 2025, she painted alongside Karel Garcia, Amy Ilic-Volpe,
Brain The Genius, and others at 31st Street and 7th Avenue South.

searchBio: |
In 2018, she was one of ten painters from seven countries invited to the
International Painting Symposium at the Mark Rothko Art Center in Daugavpils,
Latvia, marking Rothko's 115th anniversary. She grew up in the Mississippi
Delta, where her parents owned a music store.

Elizabeth Barenis was born and raised in Greenville, Mississippi. She studied
piano with her mother and later learned guitar. She enrolled in private art
lessons throughout childhood. She attended the Mississippi School for Math and
Science in Columbus, Mississippi, then graduated from Sarah Lawrence College
in Bronxville, New York in 2004. A summer at the Chautauqua Institute for Art
that same year sealed it — she committed to painting full-time.

She calls her approach "Abstract Precisionism" — generalized shapes rendered
with clean, precise lines reminiscent of Cubism. Her primary subjects are palm
fronds, tropical flora, seascapes, forests, rainbows, and foggy scenes. She
works from her own photography, particularly nighttime reference photos where
she reconstructs pattern and form. She also has a background in medical
illustration, which informs her sensitivity to detail. She works in acrylic
and oil. Her philosophy: beauty is "an indication of a greater force at work
in our world, one of harmony and well-being."

She relocated to St. Petersburg, Florida in 2015 for family reasons and
stayed for the arts community. Awards include a 2017 Creative Pinellas
Emerging Artist Grant, a 2023 Creative Pinellas Professional Artist Grant, a
2025 Award of Excellence at Art Festival Beth-El, and the Margaret Murphy
Steward Best of Show at a Morean Arts Center members exhibition (which led to
her solo show there). Solo exhibitions include "Palmistry" at Art Center
Sarasota (October-November 2022) and "Tropical Splendor" at the Morean Arts
Center (2024, with gallery talk August 8). Group exhibitions include
"Perspectives" at Studio@620 (with Vincent Mancuso), ARTicles Art Gallery in
the Uptown Arts District, SixStar Art Studios, and Art Festival Beth-El
(2025, 2026).

Public art includes "Super Palm" — a 300 sq ft mural on the Parks and
Recreation building at 1700 Solon Ave in Dunedin, Florida (November 2022, her
first public mural), signal box designs in Clearwater, and an ambulance bay
mural at Bayfront Hospital (now Orlando Health Bayfront) in St. Petersburg —
based on a 48 by 144 inch diptych painting, photographed and enlarged for the
louvered exterior wall. Healthcare clients include Moffitt Cancer Center
(artwork acquisition), Bayfront Health St. Petersburg (mural and painting
commissions), and Premier Community Healthcare in New Port Richey (extensive
series of prints).

For SHINE Origins 2025, she was one of approximately 17 local artists
selected. She painted at 31st Street and 7th Avenue South alongside Karel
Garcia, Amy Ilic-Volpe, Brain The Genius, Jenipher Chandley, Zulu Painter,
and Rebekah Lazaridis. Website: elizabethbarenis.com.
Instagram: @elizabethbarenis.

muralDescription: |
A wide mural on a cinder block wall depicting oversized tropical fan palm
fronds in layered shades of green, teal, and soft yellow. The leaves
radiate outward in clean, geometric planes — Barenis's "Abstract
Precisionism" style — creating depth through overlapping translucent shapes.
Signed "Elizabeth Barenis 2025" in the lower left corner.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A wide mural on a cinder block wall depicting oversized tropical fan palm
fronds in layered shades of green, teal, and soft yellow. The leaves
radiate outward in clean, geometric planes — Barenis's "Abstract
Precisionism" style — creating depth through overlapping translucent shapes.
Signed "Elizabeth Barenis 2025" in the lower left corner.

## Isac Gres (2025)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Cuba / St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 699 Central Ave
**Building:** Melting Pot
**Instagram:** @isacgres.art

### Bio
He left Cuba in 2019 and walked through twelve countries — including the
Darien Gap — before reaching the United States. He brought his brushes.

Isac Gres was born in 1993 in Bayamo, Cuba. He studied cubism, hyperrealism,
surrealism, and conceptual thinking at the E.I.A Jose Marti Perez art
academy, then spent three years studying psychology at the Celia Sanchez
Manduley institute, followed by a year of mandatory military service. He left
Cuba on November 12, 2019, established a tattoo studio called Isac Ink in
Uruguay, then relocated to Miami in 2022 to reunite with family before
settling in St. Petersburg.

His work is contemporary surrealism in mixed media — acrylic, oil, spray
paint. Fragmented figures, dreamlike landscapes, hybrid beings. Pathways and
doorways recur. So do jellyfish, which he reads as resilience and
immortality, and apples, which stand for knowledge and identity. Light drives
everything. "Color for me means light," he says, "and also good energy."

For SHINE Origins 2025 he painted the tallest wall of his career — a
surrealist design with melting clocks (a nod to Dali) on the Melting Pot
restaurant.

searchBio: |
He left Cuba in 2019 and walked through twelve countries — including the
Darien Gap — before reaching the United States. He brought his brushes.

Isac Gres was born November 29, 1993 in Bayamo, Cuba. He studied cubism,
hyperrealism, surrealism, and conceptual thinking at the E.I.A Jose Marti
Perez art academy, then spent three years studying psychology at the Celia
Sanchez Manduley institute, followed by a year of mandatory military service
in Cuba's army. He left Cuba on November 12, 2019 and traveled through 12
countries including the Darien Gap. He established "Isac Ink" tattoo studio
in Uruguay (approximately 2019-2022), then relocated to Miami in 2022 to
reunite with family. He worked at a tattoo studio while painting before
settling in St. Petersburg, Florida.

His work is contemporary surrealism in mixed media — acrylic, oil, spray
paint. Bold abstractions balancing vibrant color, texture, and form.
Fragmented figures, dreamlike landscapes, hybrid beings. Recurring symbols
include pathways and doorways, jellyfish (resilience and immortality), and
apples (knowledge and identity). Light drives everything: "Color for me means
light...and also good energy." He is represented by Patti Suzette Art and has
exhibited in South Carolina and multiple galleries nationwide. He has an NFT
collection on OpenSea.

For SHINE Origins 2025 he painted the tallest wall of his career — a
surrealist design with melting clocks (a nod to Dali) on the Melting Pot
restaurant. Instagram: @isacgres.art.

muralDescription: |
A massive two-story mural wrapping around the corner of a building in
swirling cosmic surrealism. The composition features molten planetary
spheres, a city skyline encased in a dripping glass orb, melting candles
nodding to Dalí, and a sculptural elephant form emerging from fiery nebula
clouds. The palette is dominated by deep space blues and purples contrasted
with blazing oranges, reds, and golds. Signed "Isac Gres" in the lower
corners of both walls.

muralInspiration: |
The mural is on a restaurant called the Melting Pot, and Gres appreciated
how the work represents a melting pot of cultures. The melting clocks are
a nod to Dalí.

muralAwards: ""

### Mural Description
A massive two-story mural wrapping around the corner of a building in
swirling cosmic surrealism. The composition features molten planetary
spheres, a city skyline encased in a dripping glass orb, melting candles
nodding to Dalí, and a sculptural elephant form emerging from fiery nebula

## Jenipher Chandley — Stronger Together (2025)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 31st St & 7th Ave S
**Instagram:** @jenipherchandley

### Bio
She started painting outside Sake Bomb on Central Avenue around 2011. She is
a survivor — of economic hardship, of domestic abuse — and she paints that
survival into everything.

Jenipher Chandley was born and raised in St. Petersburg. She earned degrees
in interior design and graphic design from IADT Tampa, and is a mother of
four who recently became a grandmother. She works predominantly in oils —
representational figurative artwork infused with surrealism and symbolism.
The female form recurs, embodying fragility and strength. Her central theme
is "the story of love" — seeking, experiencing, losing, rediscovering.

For over twelve years she has worked with nonprofits and community
initiatives. She is lead curator at Bar@548 in downtown St. Pete, hosts free
weekly Wednesday night art meetups (no commission taken), and curates monthly
emerging artist shows. She has co-curated and exhibited in hundreds of shows
throughout Tampa Bay. Her studio is at the ArtsXchange, Studio Gallery 104.

She received a 2023 Emerging Artist Grant from Creative Pinellas and has been
a Local Artist Spotlight at the Gasparilla Festival of the Arts and American
Stage Theater. For SHINE 2021 she painted her first festival mural, and for
SHINE Origins 2025 she returned with "Stronger Together" at 31st Street and
7th Avenue South — assisted by her daughter Becka.

muralDescription: |
A wide mural on a cinder block wall with the words "Stronger Together" in
large flowing white script across a deep teal background. The text is
framed by a lush border of curling green vines and tropical flame-colored
flowers — gloriosa lilies in reds, oranges, and yellows. Signed "Jenipher
Chandley" in the upper right corner.

## John Vitale — Power to the Patients (2025)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 790 Central Ave
**Instagram:** @vitalebros

### Bio
Their first mural came from a sketch on a paper napkin, handed to a
restaurant owner. That was 1992.

Johnny Vitale and his brother Paul started Vitale Bros in Tampa Bay
hand-lettering signs and painting storefront windows. Their parents are
artists. So were their grandparents. No formal training — just family
tradition and thirty-plus years of practice. Their late aunt Nancy, who had
Down syndrome and attended Parc Center for Disabilities, turned out to be an
artist too, discovered in her final decade.

In the late 1990s they opened a warehouse studio that hosted group art,
music, and fashion shows that helped birth St. Pete's emerging art scene.
They collaborated on set design for Harmony Korine's Spring Breakers. They
have painted for the Tampa Bay Rays, the Buccaneers, L'Oreal, and dozens of
local businesses. They call themselves the "mural police" — restoring damaged
walls around the city for free.

In 2023, Johnny launched Overspray, a street art shop and graffiti school now
based in Gulfport, teaching tagging, block letters, Wildstyle, and advanced
mural technique to classes of up to twenty students. After Hurricane Milton,
they painted free signs for businesses that lost theirs.

They have painted SHINE every year since its inception — "The Cool Kids" in
2015, "Bait and Switch" in 2019, and multiple walls since. For SHINE Origins
2025, Johnny painted two solo murals: one on the 600 block where it all
began, and another at The Factory on Fairfield Ave South with his daughter
Sionna.

searchBio: |
Their first mural came from a sketch on a paper napkin, handed to a
restaurant owner. That was 1992.

Johnny Vitale and his brother Paul founded Vitale Bros in 1992 in Tampa Bay,
starting with hand-lettered signs and painted storefront windows. Youngest
brother Joey later joined. Their parents are artists — the family artistic
tradition goes back to their grandparents. No formal art education has been
documented; the craft appears entirely self-taught and family-transmitted.
Their late aunt Nancy had Down syndrome and attended Parc Center for
Disabilities, where staff discovered her artistic talent in her final decade
of life.

In the late 1990s and early 2000s, they opened a warehouse studio that hosted
legendary group art, music and fashion shows credited with helping birth St.
Pete's emerging art scene. They collaborated in set design for Harmony
Korine's Spring Breakers (filmed in the St. Pete area). Their style blends
pop art, graffiti, and traditional mural techniques — fine art meets pop art
sensibility, incorporating local culture, historical elements, and visual
stories reflecting community identity.

Clients include the Tampa Bay Rays, Tampa Bay Buccaneers, L'Oreal /
SalonCentric (national locations), Anchor Skate Supply, and Great Bay
Distributors, plus numerous local businesses and private residences. Notable
murals include the Marilyn Monroe on the side of the historic Playhouse
Theater on Central Ave, a Virgin Mary at St. Pete Catholic High School,
Power to the Patients giant fist at the Morean Arts Center, and the Nueva
Cantina restaurant mural in Tampa. They describe themselves as the mural
police — restoring damaged murals around the city for free.

The current Vitale Bros team includes Johnny, Paul, Nick and Jason Kekllas,
Bruce Statton, and Jake Gerow. They have also collaborated under the name
Up and Over with Tes One, and painted behind the State Theatre with Tes One
and Palehorse.

In July 2023, Johnny launched Overspray — a retail street art shop and
graffiti school, initially on the Pinellas Trail, relocated to 5013 20th Ave
S. in Gulfport. Overspray teaches graffiti, tagging, block and bubble
letters, Wildstyle, and advanced mural classes to groups of up to 20
students. The five-artist teaching team includes John Vitale, Nicole Salgar,
Hinson Thehealer, Digital (LA), and Jujmo. After Hurricane Milton, they
painted free signs for businesses that lost theirs. They host events like
Hot Walls during SHINE.

SHINE participation: The Cool Kids (2015, 2063 Central Ave — faces of the
artists' children), Bait and Switch (2019 — red snapper and sea turtle),
SHINE 2022, SHINE 2024 (mural at Anchor Skate Supply with pro skateboarder
Kris Markovich, inspired by Rays City Connect uniforms; limited-edition
skateboard L'Orange). For SHINE Origins 2025, Johnny painted two solo
walls: 600 1st Ave N (the 600 block where SHINE began) and The Factory at
2606 Fairfield Ave S (collaboration with Sionna Sio Macleish, celebrating
St. Pete history and landmarks).

Studio: 5013 20th Ave S., Gulfport, FL. Website: vitalebrothers.com.
Instagram: @johnvitale (solo), @vitalebros (collective).

muralDescription: |
A propaganda-style mural in the tradition of Shepard Fairey's OBEY
aesthetic. A raised fist gripping a caduceus medical symbol is centered
in a bold red circle, with "Power to the Patients" in block letters below.
The top reads "Prices Are Now a Patient's Right" and the bottom banner
declares "Demand Them!!!" in large cream type on red. The palette is
limited to navy blue, red, cream, and gray — evoking classic activist
poster art. "PowerToThePatients.org" runs along the bottom, with Vitale
Bros logos flanking it.

muralInspiration: |
Part of Shepard Fairey's national Power to the Patients campaign to combat
hidden healthcare pricing and raise awareness that prices are a patient's
right prior to receiving care.

muralAwards: ""

### Mural Description
A propaganda-style mural in the tradition of Shepard Fairey's OBEY
aesthetic. A raised fist gripping a caduceus medical symbol is centered
in a bold red circle, with "Power to the Patients" in block letters below.
The top reads "Prices Are Now a Patient's Right" and the bottom banner

limited to navy blue, red, cream, and gray — evoking classic activist

muralInspiration: |

In the late 1990s and early 2000s, they opened a warehouse studio that hosted
legendary group art, music and fashion shows credited with helping birth St.
Pete's emerging art scene. They collaborated in set design for Harmony
Korine's Spring Breakers (filmed in the St. Pete area). Their style blends
pop art, graffiti, and traditional mural techniques — fine art meets pop art
sensibility, incorporating local culture, historical elements, and visual
stories reflecting community identity.

Clients include the Tampa Bay Rays, Tampa Bay Buccaneers, L'Oreal /
SalonCentric (national locations), Anchor Skate Supply, and Great Bay
Distributors, plus numerous local businesses and private residences. Notable
murals include the Marilyn Monroe on the side of the historic Playhouse
Theater on Central Ave, a Virgin Mary at St. Pete Catholic High School,
Power to the Patients giant fist at the Morean Arts Center, and the Nueva
Cantina restaurant mural in Tampa. They describe themselves as the mural
police — restoring damaged murals around the city for free.

The current Vitale Bros team includes Johnny, Paul, Nick and Jason Kekllas,
Bruce Statton, and Jake Gerow. They have also collaborated under the name
Up and Over with Tes One, and painted behind the State Theatre with Tes One
and Palehorse.

In July 2023, Johnny launched Overspray — a retail street art shop and
graffiti school, initially on the Pinellas Trail, relocated to 5013 20th Ave
S. in Gulfport. Overspray teaches graffiti, tagging, block and bubble
letters, Wildstyle, and advanced mural classes to groups of up to 20
students. The five-artist teaching team includes John Vitale, Nicole Salgar,
Hinson Thehealer, Digital (LA), and Jujmo. After Hurricane Milton, they
painted free signs for businesses that lost theirs. They host events like
Hot Walls during SHINE.

SHINE participation: The Cool Kids (2015, 2063 Central Ave — faces of the
artists' children), Bait and Switch (2019 — red snapper and sea turtle),
SHINE 2022, SHINE 2024 (mural at Anchor Skate Supply with pro skateboarder
Kris Markovich, inspired by Rays City Connect uniforms; limited-edition
skateboard L'Orange). For SHINE Origins 2025, Johnny painted two solo
walls: 600 1st Ave N (the 600 block where SHINE began) and The Factory at
2606 Fairfield Ave S (collaboration with Sionna Sio Macleish, celebrating
St. Pete history and landmarks).

Studio: 5013 20th Ave S., Gulfport, FL. Website: vitalebrothers.com.
Instagram: @johnvitale (solo), @vitalebros (collective).

muralDescription: |
A multi-panel vintage Florida postcard mural inside The Factory. The left
panel features a glamorous 1950s-style pin-up woman with black hair and a
pink hibiscus, turquoise jewelry, and a leopard-print top, set against the
Thunderbird motel sign framed by tropical hibiscus flowers. Below reads
"St. Petersburg, Florida" in retro blue script on golden yellow. The right
panel, painted by Sionna MacLeish, shows a roseate spoonbill in soft pinks
against a pale blue polka-dot background with a bold orange sun. The whole
composition evokes mid-century Florida tourism nostalgia.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description

pink hibiscus, turquoise jewelry, and a leopard-print top, set against the
Thunderbird motel sign framed by tropical hibiscus flowers. Below reads

against a pale blue polka-dot background with a bold orange sun. The whole
composition evokes mid-century Florida tourism nostalgia.

## Karel Garcia (2025)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 31st St & 7th Ave S
**Instagram:** @karelgarcia.fineartist

### Bio
His SHINE 2025 mural was his first public art installation in America. He
trained as an architect in Havana.

Karel Garcia was born December 27, 1990 in Vedado, Cuba. He earned an
architecture degree in Havana in 2017, working as an architect while
exhibiting art privately and participating in projects at the Habana Espacio
Creativo Center. His focus has always been the human figure — sexuality, the
bond between humans and nature, consciousness.

Now based in St. Petersburg, he holds a studio in the Warehouse Arts District
through the Green Book of Tampa Bay artist residency. His current series,
"Human," explores themes of human experience through detailed drawing and
sculpture. His mural work uses bold color — oranges, blues, greens — with
stylized graphic linework.

For SHINE Origins 2025, he painted at 31st Street and 7th Avenue South. The
themes were personal: migration, freedom, family, social justice — reflecting
his own journey from Cuba.

searchBio: |
His SHINE 2025 mural was his first public art installation in America. He
trained as an architect in Havana.

Karel Garcia (sometimes listed as Karel Garcia de Leon) was born December 27,
1990 in Vedado, Cuba. He earned an architecture degree in Havana, completed
in 2017, and worked as an architect while exhibiting art privately. He
participated in projects at the Habana Espacio Creativo Center in Havana.
His focus is the human figure — sexuality, the bond between humans and nature
and environment, consciousness.

Now based in St. Petersburg, Florida, he holds a studio in the Warehouse Arts
District through the Green Book of Tampa Bay artist residency. His notable
series "Human" explores themes of human experience and consciousness through
detailed drawing and sculpture. His mural work uses bold color — oranges,
blues, greens — with stylized graphic linework.

For SHINE Origins 2025, he painted at 31st Street and 7th Avenue South
alongside Amy Ilic-Volpe, Elizabeth Barenis, Zulu Painter, Brain The Genius,
and others. The themes were personal: migration, freedom, family, social
justice — reflecting his own journey from Cuba. He is listed on Creative
Pinellas. Instagram: @karelgarcia.fineartist.

muralDescription: |
A wide mural on a cinder block wall depicting bold, swirling ocean waves
in deep blues and greens against a golden-orange sky. Three carved wooden
totem-like hands rise from the waves, and small red paper boats ride the
cresting swells — symbols of migration and hope. The flowing, graphic
linework gives the waves a stylized, rhythmic energy. Signed "Karel Garcia"
in the lower right.

muralInspiration: |
Garcia's mural explores themes of migration, freedom, and social justice,
reflecting his own journey from Cuba to America. The paper boats and
reaching hands symbolize the search for freedom and family.

muralAwards: ""

### Mural Description

in deep blues and greens against a golden-orange sky. Three carved wooden
totem-like hands rise from the waves, and small red paper boats ride the
cresting swells — symbols of migration and hope. The flowing, graphic
linework gives the waves a stylized, rhythmic energy. Signed "Karel Garcia"

## Rebekah Lazaridis (2025)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Pinellas County, FL
**Address:** 31st St & 7th Ave S
**Instagram:** @rebekahlaz

### Bio
She is a third-generation St. Petersburg native. Her great-grandfather
founded Wilhelm Funeral Home in 1918.

Rebekah Lazaridis earned her BFA in Painting and Drawing from the School of
the Art Institute of Chicago in 2005. She started painting scenery at
fourteen for theater friends, and the elaborate Halloween sets she built
helped get her into art school. After Chicago she completed a one-year
apprenticeship with USA829, the scenic artists' union, in New York — working
on Broadway productions, SNL, live television, and large-scale films. Clients
from that world include Montblanc, Louis Vuitton, and Tiffany.

Back in St. Pete, her work integrates story, nostalgia, and sense of
place — medieval art, fairy tales, spirituality, and the architecture of her
hometown. Her solo show "CIRCA" at Mize Gallery featured nostalgic paintings
of local landmarks: the Emerald Cocktail Lounge, the Thunderbird Hotel,
Haslam's Books, Biff Burger. She designs sets for Jobsite Theater, American
Stage, freeFall, and the Straz Center. She is an Official Roycroft Artisan.

She also runs @homeillustrations — custom colored-pencil portraits of
historic homes, starting at $375. She co-operated Eugenia Woods, a
hand-painted bags line, with her sister. For SHINE Origins 2025, she painted
at 31st Street and 7th Avenue South.

muralDescription: |
A dramatic monochromatic mural on a black cinder block wall. A large
ornate Gothic letter "B" with intricate Celtic knotwork fills the center,
overlaid on a detailed full moon rendered in grays and whites. Bats swarm
from the left across the night sky, and a barn owl with outstretched wings
emerges from storm clouds on the right, with rain streaking diagonally
downward. Signed "ReAh @rebekahlaz" in the lower right corner. The entire
palette is black, white, and silver — a stark contrast to the colorful
murals flanking it.

muralInspiration: ""

## Reid Jenkins (2025)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 2039 1st Ave S
**Building:** Founders Pro
**Instagram:** @reidmjenkins

### Bio
He paints the people seldom seen in the 'Burg. He also tattoos them.

Reid Jenkins is a St. Petersburg native. He graduated from the Pinellas
County Center for the Arts at Gibbs High School — the same program that
produced Derek Donnelly — then earned a BFA from the Maryland Institute
College of Art. He tattoos at Fabled Trade Tattoo Company, a queer-owned
studio in the Lealman area, and keeps a studio at the ArtsXchange in the
Warehouse Arts District.

His murals tend toward photorealistic portraiture and botanicals — his SHINE
2021 piece at the St. Pete Distillery featured portraits of his two daughters
surrounded by sunflowers and greenery. He has noted that roughly a third of
mural pay goes to supplies alone. He collaborated with Iboms and Zulu Painter
on "Imagine Gas Plant & Laurel Park" at Campbell Park Recreational Center,
and painted the cheetah with geometric 90s-inspired colors at Star Booty
Salon on 16th Street South.

For SHINE Origins 2025, he painted a botanical piece at Founders Pro using a
projection method for the outline.

searchBio: |
He paints the people seldom seen in the 'Burg. He also tattoos them.

Reid Jenkins is a St. Petersburg native. He graduated from the Pinellas
County Center for the Arts (PCCA) program at Gibbs High School, then earned
a BFA from the Maryland Institute College of Art (MICA). He is a muralist,
painter, illustrator, and tattoo artist. He tattoos at Fabled Trade Tattoo
Company (formerly Ink Werkz Tattoo Studio), a queer-owned studio at 4195
54th Ave N in the Lealman area of St. Petersburg. He keeps a studio at the
ArtsXchange in the Warehouse Arts District.

His aim is to "paint the human experience and the experience of those seldom
seen in the 'Burg," focusing on capturing small, overlooked moments. His work
tends toward photorealistic portraiture and botanicals. He has noted that
roughly 33% of mural pay goes to supplies alone. He supports MIZE Gallery,
COVE in Pinellas Park, Woodfield Fine Art, and d-gallerie.

For SHINE 2021, he painted at the St. Pete Distillery — portraits of his two
daughters with sunflowers and greenery. He collaborated with Iboms and Zulu
Painter on "Imagine Gas Plant & Laurel Park" at Campbell Park Recreational
Center. Other murals include the large cheetah with geometric 90s-inspired
colors at Star Booty Salon (1741 16th St S). He exhibited in "Renew," a
group show with Bekky Beukes at Mergeculture, Tampa (February 2022).

For SHINE Origins 2025, he painted a botanical piece at Founders Pro, using
a projection method for the outline. Instagram: @reidmjenkins.

muralDescription: |
A stunning photorealistic botanical mural on the side of a commercial
building against a deep black background. An overflowing arrangement of
tropical flowers fills the wall — king proteas, red ginger, heliconia,
pincushion proteas, orchids, and ferns rendered in rich crimsons, pinks,
corals, golds, and greens. The hyperrealistic detail and dramatic dark
background give it the feel of a Dutch Golden Age still life painting
scaled up to building size.

muralInspiration: ""

## Sara Salem — We Are St. Pete (2025)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 2180 4th St N
**Building:** LMCU
**Instagram:** @sarasalemartsy

### Bio
She used VR to plan the composition before a single can of paint was opened.
Then she handed the wall to the community.

Sara Salem is originally from Egypt, now based in St. Petersburg. She holds a
BFA (2011), a 200-hour yoga teacher certification, Reiki Level II, and an art
therapy certification. She co-founded Phi Phi Artland with her husband
Fahd — a space that started as a martial arts facility and expanded into a
comprehensive arts and wellness hub combining fine arts, yoga, sound baths,
and Reiki.

She has over twelve years of teaching experience across Africa, Asia, and the
United States, working with students from kindergarten through adults. She
believes art is "a tool to achieve higher levels of well-being,
self-awareness." She started murals as a side hustle painting for friends and
found the process meditative.

For SHINE Origins 2025, she created "We Are St. Pete" — a community mural
wrapping around the LMCU Bank building. She designed it in VR, then opened it
to volunteers for a community weekend. After the public phase, she and her
advanced students spent seven more days refining to festival quality. "This
wasn't a mural for St. Pete," she said. "It was a mural created with St.
Pete."

muralDescription: |
A large mural on the side of a building featuring a colorful pelican
wearing a white baseball cap and reflective sunglasses that show St. Pete
landmarks in their lenses. The pelican's wings are spread wide in vibrant
pinks, oranges, blues, and deep reds. A golden spiral sun radiates in the
upper left corner, and "We Are St. Pete" appears in playful navy blue
script to the right. Signed "Phi Phi Artland @Sarasalemartsy" at the
bottom. The white background keeps the whole piece bright and inviting.

muralInspiration: ""

## Morning Breath (2015)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Brooklyn, NY
**Address:** 2042 Central Ave
**Instagram:** @morningbreathinc

### Bio
They met at Think Skateboards in San Francisco in 1996. They have designed
over a hundred album covers since.

Doug Cunningham and Jason Noto are Morning Breath Inc., a Brooklyn-based
design studio they formalized in 2002. Cunningham studied at the Academy of
Art University in San Francisco. Their style pulls from 1950s-1980s
Americana — comics, Mad magazine, punk rock, hip-hop, heavy metal, and
skateboard graphics — rendered in limited color palettes with bold lines.

The client list is absurd: Foo Fighters, Kanye West, Jay-Z, Eminem, Slayer,
Queens of the Stone Age, AFI. Brands: Vans, Adidas, Ray-Ban, Absolut Vodka,
MTV, KFC (a full restaurant redesign). They won the Grammy for Best Recording
Package in 2021 for Desert Sessions Vols. 11 and 12 — a Josh Homme project
with a mix-and-match booklet and sticker set. They have published two
monographs: The Early Bird (2007) and By the Skin of Our Teeth
(HarperCollins, 2016).

Their SHINE 2015 mural on Central Avenue is mostly hidden behind new
construction — only a narrow vertical slice survives. They returned for SHINE
2019 with a 101-foot-wide piece at 250 Dr. MLK Jr. Street North in their
signature black-white-and-red style: open lips, aviator sunglasses, a
smiling devil face.

muralDescription: |
Only a narrow vertical slice of Morning Breath's 2015 SHINE mural remains
visible, sandwiched between a blue-painted building at 2044 and a newer
white structure built in 2019 that obscured most of the original work.
The surviving fragment shows their signature style on a golden-yellow
background — bold black hand-lettering reading "WARNING," "UNSIGHTLY
CROOKED," "A WICKED EYEFUL!," "PANIC," "Have a SECOND LOOK," and "SIGNS
YOU NEED MONEY." A grinning skull, all-seeing eye, and the "Morning
Breath" nameplate at top are still intact. A barber pole stands in front
of the adjacent storefront. The address 2044 is visible on the blue
building to the left, with a palm tree partially blocking the lower
portion of the remaining mural.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description

visible, sandwiched between a blue-painted building at 2044 and a newer

## Zulu Painter (2025)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 31st St & 7th Ave S
**Instagram:** @zulupainter1

### Bio
He calls them "Mindful Murals." He wants to grow art everywhere.

Carlos Culbertson — Zulu Painter — is a native West Virginian who started
pursuing art at age eight. He holds a degree in fine arts painting and now
works out of a studio at the ArtsXchange on 22nd Street South. He paints in
acrylic and spray paint, favors reclaimed materials, and has worked as a
scenic artist for Disney on Ice, the Science Channel, HSN, and SeaWorld Parks
and Entertainment.

His mural projects span the country: Denver, Chattanooga, Atlanta, Austin,
Miami, Las Vegas, Los Angeles, Kansas City, Flint, and Binghamton, New
York — where he painted "Cultivate Kindness," a 40-foot mural with West Side
Middle School students. In St. Pete, he painted murals at CASA Family Justice
Center on Arlington Ave (domestic violence survivor themes) and a portrait of
Betty Reid Soskin at the Florida Botanical Gardens in Largo.

He is a four-time SHINE artist — "Moon Beam Dream 2" in 2016, "Legacy" with
Thirst in 2017, "Wisdom, Growth, Progress" in 2018, and the AMIKids wall in
2024. He received a St. Pete Arts Alliance Muse Award in 2022. He also
collaborated with Reid Jenkins and Iboms on "Imagine Gas Plant and Laurel
Park" at Campbell Park Recreational Center.

muralDescription: |
A vibrant mural on a cinder block wall blending multiple styles. The left
side features a photorealistic portrait of a young Black woman in profile
against a houndstooth pattern with a boombox rendered in grayscale. The
center and right side shift to a cartoon aesthetic — a wild-eyed character
with a mischievous grin faces off against a massive green alligator with
bared teeth, set against a turquoise geometric diamond pattern and black-
and-white checkerboard. Colorful polka dots in orange, pink, and yellow
punctuate the composition. Signed "zulupainter.com" in the lower right.

## Abys (2024)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Nancy, France
**Address:** 201 17th Street South
**Building:** Modera
**Instagram:** @abys_osmoz

### Bio
His first graffiti piece was a Rasta sheep with a joint. He was in high
school.

Julien Brouand — Abys — was born in 1991 in Nancy, France. His father put a
pencil in his hand at eighteen months. He originally wanted to design
characters for video games or cartoons. He has no formal art education. He
discovered graffiti in high school after meeting fellow artist Suroh, and
they formed Osmoz Colors together.

His style is cartoon-driven — whimsical, exaggerated creatures in lively
narrative scenes. A "fantastic bestiary" of jazzman rats, thieving raccoons,
gators, crabs. He describes each piece as "a freeze-frame in full action."
He works in spray paint, drawing, silk-screening, and tattoo. He cites
Mario, Ratchet & Clank, Zelda, Worms, and Banjo & Kazooie as influences.

He has painted across France — Paris, Marseille, Toulouse, Bordeaux, Calais,
Carcassonne — and in Luxembourg and Belgium. For SHINE 2024, he crossed the
Atlantic and painted a gator mural at Modera on 17th Street South in about
four days, plus a bonus crab at Anchor Skate Shop next door.

muralDescription: |
A six-story-tall mural on the side of the Modera apartment building
depicting a cartoonish, pot-bellied alligator with a mischievous grin,
riding a red surfboard through splashing turquoise waves. A pink hibiscus
flower sits atop the gator's head, and teal smoke curls above. The
surfboard reads "SHINE" in stylized lettering. At the base, a triangular
logo anchors the composition. The character's exaggerated features and
vibrant green-on-gray color scheme make it visible from blocks away.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A six-story-tall mural on the side of the Modera apartment building
depicting a cartoonish, pot-bellied alligator with a mischievous grin,
riding a red surfboard through splashing turquoise waves. A pink hibiscus
flower sits atop the gator's head, and teal smoke curls above. The
surfboard reads "SHINE" in stylized lettering. At the base, a triangular
logo anchors the composition. The character's exaggerated features and
vibrant green-on-gray color scheme make it visible from blocks away.

## Cristi López — Moo (2024)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 2655 Fairfield Ave S
**Building:** Groove Haven
**Instagram:** @cristi.lopez.s

### Bio
In college, she used unerasable media on purpose. It was exposure
therapy — she has OCD, and the inability to fix a line was the point.

Cristi Lopez is a first-generation American of Dominican, Spanish, and Cuban
descent. Her parents immigrated from the Dominican Republic. She grew up in
New England, relocated to Florida as a kid, and earned a BFA in Illustration
from Ringling College of Art and Design in 2016. She completed a drawing and
painting residency at Lillstreet Art Center in Chicago (2017-18) and moved to
St. Petersburg around 2023.

Her work explores mental illness, femininity, faith, and heritage. The
influences are specific: Vienna Secession, Symbolist painters, Candido Bido,
Dominican folk art, Ukiyo-e, Catholic iconography. Recurring motifs include
scissors — reclaiming a symbol of fear — cows, and Florida wildness. Her solo
show "Unravel: Portraits of My Obsessions" at A Very Serious Gallery in
Chicago (2023) featured fifteen paintings about her OCD, partnered with NAMI
Chicago.

She co-hosts Queer Contours, a donation-based figure drawing series with
Queer Expression St. Pete. She teaches vinyasa yoga. She received a 2025
Individual Artist Grant from the St. Pete Arts Alliance and was nominated for
Creative Loafing's Best of the Bay in both Best Muralist and Best Mural.

For SHINE 2024, she painted "Moo" at Groovehaven Music on Fairfield Avenue
South — her first Tampa Bay mural. She called it "a love letter to my visual
and cultural influences."

muralDescription: |
A large surrealist mural on the side of Groove Haven on a pale blue sky background. A central female figure with dark hair, green eyeshadow, rosy cheeks, and red lips gazes outward, wearing a pearl necklace and dark clothing. She holds a large maroon sphere with a single eye painted on it — a recurring motif in Lopez's work. Behind her, a full white moon glows against the horizon where a turquoise ocean meets the sky. Green tropical fern fronds frame the left side, while small white goats balance on a red clothesline to the right. A blue monkey sits among decorative green mandala patterns at the lower right. The composition blends Dominican folk art, Catholic iconography, and surrealist symbolism into a dreamlike tropical scene. Signed "Cristi López 24" in the lower right corner. Lopez described the piece as "a love letter to my visual and cultural influences."

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A large surrealist mural on the side of Groove Haven on a pale blue sky background. A central female figure with dark hair, green eyeshadow, rosy cheeks, and red lips gazes outward, wearing a pearl necklace and dark clothing. She holds a large maroon sphere with a single eye painted on it — a recurring motif in Lopez's work. Behind her, a full white moon glows against the horizon where a turquoise ocean meets the sky. Green tropical fern fronds frame the left side, while small white goats balance on a red clothesline to the right. A blue monkey sits among decorative green mandala patterns at the lower right. The composition blends Dominican folk art, Catholic iconography, and surrealist symbolism into a dreamlike tropical scene. Signed "Cristi López 24" in the lower right corner. Lopez described the piece as "a love letter to my visual and cultural influences."

## Dwayne Shepherd & Brian McAllister — The Importance of ART Education (2024)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 3535 Central Ave
**Building:** Bell Pharmacy
**Instagram:** @dwayneshepherd3499

### Bio
McAllister teaches visual arts at Gibbs High School's Pinellas County Center
for the Arts — the same program that trained Derek Donnelly and Reid Jenkins.
Shepherd is a Dali Museum docent who paints impressionist landscapes from his
Historic Kenwood home studio.

Brian McAllister holds an art degree from Ringling College of Art and Design.
He is an international muralist and illustrator whose work focuses on local
folklore, history, and culture. His SHINE 2019 piece at the historic Royal
Theater in the Deuces District featured Angela Bassett and the Griffin twins,
painted with Boys & Girls Club members and Gibbs High Mural Club students.

Dwayne Shepherd studied art, psychology, and theology at Austin Peay State
University. He works mostly in acrylics, inspired by nature. He is a member
of the Art Enclave Historic Kenwood and the Art Alliance of St. Petersburg.

For SHINE 2024, they collaborated together in St. Petersburg.

muralDescription: |
A vibrant marine-themed mural on the side of Bell Pharmacy celebrating art
education. The composition centers on an underwater scene with sea turtles
swimming among mangrove roots, tropical fish, and coral. Dolphins arc through
the water above while the text "The Importance of ART Education" anchors the
design. The mural was painted collaboratively with art educators from across
Florida during the FAEA annual conference in October 2024 as one of four
"Bright Spots" community murals in SHINE's 10th anniversary festival.

muralInspiration: ""

## Emmanuel Jarus (2024)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Toronto, Canada
**Address:** 226 5th Ave N
**Building:** Walker-Whitney
**Instagram:** @youngjarus

### Bio
He started by painting portraits on freight trains in Saskatchewan. They went
viral on the internet before he ever had a gallery show.

Emmanuel Jarus was born around 1992 in Regina, Saskatchewan. His grandmother,
a watercolor painter, introduced him to art at four or five. He started
graffiti around fourteen, left high school early, studied briefly at the
Academy of Realist Art in Toronto, and is otherwise self-taught. He credits
his grandmother, graffiti culture, and his mentor Kwest for his work ethic.

His style is post-Impressionist applied to massive walls — figurative
portraits of people he meets in his travels, painted with basic
hardware-store supplies: bucket paint, brushes, rollers, sometimes brooms.
"I observe life, characters and paint people I meet," he says. "Each painting
is a unique avatar for a city, a mood, memory or mostly a person."

He painted the United Nations 75th anniversary mural in Manhattan. A
17-story building in Boulogne-sur-Mer, France. A collaboration with
Francisco Bosoletti for Urban Nation's "One Wall" project in Berlin. A
glow-in-the-dark mural for Tourism Toronto. Festivals across Canada, the US,
and Europe.

For SHINE 2024, he painted the Walker-Whitney building at 226 5th Ave
North — 14 stories, roughly 180 by 60 feet. The largest mural in SHINE's
ten-year history.

searchBio: |
He started by painting portraits on freight trains in Saskatchewan. They went

Emmanuel Jarus was born around 1992 in Regina, Saskatchewan. His grandmother,

muralDescription: |
A 14-story photorealistic portrait of an enigmatic woman in flowing black
garments spanning the entire west-facing wall of the Walker-Whitney building —
the largest mural in SHINE's 10-year history at roughly 180 feet tall by 60
feet wide. The woman has long brown hair and one hand resting on her chest,
gazing out with a serene, mysterious expression against a muted gray-teal
background. Jarus intentionally keeps her identity a secret. The figure is a
recurring subject he first painted on a seaside structure in Boulogne-sur-Mer,
France. Painted with brushes, rollers, and bucket paint over 20 days —
interrupted by Hurricane Milton's evacuation — in time for SHINE's 10th
anniversary festival in November 2024.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description

background. Jarus intentionally keeps her identity a secret. The figure is a
recurring subject he first painted on a seaside structure in Boulogne-sur-Mer,
