## Fintan Magee (2024)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Australia
**Address:** 777 3rd Ave North
**Building:** Reflection St. Pete
**Instagram:** @fintan_magee

### Bio
He has been called the "Australian Banksy." He considers it lazy journalism.

Fintan Magee was born in 1985 in Lismore, New South Wales. His father, from
Derry, Northern Ireland, is a sculptor. His mother, English-born, is an
architect. He grew up around the political murals of the Irish tradition —
they stayed with him. He earned his BFA from the Queensland College of Art at
Griffith University in 2009 and started painting full-time the day he
graduated. He was already active as a graffiti writer before university.

His style is social realist — large-scale photorealistic figures on buildings
in isolated or abandoned urban locations, integrated with their surroundings.
Climate change, endangered species, migration, refugee crises, loss,
consumption. In Amman, Jordan, he painted "The Exile" — a refugee camp girl.
In Bali, "Plastic Tiger" — endangered species and colonialism's environmental
toll. In Berlin-Tegel, a 41.5-meter wall for Urban Nation's "One Wall"
project. He has painted in over twenty countries.

His recent work experiments with a "beveled glass technique" — subjects
appear out of focus, glass breaking and deconstructing images as allegory for
memory and lost time. He is represented by Thinkspace Projects in Los Angeles.

For SHINE's 10th anniversary in 2024 — postponed by Hurricanes Helene and
Milton — he painted a multi-story mural on the Reflection St. Pete building
at 777 3rd Ave North.

muralDescription: |
A four-story mural in shades of blue and teal covers the face of the
Reflection St. Pete building. The scene depicts objects and a figure
submerged underwater — a glass perfume-style bottle, crystalline
formations, and a pineapple float in rippling blue water. The
photorealistic rendering captures light refracting through the water
surface above. The building's "REFLECTION" signage sits just below
the mural's lower edge.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A four-story mural in shades of blue and teal covers the face of the
Reflection St. Pete building. The scene depicts objects and a figure
submerged underwater — a glass perfume-style bottle, crystalline
formations, and a pineapple float in rippling blue water. The
photorealistic rendering captures light refracting through the water
surface above. The building's "REFLECTION" signage sits just below
the mural's lower edge.

## Frankie Gonzalez (2024)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Tampa, FL
**Address:** 2606 Fairfield Ave South
**Building:** FloridaRAMA
**Instagram:** @frankieg_art

### Bio
He spent nine years in corporate shipping before coming back to paint.
Twenty-five days after hand surgery, he painted Tom Brady.

Francisco "Frankie G" Gonzalez was born in Miami to parents from Denmark and
Cuba. He started drawing comic book scenes as a kid, began painting seriously
at fifteen, and attended the Atlanta College of Art. After school he landed in
Salt Lake City and spent nine years in corporate transportation before
returning to Florida, quitting the desk, and going full-time on art. He took
a part-time job at a Tampa art store to learn the local scene while
rebuilding.

His style blends traditional painting techniques with spray paint and
latex — bold lines, vivid colors, compositions rooted in Latin heritage that
range from realism to pop art. He orchestrates mural camps with local schools
and aspiring young artists, and partners with Habitat for Humanity and the
Down Syndrome Foundation of Florida.

Clients include ESPN, the Tampa Bay Buccaneers, the Miami Heat, Norwegian
Cruise Lines, the Houston Astros, Ducati, and PODS Moving & Storage. He
painted "Tampa's LIGHT" at Tampa Fire Rescue headquarters — a visual timeline
of the department's 128-year history — and two murals for the Pinellas County
Tax Collector.

For SHINE 2024, he painted at FloridaRAMA on Fairfield Avenue South.

searchBio: |

muralDescription: |
A wide, energetic mural on the side of FloridaRAMA blending photorealism with
street art. At center, a boy in a baseball cap and tan shirt launches a paper
airplane, his outstretched arm dissolving into bold black calligraphic strokes
and cartoon-style characters. Oversized pink peonies, sunflowers, and white
daisies bloom along the bottom, while swirls of pink, yellow, orange, and
turquoise fill the background. The composition moves from lush botanical
realism on the left through the figurative center to playful abstraction on
the right, creating a dynamic sense of childhood imagination taking flight.

muralInspiration: ""

## George F Baker III — At Your Show (2024)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Atlanta, GA
**Address:** 620 1st Ave South
**Building:** Studio@620
**Instagram:** @gfb3

### Bio
He painted a mural on ESPN's College GameDay. Live, on air.

George F. Baker III grew up in Detroit, raised in Atlanta. He earned a BFA in
Graphic Design from Georgia State University in 2015, worked as a creative
marketing manager at a screen-printing company, and transitioned to full-time
muralist and illustrator. His first mural was in 2018 at Switchyards Downtown
Club in Atlanta. He has painted 25-plus walls since, across Miami, Atlanta,
Chicago, and beyond.

His style is playful and cartoon-inspired — larger-than-life characters in
bright bold color with positive messaging. "Take Care." "Be You." "Be A
Light." He cites Keith Haring, Nickelodeon cartoons, Pokemon trading cards,
and Dragon Ball Z as influences. He is creative director of Foster, a
studio-collective "striving to make people unafraid to play." He describes
himself as a sneakerhead with 78-plus pairs.

The client list reads like a brand deck: Nike, Adidas, Jordan Brand, Levi's,
ESPN, NFL, Cartoon Network, Adult Swim, Adobe, Mailchimp, Red Bull,
Patagonia, Mercedes-Benz. His "Be Playful" project for Mailchimp was
longlisted for the 2021 World Illustration Awards. His dream project is
designing a Nike sneaker to benefit L.E.A.D., a nonprofit mentoring young
Black boys in Atlanta.

For SHINE 2024, he painted at Studio@620 on 1st Avenue South.

muralDescription: |
A full-building mural on a black background packed with a joyful crowd of
cartoon-style characters rendered in bold pinks, teals, purples, blues, and
warm browns. The figures are chunky and expressive with exaggerated grins,
wild hair, and animated poses — dancing, laughing, taking selfies, playing
instruments, and celebrating. Across the top in large bubble letters with
teal outlines and cream fill reads "WE'RE ALL FRONT ROW AT YOUR SHOW."
White diamond-shaped dots scatter across the black background like confetti
or stars. The GFB3 tag appears in a small heart-shaped logo in the lower
right corner. The piece wraps around garage doors and windows, covering
the entire rear wall of Studio@620.

muralInspiration: ""

## Kris Markovich & Johnny Vitale (2024)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 501 16th St S
**Building:** Anchor Skate Supply
**Instagram:** @kris_markovich

### Bio
He turned pro for Dogtown Skateboards in 1990. Thirty years later, he paints
the walls.

Kris Markovich is a professional skateboarder who rode for Dogtown, appeared
on the cover of Transworld Magazine, featured in Tony Hawk's video games, and
was named one of the 15 Most Influential People in the Tampa Art Scene by
MergeCulture. He is self-taught as an artist, coming up through skateboard
graphics and graffiti. His largest mural is at Harrah's Casino in San Diego.
He has painted from the US West Coast to Dubai.

Johnny Vitale is the co-founder of Vitale Bros, painting Tampa Bay walls
since 1992. Together they are Anchor Skate Supply's resident artists.

For SHINE 2024, they collaborated on a mural at 501 16th Street South
(Anchor Skate Supply), inspired by the Tampa Bay Rays City Connect uniforms,
and produced a limited-edition SHINE mural skateboard called "L'Orange."

muralDescription: |
A bold, graphic mural wrapping around the entire exterior of Anchor Skate
Supply. The building is painted in a black base with vibrant tropical and
tiki-inspired imagery. A large pink and green sugar skull or tiki mask
dominates the left side, while cartoon-style palm trees and waves in neon
green and teal sweep across the front. A character with sunglasses peers
over the roofline. The right side features more black-and-white tribal/tiki
patterns and the text "OVER SPRAY ART COLLECTIVE." The whole building reads
as a single immersive artwork, turning the skate shop into a neighborhood
landmark.

muralInspiration: "Inspired by the Tampa Bay Rays' City Connect uniforms, which drew from St. Pete's skate culture, neon signs, tattoo culture, and street art."

## NeSpoon (2024)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Warsaw, Poland
**Address:** 601 24th St South
**Building:** Cemex
**Instagram:** @nes.nespoon

### Bio
NeSpoon is her legal name. It is on her ID. She changed it.

She grew up partly in West Berlin in the late 1980s, crossing Checkpoint
Charlie to attend Polish school in East Berlin. She wanted to be a real
artist from age five. She has no formal art degree — she taught herself
ceramics after hitting a dead end with abstract oil paintings.

In 2009, she started placing ceramic lace objects on walls in public spaces
in Warsaw. That became the work. She researches each city's lace heritage,
visits local museums, and uses region-specific motifs to create large-scale
spray-painted murals, ceramic installations, stencil work, and crocheted
pieces. She has painted in over forty countries across five continents.
"Instead of fighting evil," she says, "it's better to spread good."

In 2020, she covered the facade of the Cite de la Dentelle et de la Mode in
Calais — the city's lace museum — with a monumental mural using an 1894
archival pattern. She has shown at the Louvre-Lens, EXPO Dubai 2020, the
European Parliament, and Urban Nation in Berlin. She co-founded an NGO that
lobbied the Polish Parliament for seven years to pass legislation controlling
outdoor advertising.

She has a thirty-year project called "Thoughts" — making porcelain petals
from clay remnants, fifty kilograms a year, targeting 1,500 kilograms for a
final exhibition in 2042.

searchBio: |
NeSpoon is her legal name. It is on her ID. She changed it.

She grew up partly in West Berlin in the late 1980s, crossing Checkpoint
Charlie to attend Polish school in East Berlin. She wanted to be a real
artist from age five. She has no formal art degree — she taught herself
ceramics after hitting a dead end with abstract oil paintings.

In 2009, she started placing ceramic lace objects on walls in public spaces
in Warsaw. That became the work. She researches each city's lace heritage,
visits local museums, and uses region-specific motifs to create large-scale
spray-painted murals, ceramic installations, stencil work, and crocheted
pieces. She has painted in over forty countries across five continents.
"Instead of fighting evil," she says, "it's better to spread good."

In 2020, she covered the facade of the Cite de la Dentelle et de la Mode in
Calais — the city's lace museum — with a monumental mural using an 1894
archival pattern. She has shown at the Louvre-Lens, EXPO Dubai 2020, the
European Parliament, and Urban Nation in Berlin. She co-founded an NGO that
lobbied the Polish Parliament for seven years to pass legislation controlling
outdoor advertising.

She has a thirty-year project called "Thoughts" — making porcelain petals
from clay remnants, fifty kilograms a year, targeting 1,500 kilograms for a
final exhibition in 2042.

muralDescription: |
A wide mural on the Cemex building featuring NeSpoon's signature lace patterns
in white and pale blue against a deep plum-purple background. Intricate
medallions with floral and geometric motifs occupy the left and center, their
delicate threadwork rendered with illusory three-dimensional depth through
careful shading and layering. The right side transitions into flowing,
organic swirls that evoke wind or waves. The effect transforms the industrial
concrete wall into what appears to be an enormous draped lace textile. Signed
"NeSpoon" in the lower left corner.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A wide mural on the Cemex building featuring NeSpoon's signature lace patterns
in white and pale blue against a deep plum-purple background. Intricate
medallions with floral and geometric motifs occupy the left and center, their
delicate threadwork rendered with illusory three-dimensional depth through
careful shading and layering. The right side transitions into flowing,
organic swirls that evoke wind or waves. The effect transforms the industrial
concrete wall into what appears to be an enormous draped lace textile. Signed
"NeSpoon" in the lower left corner.

## Quinn Cale (2024)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Tampa, FL
**Address:** 225 2nd Ave North
**Building:** Main Grove
**Instagram:** @quinnsessential

### Bio
She started painting as a teenager on wood slats because she couldn't afford
to go out with friends. Now she makes the paintings move.

Quinn Cale is from Middletown, Delaware, now based in Tampa. She has no
formal art degree — she is self-taught. She worked in sales at Apple, then
became an Apple Creative around 2020, teaching children digital art on iPads.
That pivot from tech to art stuck.

Her style channels late-90s and early-2000s childhood nostalgia — arcade
machines, tropical motifs, instruments, TV-headed figures — in bold, surreal
color compositions. She pioneers augmented reality overlays on her paintings
using Adobe Aero: viewers scan with a phone and the work animates. Her SHINE
2024 mural at 225 2nd Ave North featured an animated cat and mouse in AR.

She has exhibited at MOSI in Tampa and the Straz Center's First Annual Black
Art show. For SHINE Origins 2025, she painted at Tyrone Middle School.

searchBio: |
She started painting as a teenager on wood slats because she couldn't afford
to go out with friends. Now she makes the paintings move.

Quinn Cale is from Middletown, Delaware, now based in Tampa, Florida. She is
approximately 32 years old (as of late 2024). She has no formal art degree —
she is self-taught, starting with paint on scrap wood as a teenager. She
worked in sales at Apple, then became an Apple Creative around 2020, teaching
children digital art on iPads.

Her style channels late-90s and early-2000s childhood nostalgia — arcade
machines, tropical motifs, instruments, TV-headed figures — in bold, surreal
color compositions using surrealism and color theory, with themes of animals
and space. She pioneers augmented reality overlays on her paintings using
Adobe Aero (no-code platform): viewers scan with an iPad or phone and the
work animates.

For SHINE 2024, she painted a mural at 225 2nd Ave North (Main Grove) in St.
Petersburg, featuring an animated cat and mouse with AR. For SHINE Origins
2025, she painted at Tyrone Middle School (2375 66th St N). She has exhibited
at MOSI (Tampa) and the Straz Center "First Annual Black Art" show. She has a
profile on RAW Artists. Website: quinncale.com.
Instagram: @quinnsessential.

muralDescription: |
A large cat rendered in electric purples, teals, oranges, and pinks
leaps across the wall, chasing a pixelated orange mouse and a retro
cursor hand icon. The cat's fur is broken into bold color blocks that
read like a neon stained-glass window. The whole piece pops against
a dark teal and sage background.

muralInspiration: ""

## Stevie Shao (2024)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Seattle, WA
**Address:** 850 5th Ave South
**Building:** Volunteers of America
**Instagram:** @stepfrae

### Bio
She had never painted a mural before April 2020. She painted her first one
on plywood covering a boarded-up business during lockdown. It went viral.

Stevie Shao is a second-generation Chinese American whose parents immigrated
from Shanghai in the 1990s. She grew up in Seattle's Magnolia neighborhood,
one of few Asian-American students in predominantly white schools. She
studied interior design at Seattle Pacific University, where an illustration
course under professor Rachell Sumpter introduced her to Chinese-American
illustrators and changed her trajectory. She worked as an interior designer
at a Seattle architecture firm until she was laid off in the summer of 2020.
She has not gone back.

Her style is vibrant and saturated — hot pink, marine blue, kohl black.
Flattened two-dimensional forms inspired by folk art, Chinese ceramics,
traditional American tattoos, and Pacific Northwest wildlife meeting Chinese
folklore. Seattle Magazine named her one of the city's 25 Most Influential
People reshaping the region.

The client list came fast: Anna Sui used her designs in a fashion collection.
Starbucks Reserve commissioned an exclusive artist merchandise line. The
Seattle Kraken had her design their Women in Hockey jersey. Then Google,
Nordstrom, REI, Sub Pop Records, Russell Wilson.

For SHINE 2024, she painted at the Volunteers of America building on 5th
Avenue South.

searchBio: |
She had never painted a mural before April 2020. She painted her first one

muralDescription: |
A multi-story mural on the side of the Volunteers of America building
depicting a sinuous, serpentine dragon in bold navy blue, red, gold, and
turquoise against a white background. The dragon coils upward across the
full height of the building, its body decorated with pink scalloped scales
and chartreuse dots. Two stylized birds with spread wings — one near the top
and one at the bottom — intertwine with the dragon's twisting form. Blue
flames curl from the dragon's mouth. The flat, graphic style draws on Chinese
folk art and traditional tattoo aesthetics. Signed "Stevie Shao 24" in the
lower right corner.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description

depicting a sinuous, serpentine dragon in bold navy blue, red, gold, and

## Up & Over (2024)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Tampa Bay, FL
**Address:** 201 17th Street South
**Building:** Modera
**Instagram:** @upandovermurals

### Bio
Tes One co-founded the SHINE Mural Festival. The Vitale Bros have been
painting Tampa Bay walls since 1992. In 2020, they started painting together.

Up & Over is Leon "Tes One" Bedore and the Vitale Brothers — Johnny, Paul,
and crew. Tes One started tagging at fourteen in 1992, came up through the
Pinellas County Center for the Arts at Gibbs High School, and built a career
spanning Nike, L'Oreal, Absolut Vodka, Toyota, The New Yorker, Architectural
Digest, and the Brooklyn Nets. In 2012 he curated "Leave a Message" at the
Morean Arts Center — the show that planted the seed for SHINE. He co-founded
the festival in 2015. His thirty-year retrospective "Good Intentions" showed
at the Morean in 2022, and he was featured in the Dali Museum's "Outside In"
exhibition in 2025.

Together, their style merges Tes One's bold graphic design and typography
with the Vitale Bros' figurative realism and pop culture sensibility. Their
wraparound mural for First Citrus Bank in downtown St. Pete is a local
landmark. They have also painted for the Tampa Bay Buccaneers and Duke Energy.

For SHINE's 10th anniversary in 2024, they painted at 2214 2nd Ave North — a
full-circle moment: the festival's founder painting alongside the city's most
prolific muralists.

searchBio: |
Tes One co-founded the SHINE Mural Festival. The Vitale Bros have been
painting Tampa Bay walls since 1992. In September 2020, they started painting
together under the name Up & Over.

Leon "Tes One" Bedore (born c. 1977) started tagging at fourteen in 1992 and
came up through the Pinellas County Center for the Arts at Gibbs High School.
He built a career spanning Nike, L'Oreal, Absolut Vodka, Toyota, The New
Yorker, Architectural Digest, MTV, and the Brooklyn Nets. In 2012 he curated
"Leave a Message" at the Morean Arts Center — the show that planted the seed
for what became the SHINE Mural Festival. He co-founded and co-curated the
inaugural festival in 2015, which brought 17 murals to downtown and has since
produced nearly 170. His thirty-year retrospective "Good Intentions" showed
at the Morean in 2022. He was featured in the Dali Museum's "Outside In"
exhibition in 2025. Awards include Urban Excellence Social Impact Award
(Tampa Downtown Partnership, 2017), Arts Ambassador Award (St. Petersburg
Arts Alliance, 2016), and Best Public Art and Best Art Festival (Best of the
Bay, 2015). Featured in Juxtapoz Magazine, Flair Magazine, and Designing
Obama.

The Vitale Brothers — Johnny, Paul, and crew including Nick and Jason Kekllas,
Bruce Statton, and Jake Gerow — have been painting since 1992. Their warehouse
studio hosted legendary art and music shows in the late 1990s that helped
build St. Pete's arts scene. Clients include the Tampa Bay Rays, Tampa Bay
Buccaneers, and L'Oreal SalonCentric.

Together as Up & Over, they painted murals for First Citrus Bank (downtown
St. Pete wraparound mural), the Tampa Bay Buccaneers, and Duke Energy. For
SHINE's 10th anniversary in 2024, they painted at 2214 2nd Ave North.
Instagram: @upandovermurals.

muralDescription: |
A towering abstract geometric mural spanning the full height of the Modera
apartment building — roughly five stories. Bold, interlocking organic shapes
in saturated primary and secondary colors — red, teal, orange, yellow, deep
blue, maroon, and white — overlap and flow across the facade. The forms are
rounded and architectural, somewhere between hard-edge abstraction and
biomorphic design, with circles nested inside flowing vertical bands. The
color relationships are carefully balanced, creating a sense of depth and
movement. The scale dominates the block and is visible from several streets
away.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description

## Pantonio (2016)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Lisbon, Portugal
**Address:** 1901 Central Ave
**Building:** Imagine Museum
**Instagram:** @pantonio.o

### Bio
His sixty-six-meter fish mural on a Paris housing tower was the tallest
piece of street art in Europe when it went up in 2014.

António Correia — Pantonio — grew up on Terceira Island in the Azores,
surrounded by the Atlantic. He studied sculpture, drawing, and photography
in the Azores and graphic arts at the Polytechnic Institute of Tomar before
moving to Lisbon in the 1990s. He worked in animation design, advertising,
and children's illustration before dedicating himself to urban art full-time
around 2012.

His signature is swarms of marine life — sardines, cod, manta rays,
octopus — rendered in Atlantic blues, volcanic blacks, and seafoam whites
with simple, fluid lines that make the walls feel like they are moving. The
palette comes straight from the Azores. He has painted walls in over
eighteen countries, from a Paris bridge (Pont des Arts, after the city
removed the love locks) to grain silos on his home island.

For SHINE 2016 he wrapped two walls of the Imagine Museum on Central Avenue
with a swirling school of dark fish and aquatic foliage in black, gray, and
teal — one of the festival's largest pieces, spanning roughly a hundred and
thirteen feet across the corner of the building.

searchBio: |
His sixty-six-meter fish mural on a Paris housing tower was the tallest
piece of street art in Europe when it went up in 2014.

António Correia — Pantonio — was born in 1975 on Terceira Island in the
Azores, Portugal. He studied sculpture, modeling, drawing, and painting at
the Instituto da Cultura dos Acores, then drawing and photography at the
Professional School of Artistic Crafts in Vila Nova de Cerveira starting in
1989, and graphic arts technologies at the Polytechnic Institute of Tomar.
He moved to Lisbon in the 1990s and worked professionally in animation
design, advertising, and children's illustration. In 1993 he represented
Portugal at UNESCO's fiftieth anniversary celebration in Romania. In 2005 he
received a distinction at the Macaronesian Young Artists Show for
illustration. He dedicated himself exclusively to urban art and fine art
around 2011-2012.

His signature subjects are swarms of marine life — sardines, cod, manta
rays, octopus — along with birds, rabbits, ropes, boats, and fantastical
creatures. His palette draws from the Azores: Atlantic blues, volcanic
blacks, seafoam whites, with occasional greens and purples. The style blends
realism with abstraction through simple, flexible, fluid lines and
undulating compositions that create a sense of movement on static walls.

Major murals include the Tour Sienne in Paris's 13th arrondissement (2014,
sixty-six meters high by fifteen meters wide — the largest street art mural
in Europe at the time, taking one month to complete), the Pont des Arts
bridge in Paris (2015, painted after the city removed the love-lock
grilles), the Aquarium of the Pacific in Long Beach, California (2016, his
first US mural, a manta ray on approximately 2,600 square feet), and grain
silos on Terceira Island in the Azores. He has painted in over eighteen
countries including Portugal, France, Italy, Spain, Belgium, Denmark,
Poland, Sweden, Ukraine, Russia, China, Brazil, Canada, the United States,
Tunisia, Morocco, Senegal, and Cape Verde.

He is represented by Galerie Itinerrance in Paris. Solo exhibitions include
"Exodus" at Galerie Itinerrance (2014), "Atlantida" at Galerie Itinerrance
(2017), and "Vento" at the Instituto Acoriano de Cultura in the Azores
(2019).

For SHINE 2016 he wrapped the east and north walls of the Imagine Museum at
1901 Central Avenue in St. Petersburg with a swirling underwater scene of
black fish and aquatic plant life, approximately 113 feet wide by 19 feet
high. The piece is rendered in black, gray, and teal tones, full of fluid
movement.

Festival credits include SHINE 2016 (St. Petersburg), POW! WOW! Long Beach
(2016), Artscape White Moose Project (Karlstad, Sweden, 2017), Big City
Life (Rome, 2015), and Fresh Paint OLLN (Louvain-la-Neuve, Belgium, 2021).

Instagram: @pantonio.o.

muralDescription: |
A massive wraparound composition covering two walls of the Imagine Museum.
Swirling schools of dark fish — rendered in black, charcoal gray, and deep
teal — spiral and twist through pale aquatic foliage in mint green and sage
tones against a light background. The fish move in dense, fluid formations
that create a vortex-like sense of motion across the full width of the
building. Broad leaves and plant forms weave between the fish, adding depth
and organic rhythm. The piece wraps around the corner of the building,
creating a continuous underwater scene visible from multiple angles.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A massive wraparound composition covering two walls of the Imagine Museum.
Swirling schools of dark fish — rendered in black, charcoal gray, and deep
teal — spiral and twist through pale aquatic foliage in mint green and sage
tones against a light background. The fish move in dense, fluid formations
that create a vortex-like sense of motion across the full width of the
building. Broad leaves and plant forms weave between the fish, adding depth
and organic rhythm. The piece wraps around the corner of the building,
creating a continuous underwater scene visible from multiple angles.

## Zulu Painter (2022)

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** Arlington Ave N
**Building:** CASA Family Justice Center
**Instagram:** @zulupainter1

### Bio
He calls them "Mindful Murals." He wants to grow art everywhere.

Carlos Culbertson — Zulu Painter — is a native West Virginian who started
pursuing art at age eight. He holds a degree in fine arts painting and now
works out of a studio at the ArtsXchange on 22nd Street South. He paints in
acrylic and spray paint, favors reclaimed materials, and has worked as a
scenic artist for Disney on Ice, the Science Channel, HSN, and SeaWorld Parks
and Entertainment.

His mural projects span the country: Denver, Chattanooga, Atlanta, Austin,
Miami, Las Vegas, Los Angeles, Kansas City, Flint, and Binghamton, New
York — where he painted "Cultivate Kindness," a 40-foot mural with West Side
Middle School students. In St. Pete, he painted murals at CASA Family Justice
Center on Arlington Ave (domestic violence survivor themes) and a portrait of
Betty Reid Soskin at the Florida Botanical Gardens in Largo.

He is a four-time SHINE artist — "Moon Beam Dream 2" in 2016, "Legacy" with
Thirst in 2017, "Wisdom, Growth, Progress" in 2018, and the AMIKids wall in
2024. He received a St. Pete Arts Alliance Muse Award in 2022. He also
collaborated with Reid Jenkins and Iboms on "Imagine Gas Plant and Laurel
Park" at Campbell Park Recreational Center.

searchBio: |
He calls them "Mindful Murals." He wants to grow art everywhere.

Carlos Culbertson — Zulu Painter — is a native West Virginian who started

muralDescription: |
Four diverse faces fill the two-story CASA Family Justice Center
building, representing different demographics affected by domestic
violence. On one side, a woman cradles a baby, with words embedded
in the curls of her hair — "Drive, Bravery, Family, Growth, Hope,
Empathy, Love, Community, Wisdom, Faith, Create, and Unity." The
other side depicts a young woman extending a helping hand and a young
man making direct eye contact. Colorful geometric patterns, blue
polka dots, swirling vines, and flowers in purples and golds
surround the figures. A pathway of white polka dots and purple
triangles crosses Arlington Avenue as part of the design.

muralInspiration: |
As Zulu Painter described the faces: "They're everyone... people from
our community who have in some way been affected by family turmoil."

muralAwards: ""

## Andrea Wan — Earth Song (2023)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Vancouver, BC
**Address:** 155 17th St S
**Instagram:** @andrea_wan

### Bio
She used to be a piano teacher. Her client list includes Apple, The New
York Times, and Microsoft.

Andrea Wan was born in Hong Kong in 1985 and moved to Vancouver at ten.
Her parents were graphic designers. She studied Film, Video and Integrated
Media at Emily Carr University of Art and Design, then spent six months at
Designskolen Kolding in Denmark — where she developed her current drawing
style. She lived in Berlin for eight years before returning to Vancouver
around 2019.

Her work is surrealist narrative in ink on paper and paint on walls —
human figures dissolving into botanical forms, celestial elements, and
subconscious landscapes. She describes each drawing as "a still image from
a scene in an ongoing story." Influences include European fables, Chinese
folklore, and Roald Dahl. Clients include Apple, The New York Times,
Microsoft, Absolut, AirBnB, Ray-Ban, Wired, and Lululemon. She is
represented by Cosmopola in Berlin.

For SHINE 2023 she painted "Earth Song" at 155 17th St S — surreal figures
intertwined with plant forms in blue and lavender against a warm yellow
dome, at the gateway between the Pinellas Trail and the Grand Central
District.

searchBio: |
She used to be a piano teacher. Her client list includes Apple, The New
York Times, and Microsoft.

Andrea Wan was born in 1985 in Hong Kong and moved to Vancouver, BC at age
ten. Her parents were graphic designers. She studied Film, Video and
Integrated Media at Emily Carr University of Art and Design in Vancouver,
then illustration and design at Designskolen Kolding in Denmark
(approximately six months, circa 2008), where she developed her current
drawing style. She lived in Berlin from 2012 to approximately 2019 and is
now based in Vancouver.

Her work is surrealist, narrative-driven — human figures blended with
botanical and celestial elements, rendered primarily in ink on paper and
paint on walls. She describes her practice as "a journal of thoughts
processed through a fictional filter" and each drawing as "a still image
from a scene in an ongoing story." Themes include nature, personal
mythology, identity, interconnectedness of nature and human consciousness,
psychology, and spirituality. Influences include European fables, Chinese
folklore, Roald Dahl, dreams, and the subconscious mind.

Commercial clients include Apple, The New York Times (editorial
illustrations for NYT Magazine), Microsoft, Mozilla, Absolut, AirBnB,
WeTransfer, Ray-Ban, Jagermeister, Wired Magazine, Lululemon, Herschel
Supply Co., and Poketo. She is represented by Cosmopola (Berlin) and has
exhibited with Affenfaust Galerie (Hamburg) and Rabbit Eye Movement
(Vienna). She has been featured in Juxtapoz, Hi-Fructose, i-D Magazine,
and on CBC Arts.

Solo exhibitions include "Pen Pals" at Idrawalot Gallery, Berlin (2012),
"Mirror Mirror" at Rabbit Eye Movement, Vienna (2014), and "Reunion" at
Urban Spree Galerie, Berlin (2022). She has shown at Spoke Art (San
Francisco), Giant Robot Gallery (LA), Superchief Gallery (Brooklyn),
Gallery Nucleus (LA), and CASS Contemporary (Tampa). She completed
residencies in India and a teaching residency in Iran (both 2019).

Mural credits include SHINE 2023 ("Earth Song," 155 17th St S, St.
Petersburg), POW! WOW! Hawaii (2013, collaborative with Jeff Hamada and
Wu Yue), Vancouver Mural Festival 2016 ("Handhug," 3010 Main Street),
BUMP Festival 2024 in Calgary ("Maa," 1302 4 St SW), and walls in
Germany, Italy, Denmark, and Finland. She has exhibited in New York, Paris,
Berlin, San Francisco, Brussels, Barcelona, Stockholm, and Rome.

Instagram: @andrea_wan. Website: andreawan.com.

muralDescription: |
A large-scale mural on a wide building wall set against a bright yellow
background shaped in a soft dome. The composition features surreal, stylized
human figures intertwined with nature — flowing blue and lavender forms merge
with botanical leaves, stems, and flower petals. A golden sun or moon sits
at the left, with waterdrop shapes falling from above. The palette centers
on cool blues, lavenders, and teals contrasted against the warm yellow
background, with accents of pink, green, and gold. The figures appear to
grow from or dissolve into the plant forms, creating a dreamlike scene
of humans and nature as one continuous organism.

muralInspiration: "The deep connection and balance of life on our planet."

### Mural Description
A large-scale mural on a wide building wall set against a bright yellow
background shaped in a soft dome. The composition features surreal, stylized
human figures intertwined with nature — flowing blue and lavender forms merge

muralInspiration: "The deep connection and balance of life on our planet."

## Artist Jones (2023)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Tampa, FL
**Address:** 350 2nd Ave N
**Building:** PSTA Ticket Office
**Instagram:** @theartistjones

### Bio
Her great-great grandmother was a Chester County painter. The family has been
at it for generations.

Tracey Jones — Artist Jones — is based in Tampa. She holds a degree in
graphic design and spent ten-plus years as a graphic and web designer before
transitioning to fine art painting. She was born in Trinidad and Tobago, and
the Caribbean tones show — vibrant-colored portraits of powerful Black and
brown people in mixed media, with composition informed by her design training.

Clients include the Tampa Bay Lightning, the Tampa Bay Buccaneers, Outback,
and Walmart. She participated in the City of Tampa's Art on the Block program
and the Tampa Bay Born Murals series. For SHINE 2023, she painted in St.
Petersburg.

searchBio: |
Her great-great grandmother was a Chester County painter. The family has been
at it for generations.

Tracey Jones — Artist Jones — is based in Tampa. She holds a degree in
graphic design and spent ten-plus years as a graphic and web designer before
transitioning to fine art painting. She was born in Trinidad and Tobago, and
the Caribbean tones show — vibrant-colored portraits of powerful Black and
brown people in mixed media, with composition informed by her design training.

Clients include the Tampa Bay Lightning, the Tampa Bay Buccaneers, Outback,
and Walmart. She participated in the City of Tampa's Art on the Block program
and the Tampa Bay Born Murals series. For SHINE 2023, she painted in St.
Petersburg.

muralDescription: |
A vibrant full building wrap covering every surface of the small PSTA Ticket Office at Williams Park. The mural is a patchwork of bold, densely layered abstract patterns — oversized fan-shaped leaves in hot pink and sky blue, swirling navy spirals, lime green tiger stripes, red-orange organic shapes, yellow leopard spots, teal swirls, and geometric black-and-white dashes and lines. The patterns collide and overlap in irregular blocks, creating a quilt-like effect that draws from African textile traditions and graphic design. The saturated color palette — pink, blue, green, orange, yellow, navy, and teal — wraps seamlessly around corners and over utility boxes, transforming the entire structure into a single vibrant artwork. Signed "@theartist jones" in white at the bottom left.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A vibrant full building wrap covering every surface of the small PSTA Ticket Office at Williams Park. The patterns collide and overlap in irregular blocks, creating a quilt-like effect that draws from African textile traditions and graphic design. The saturated color palette — pink, blue, green, orange, yellow, navy, and teal — wraps seamlessly around corners and over utility boxes, transforming the entire structure into a single vibrant artwork. ## Bryan Beyung & James Lee Chiahan — When the Sun is Up, the Moon is Absent! (2023)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Montreal, Canada
**Address:** 2875 7th Ave S
**Building:** Stewart Moving & Storage
**Instagram:** @bryanbeyung

### Bio
Bryan Beyung painted a mural on a bulk carrier — the first produced on a
cargo ship in Canada. James Lee Chiahan's clients include Apple, The New
Yorker, and The New York Times. They paint together.

Beyung was born in Montreal to a Chinese-Cambodian refugee family. He studied
graphic design, started as a graffiti artist as a teenager, and built a
practice exploring diasporic heritage through memories and images. His work
won the International Corporate Art Awards in Italy. He has painted across
the US, Canada, Haiti, France, Morocco, and Cambodia.

Chiahan was born in 1990 in Tainan, Taiwan, and is self-taught. His oil
paintings and charcoal drawings explore the bittersweetness of mundane
moments. He won the Canadian Magazine Awards Grand Prize for Cover Art (2024)
and was shortlisted for the Jackson's Art Prize. He is represented by
Pierre-Francois Ouellette art contemporain in Montreal.

For SHINE 2023, they collaborated on "When the Sun is Up, the Moon is
Absent!"

muralDescription: |
A two-panel mural on the Stewart Moving & Storage building. The left side
features a large black circle on a golden background containing a white tree
silhouette and crescent moon — based on a drawing by six-year-old Amelia Kien
— with the word "ABSENT" below and colorful childlike figures along the bottom
painted by the Kien family. The right side shows a photorealistic sunset
landscape with four family members — two adults and two children — standing on
a hillside, connected by a red textile panel with traditional patterns. A navy
blue band along the base depicts fish silhouettes. The mural tells the story
of the Kien family, who fled Cambodia in 1987 and eventually settled near
Bradenton, Florida, just minutes from this wall.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description

## Bunnie Reiss — Phoebe & Friends (2023)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Landers, CA
**Address:** 2700 Central Ave
**Building:** MTBH Studios
**Instagram:** @bunnieluvrocks

### Bio
Bunnie Reiss is a muralist based near Joshua Tree, California who paints
massive folk-art walls full of animals, flowers, and dense patterning. She
grew up in a Polish/Russian family in Colorado, studied literature there,
and worked for NPR before earning her MFA from the San Francisco Art
Institute. She spent 20+ years in the Bay Area before relocating to a
5-acre art farm called Lilac Lane Farms in the Mojave Desert town of
Landers. Her style draws from Eastern European folk traditions, Mexican
folk art, Russian fairy tales, and Jewish mysticism — all filtered through
a maximalist color palette. She's painted walls in over 20 countries and
has had solo shows at Superchief Gallery LA and exhibitions at Paradigm
Gallery in Philadelphia, La Luz de Jesus, and Luna Rienne in San Francisco.
She also built "The Sky Rabbit's House," a giant rabbit sculpture on a
street-legal truck chassis. Her work has been featured in Juxtapoz,
Colossal, Vice, and Hi-Fructose.

muralDescription: |
Three elaborately patterned pink flamingos stride across the wall on long
dark legs, each bird filled with intricate floral and geometric details in
shades of pink, coral, and lavender. They step over a blue-violet rainbow
arch with rolling green hills beneath. Golden flower clusters bloom on the
right side against a bright teal sky dotted with small clouds and birds.
The piece covers the entire side of the building.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description

## John Vitale (2025)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 601 1st Ave N
**Building:** The Sage
**Instagram:** @vitalebros

### Bio
Their first mural came from a sketch on a paper napkin, handed to a
restaurant owner. That was 1992.

Johnny Vitale and his brother Paul started Vitale Bros in Tampa Bay
hand-lettering signs and painting storefront windows. Their parents are
artists. So were their grandparents. No formal training — just family
tradition and thirty-plus years of practice. Their late aunt Nancy, who had
Down syndrome and attended Parc Center for Disabilities, turned out to be an
artist too, discovered in her final decade.

In the late 1990s they opened a warehouse studio that hosted group art,
music, and fashion shows that helped birth St. Pete's emerging art scene.
They collaborated on set design for Harmony Korine's Spring Breakers. They
have painted for the Tampa Bay Rays, the Buccaneers, L'Oreal, and dozens of
local businesses. They call themselves the "mural police" — restoring damaged
walls around the city for free.

In 2023, Johnny launched Overspray, a street art shop and graffiti school now
based in Gulfport, teaching tagging, block letters, Wildstyle, and advanced
mural technique to classes of up to twenty students. After Hurricane Milton,
they painted free signs for businesses that lost theirs.

They have painted SHINE every year since its inception — "The Cool Kids" in
2015, "Bait and Switch" in 2019, and multiple walls since. For SHINE Origins
2025, Johnny painted two solo murals: one on the 600 block where it all
began, and another at The Factory on Fairfield Ave South with his daughter
Sionna.

searchBio: |
Their first mural came from a sketch on a paper napkin, handed to a
restaurant owner. That was 1992.

Johnny Vitale and his brother Paul founded Vitale Bros in 1992 in Tampa Bay,
starting with hand-lettered signs and painted storefront windows. Youngest
brother Joey later joined. Their parents are artists — the family artistic
tradition goes back to their grandparents. No formal art education has been
documented; the craft appears entirely self-taught and family-transmitted.
Their late aunt Nancy had Down syndrome and attended Parc Center for
Disabilities, where staff discovered her artistic talent in her final decade
of life.

In the late 1990s and early 2000s, they opened a warehouse studio that hosted
legendary group art, music and fashion shows credited with helping birth St.
Pete's emerging art scene. They collaborated in set design for Harmony
Korine's Spring Breakers (filmed in the St. Pete area). Their style blends
pop art, graffiti, and traditional mural techniques — fine art meets pop art
sensibility, incorporating local culture, historical elements, and visual
stories reflecting community identity.

Clients include the Tampa Bay Rays, Tampa Bay Buccaneers, L'Oreal /
SalonCentric (national locations), Anchor Skate Supply, and Great Bay
Distributors, plus numerous local businesses and private residences. Notable
murals include the Marilyn Monroe on the side of the historic Playhouse
Theater on Central Ave, a Virgin Mary at St. Pete Catholic High School,
Power to the Patients giant fist at the Morean Arts Center, and the Nueva
Cantina restaurant mural in Tampa. They describe themselves as the mural
police — restoring damaged murals around the city for free.

The current Vitale Bros team includes Johnny, Paul, Nick and Jason Kekllas,
Bruce Statton, and Jake Gerow. They have also collaborated under the name
Up and Over with Tes One, and painted behind the State Theatre with Tes One
and Palehorse.

In July 2023, Johnny launched Overspray — a retail street art shop and
graffiti school, initially on the Pinellas Trail, relocated to 5013 20th Ave
S. in Gulfport. Overspray teaches graffiti, tagging, block and bubble
letters, Wildstyle, and advanced mural classes to groups of up to 20
students. The five-artist teaching team includes John Vitale, Nicole Salgar,
Hinson Thehealer, Digital (LA), and Jujmo. After Hurricane Milton, they
painted free signs for businesses that lost theirs. They host events like
Hot Walls during SHINE.

SHINE participation: The Cool Kids (2015, 2063 Central Ave — faces of the
artists' children), Bait and Switch (2019 — red snapper and sea turtle),
SHINE 2022, SHINE 2024 (mural at Anchor Skate Supply with pro skateboarder
Kris Markovich, inspired by Rays City Connect uniforms; limited-edition
skateboard L'Orange). For SHINE Origins 2025, Johnny painted two solo
walls: 600 1st Ave N (the 600 block where SHINE began) and The Factory at
2606 Fairfield Ave S (collaboration with Sionna Sio Macleish, celebrating
St. Pete history and landmarks).

Studio: 5013 20th Ave S., Gulfport, FL. Website: vitalebrothers.com.
Instagram: @johnvitale (solo), @vitalebros (collective).

muralDescription: |
Orange blossoms and ripe oranges painted on the vertical columns and edges
of the sage-green Sage building. White flowers with yellow centers sit
among deep green leaves and bright blue accents, with whole and halved
oranges tucked between them. The design wraps the structural columns of
the building, turning the architecture itself into a tropical citrus
garden. The style is clean and illustrative with bold outlines, giving
the building a fresh Florida feel that nods to the state's citrus heritage.
Adjacent to Marina Capdevila's 2022 SHINE mural on the front face of the
same building.

muralInspiration: ""

## Chris Dyer — Ride on the sPEACEship! (2023)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 2875 7th Ave S
**Building:** Stewart Moving & Storage
**Instagram:** @chris_dyer

### Bio
He has painted in forty-five countries. His mural is in the Bob Marley Museum.

Chris Dyer was born in Ottawa in 1979, moved to Lima, Peru at four, and
returned to Canada at seventeen. He studied illustration at Dawson College in
Montreal and trained under visionary masters Alex Grey, Robert Venosa, and
Amanda Sage. His style fuses psychedelic imagery, Incan and pre-Columbian
influences, sacred geometry, and 1980s skate culture.

He founded the Positive Creations brand in 2003 — clothing, tapestries,
books, skateboards, footwear. He has been art director at Creation
Skateboards and designed over a hundred commercial skateboard graphics for
Santa Cruz, Deathwish, and Think. He has done live painting for Diplo,
Tipper, and Immortal Technique at festivals from Burning Man to Boom to
Rainbow Serpent. He published two books and sold out 5,555 NFTs in 2021.

He now lives in a purple house in Gulfport with his partner Paulina Casati.
For SHINE 2023, he painted "Ride on the sPEACEship!" on Stewart Moving and
Storage — a wildly detailed spaceship made of interlocking creatures trailing
rainbow exhaust flames.

searchBio: |

muralDescription: |
A panoramic cosmic scene against a starry black background on Stewart Moving &
Storage. A wildly detailed spaceship made of interlocking creatures, tentacles,
and geometric patterns blasts across the wall, trailing rainbow exhaust flames
and lightning bolts. The vessel is piloted by psychedelic characters with
multiple eyes and Incan-inspired headdresses, one flashing a peace sign from
inside a blue bubble cockpit. "Positive Creations" floats above in rainbow
lettering, "PEACE PLEASE" appears in a speech bubble at right, and a smiling
sun face radiates in the upper right corner. Saturn, UFOs, and alien faces
orbit the composition. Signed "Chris Dyer" at left and "LOVE!" in pink at
lower right.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A panoramic cosmic scene against a starry black background on Stewart Moving &
Storage. A wildly detailed spaceship made of interlocking creatures, tentacles,
and geometric patterns blasts across the wall, trailing rainbow exhaust flames

## Dave Bonzai — Sunshine City (2023)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** London, England
**Address:** 1229 Central Ave
**Building:** Cappa & Cappa
**Instagram:** @davebonzai

### Bio
He paints in chrome. No chrome paint — just black, white, and grey, layered
until it looks like liquid metal.

Dave Bonzai is from Southampton, England, now based in London. He has been
doing street art for over twenty years. In 2016 he developed a technique he
calls "Liquid Chrome" — hyper-realistic chrome effects created entirely with
spray paint, incorporating reflections of the surrounding environment (buses,
windows, buildings) for realism. One of those early pieces, "One Liquid
Chrome," is in the permanent collection of the STRAAT Museum in Amsterdam.

He has painted across the UK, Northern Ireland (Get Up Derry festival), and
internationally. Clients include Triumph Motorcycles (a mural at their
factory visitor experience in Hinckley) and Denham's House Guest Artist
series. Nature runs through the chrome — animals and organic forms rendered
in metallic illusion.

For SHINE 2023, he painted "Sunshine City" at 1229 Central Avenue.

searchBio: |

until it looks like liquid metal.

Dave Bonzai is from Southampton, England, now based in London. He has been

muralDescription: |
A striking mural on the side of Cappa & Cappa on Central Avenue featuring the words "Sunshine City" in Bonzai's signature Liquid Chrome technique — the script lettering appears to be made of dripping, reflective molten metal, rendered entirely with spray paint in shades of silver, white, and grey. The chrome text floats over a tropical sunset scene of palm tree silhouettes against a gradient sky of deep purple, hot pink, and fiery red. A green geometric tree shape with horizontal stripes rises behind the lettering, and sharp diagonal lines in pink and white cut across the black background, giving the piece a retro synthwave energy. Signed "@DaBonzai" in the lower right corner. Bonzai described it as "a modern piece with a retro twist that the people of St Pete could relate to."

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A striking mural on the side of Cappa & Cappa on Central Avenue featuring the words "Sunshine City" in Bonzai's signature Liquid Chrome technique — the script lettering appears to be made of dripping, reflective molten metal, rendered entirely with spray paint in shades of silver, white, and grey. The chrome text floats over a tropical sunset scene of palm tree silhouettes against a gradient sky of deep purple, hot pink, and fiery red. A green geometric tree shape with horizontal stripes rises behind the lettering, and sharp diagonal lines in pink and white cut across the black background, giving the piece a retro synthwave energy. Signed "@DaBonzai" in the lower right corner. Bonzai described it as "a modern piece with a retro twist that the people of St Pete could relate to."

## Fabstraq — The Energy of Color (2023)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Tampa, FL
**Address:** 2724 6th Ave S
**Building:** Clay Coop
**Instagram:** @fabstraq919

### Bio
He started making art at ten to process emotions he couldn't name. He is now
a certified art therapy life coach.

Shawn Rainey — Fabstraq — is based in Tampa. He has no formal art education.
His style is contemporary abstract — vibrant colors and defining lines,
one-of-a-kind pieces meant to be thought-provoking. He also works as an
audio engineer and music producer, and writes poetry.

He volunteers regularly at the Glazer Children's Museum and has done charity
shows for the National Breast Cancer Foundation and Chemo Angels. Bay News 9
profiled him as an "Everyday Hero" for his community work with children. His
eventual goal is to travel worldwide establishing art programs.

For SHINE 2023, he painted at Charlie Parker Pottery on 6th Avenue South.

muralDescription: |
A bold, graphic mural covering the entire front face of the Clay Coop
building. The composition is a patchwork of large geometric shapes —
triangles, rectangles, and angular forms — in saturated flat colors:
bright green, royal blue, hot pink, red, purple, yellow, and teal. A
heart shape, a star, zigzag patterns, and concentric rectangles are
scattered throughout the design. The overall effect is like an abstract
quilt or a Memphis Design-inspired collage, with clean edges and no
blending between the color fields. Two tiki statues flank the entrance,
and the artist's "FABSTRAQ" tag appears in the lower right corner.

muralInspiration: ""

## Greater Public Studio — Dive Into Discovery (2023)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Tampa, FL
**Address:** 6th Ave S & 2nd St S
**Building:** USF Campus
**Instagram:** @greaterpublicstudio

### Bio
Three friends bet on themselves in 2017. They opened a studio in Tampa.

Greater Public Studio was founded by Jay Giroux in Tampa, Florida. Giroux
brings fifteen-plus years across painting, photography, printmaking, digital
media, environmental graphics, and installation. The studio provides brand
strategy, hand-painted murals, fabrication, installation design, curatorial
services, and environmental graphics.

Their work explores incidental gestures and lowbrow symbolism from the urban
streetscape, balancing popular culture with high formalism. Recent work
emphasizes non-objective forms over representation. Some of their murals
include augmented reality layers viewable through the Artivive app.

Clients include the Tampa Bay Buccaneers (mural at BAR HWRD in Tampa's SoHo
District) and venues across Florida. For SHINE 2023, they painted in St.
Petersburg.

A colorful, illustrative mural spanning a large wall near the USF St.
Pete campus. The composition features a fantastical townscape with
stacked buildings, winding paths, and small figures exploring the scene.
Bright blues, oranges, and greens fill the design in a style that feels
like an oversized storybook illustration.

## Hannah Eddy — With Flying Colors (2023)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Reno, NV
**Address:** 1019 Arlington Ave N
**Building:** CASA Support Center
**Instagram:** @hannaheddyart

### Bio
She grew up on the coast of Maine. Right out of college, she started
collaborating with snowboard and skate companies.

Hannah Eddy is based in Reno, Nevada. She trained in oil painting and moved
straight into outdoor brands — Patagonia, Dakine, BlackStrap, Sierra Nevada
Brewing Co., Protect Our Winters, and Dedicated, a Swedish sustainable
clothing label. Her style is colorful pop-surrealism: bold linework, graphic
dynamism, women and animals at exaggerated scale, joyful imagery undercut by
skulls, cobwebs, and flames alongside technicolor mountains and forests.

She paints murals because "no one has to pay to see it, and there's no
barrier to entry." She has painted across the US, including Reno and Oklahoma
City. For SHINE 2023, she brought her work to St. Petersburg.

searchBio: |

Hannah Eddy is based in Reno, Nevada. She trained in oil painting and moved

muralDescription: |
A wide, whimsical mural covering the full side of the CASA Support Center on a soft mint green background. The scene is a dense tropical ecosystem rendered in bold black outlines with playful, illustration-style fills in blues, greens, purples, oranges, and pinks. A large green tree frog sits center-left among oversized leaves and flowers, while a purple heron or crane flies to the right carrying a flower in its beak — a nod to the traditional peace dove, reimagined with Florida wildlife. Snakes wind through the foliage, insects hover between blooms, and cartoon raindrops fall from stylized clouds. The left side bursts with warmer tones — orange, yellow, and pink — while the right side fades to line-art outlines on the mint base, as if the color is still spreading. The piece was designed to create a positive, lighthearted, and welcoming atmosphere for CASA's mission as a domestic violence support center.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A wide, whimsical mural covering the full side of the CASA Support Center on a soft mint green background. Snakes wind through the foliage, insects hover between blooms, and cartoon raindrops fall from stylized clouds. The left side bursts with warmer tones — orange, yellow, and pink — while the right side fades to line-art outlines on the mint base, as if the color is still spreading. ## HOXXOH — Rainbird (2023)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Miami, FL
**Address:** 2025 3rd Ave S
**Building:** Morean Workshop Space
**Instagram:** @hoxxoh

### Bio
"I'd rather be more like Diego Rivera than Banksy. I'm an artist; I'm a
painter who sometimes paints murals."

Douglas Hoekzema — HOXXOH — was born in 1980 in Boulder, Colorado and grew
up in Fort Lauderdale. He started graffiti at fifteen. He earned a five-year
degree in architecture with double majors in art history and painting, plus a
philosophy minor, graduating in 2008. After school he worked at a
salvaged-wood lumber mill while developing his spray technique behind a
tattoo shop.

His signature is massive-scale op-art — concentric circles, psychedelic
spirals, logarithmic patterns executed freehand in spray paint. He also uses
paint pendulums, garden sprinklers, and dust pans. His work is guided by
sacred geometry and time. He joined Miami Style Graffiti crew around 2008 and
now works from a studio in Little River, Miami.

He painted a 135-foot mural on Miami's Arlo Hotel, walls across Wynwood and
Overtown, and projects in Paris, Grenoble, New York, Philadelphia, Detroit,
and Sacramento. He is a two-time SHINE artist — 2015 and 2023.

searchBio: |

muralDescription: |
A huge panoramic piece spanning the full length of the Morean Workshop
building. Three birds in flight — likely roseate spoonbills or herons —
are rendered in HOXXOH's signature black-and-white ripple-line technique,
their bodies made up of dense, undulating wave patterns that give them an
almost holographic shimmer. The birds soar across a luminous teal and
aquamarine background that itself ripples with flowing white and dark
streaks, making the entire wall feel like it's in motion. The scale is
impressive — each bird spans a full story of the building. It reads as
abstract pattern from a distance and resolves into recognizable bird forms
as you get closer.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description

impressive — each bird spans a full story of the building. It reads as

## Kelly Quinn — Mangrove Connections (2023)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 2553 1st Ave N
**Building:** LPL Financial
**Instagram:** @kellyofthewild

### Bio
She was Artist-in-Residence at The Florida Aquarium — live-painting,
teaching classes, collaborating with educators. Conservation is the work.

Kelly Quinn grew up in rural Florida and graduated from the University of
Florida's College of Fine Arts in 2016. That same year she founded Kelly of
the Wild. In 2018 she co-founded Canvas of the Wild with Blake Wheeler,
where she serves as art director. Her focus is wildlife and nature — digital
illustration, acrylic painting, and large-scale conservation-driven murals.

Clients include The Florida Aquarium, the Florida Wildlife Corridor
Foundation (Crystal River Mural), Weedon Island Preserve, Coral Restoration
Foundation, Biscayne National Park, the National Parks Association, the
Oceanic Preservation Society, and the International Sea Keepers Society —
which named her Artist of the Year in both 2021 and 2022.

She is based in St. Petersburg. For SHINE 2023, she painted locally.

muralDescription: |
A nature-focused mural on a black background depicting the mangrove ecosystem of Tampa Bay. A central mangrove tree with tangled brown roots and dense green foliage spans the full height of the wall, with a large golden sun glowing behind it. Blue ocean waves splash outward on both sides. Above the canopy, a pink roseate spoonbill takes flight on the left while a brown pelican perches on the right. Below the waterline, the mangrove roots reveal an underwater world — a smalltooth sawfish (an endangered Florida species), a dolphin, a snook, a red snapper, and smaller fish swim among the roots. The piece was created in collaboration with the Oceanic Preservation Society to celebrate the 50th anniversary of the Endangered Species Act, and includes interactive NFC-tag technology linking viewers to a digital knowledge base about each species depicted.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A nature-focused mural on a black background depicting the mangrove ecosystem of Tampa Bay. A central mangrove tree with tangled brown roots and dense green foliage spans the full height of the wall, with a large golden sun glowing behind it. Blue ocean waves splash outward on both sides. Below the waterline, the mangrove roots reveal an underwater world — a smalltooth sawfish (an endangered Florida species), a dolphin, a snook, a red snapper, and smaller fish swim among the roots. The piece was created in collaboration with the Oceanic Preservation Society to celebrate the 50th anniversary of the Endangered Species Act, and includes interactive NFC-tag technology linking viewers to a digital knowledge base about each species depicted.

## Loretta Lizzio — The Sirens Call (2023)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Melbourne, Australia
**Address:** 2875 7th Ave S
**Building:** Stewart Moving & Storage
**Instagram:** @loretta_lizzio

### Bio
She was the first female artist commissioned for Melbourne's Hellenic
Museum — painting three fearless women from ancient Greek history and myth.

Loretta Lizzio was born in 1986 and is based on the Gold Coast, Australia.
Her work lives in Romanticism and nostalgia — "forgotten fairy tales,
spectacles of cinema, fleeting glances and dog-eared National Geographics."
She works across painting, murals, illustration, photography, and collage.

She has painted across Melbourne, Vancouver, London, Brisbane, and Darwin.
Festival credits include Vancouver Mural Festival, Wall to Wall Festival
Benalla, Brisbane Street Art Festival, Wonderwalls Festival Port Kembla, and
Darwin Street Festival. Clients include New Balance, Toyota x Virgin
Airlines, Element, and Wacom. She is represented by The Drawing Arm in
Australia and has shown at Haven Gallery in New York, Nucleus Gallery in
Portland, and Beinart Gallery in Melbourne. Featured in Juxtapoz and
Beautiful Bizarre Magazine.

For SHINE 2023, she traveled from Australia to paint in St. Petersburg.

muralDescription: |
A wide mural on the Stewart Moving & Storage building depicting three women
as sirens from Greek mythology against a deep teal-green background. The
figures emerge from lush green foliage, their pale skin overlaid with
intricate leaf patterns and delicate line work that makes them appear to grow
from the plants themselves. Their dark hair flows and intertwines across the
top of the composition, creating a canopy effect. The sirens gaze outward
with serene yet subtly unsettling expressions — beautiful and sinister at
once. Signed "Loretta Lizzio" in the upper right corner.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description

once. Signed "Loretta Lizzio" in the upper right corner.

## Max Sansing — Shining Hope (2023)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Chicago, IL
**Address:** 401 5th St N
**Building:** Trinity Lutheran Church
**Instagram:** @maxsansing

### Bio
Virgil Abloh hired him to paint a Nike mural at a Boys and Girls Club in
Chicago. He painted the NBA All-Star broadcast team — Ernie, Shaq, Kenny,
and Charles — during All-Star Weekend.

Max Sansing came up through Chicago's graffiti crews DC5, RK, and CYA before
entering the Gallery 37 Center for the Arts youth program and spending two
years at the American Academy of Art. His style fuses street art dynamism
with photorealistic technique — large-scale portraits of African Americans
celebrating identity and community, in oil, acrylic, spray paint, and enamel
on wood panels, canvas, and aluminum.

His client list includes Nike, the Chicago Fire FC ("Stand For Chicago" mural
in South Loop), and celebrity portraits of Barack Obama, Kris Bryant, Walter
Payton, and Fred Hampton. He has shown at Thinkspace Projects in LA, POW!
WOW! Hawaii, and Scope Miami Beach.

For SHINE 2023, he painted in St. Petersburg.

muralDescription: |
A multi-story portrait on the north face of Trinity Lutheran Church depicting
a young Black woman gazing outward with quiet intensity. Her face is rendered
in photorealistic detail with warm brown skin tones, while her body dissolves
into swirling, psychedelic layers of green, turquoise, purple, and gold —
evoking geode cross-sections and topographic forms. A large hibiscus flower
in peach and violet blooms at her shoulder, and deep blue flowers anchor the
bottom of the composition. A red archway silhouette with palm trees appears
within the flowing forms, and a bird silhouette flies above. A SHINE plaque
is visible at the base.

muralInspiration: ""

## Michael Vasquez — The Mangrove Refuge (2023)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg/Miami, FL
**Address:** 2063 Central Ave
**Building:** Little Philly
**Instagram:** @michaelvasquez_

### Bio
His work hangs in the Smithsonian. Swizz Beats and Michael B. Jordan collect
him.

Michael Vasquez earned his BFA cum laude from the New World School of the
Arts in Miami in 2005. His figurative paintings explore identity in
marginalized neighborhoods — transforming personal photographs into dramatic
compositions where clothing, hand signs, tattoos, and neighborhood landmarks
establish who someone is and where they come from.

He was included in "Portraiture Now: Staging the Self" at the Smithsonian
National Portrait Gallery (2014-15) and "The Culture: Hip Hop and
Contemporary Art in the 21st Century" at the Baltimore Museum of Art (2023).
His work is in the Perez Art Museum Miami, the Microsoft Art Collection, and
the Rubell Museum. He has shown at the Saatchi Gallery in London, Wynwood
Walls, and the Kemper Museum of Contemporary Art.

He is a two-time SHINE artist — 2015 and 2023.

muralDescription: |
A large photorealistic scene depicting a group of young men hanging around
a beige Datsun 280Z parked at a waterfront spot with mangroves visible in
the background. One figure sits shirtless on the hood, another leans against
the car in a white t-shirt, and others stand or crouch nearby — all in
jeans and casual wear that evoke a timeless, late-70s or early-80s Florida
vibe. The painting captures a specific moment of youthful camaraderie, with
the car as the gathering point. The detail in the faces, clothing, and
vehicle is remarkably precise, and the mangrove setting grounds the scene
in coastal Florida.

muralInspiration: "Depicts a displaced Lao family finding refuge at Gandy Beach circa 1980. The 1978 Datsun 280Z is a point of pride fusing car culture and masculinity. Clothing and style signal togetherness while solitary gazes allude to deeper personal histories."

### Mural Description

a beige Datsun 280Z parked at a waterfront spot with mangroves visible in
the background. One figure sits shirtless on the hood, another leans against
the car in a white t-shirt, and others stand or crouch nearby — all in
jeans and casual wear that evoke a timeless, late-70s or early-80s Florida
vibe. The painting captures a specific moment of youthful camaraderie, with
the car as the gathering point. The detail in the faces, clothing, and
vehicle is remarkably precise, and the mangrove setting grounds the scene
in coastal Florida.

## Rhys Meatyard (2023)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 2210 1st Ave S
**Building:** The Werk Gallery
**Instagram:** @the_meatyard

### Bio
His great-uncle was Ralph Eugene Meatyard, the pioneering photographer. His
grandfather Jerry was a metal sculptor. He is third-generation.

Rhys Meatyard graduated from the Pinellas County Center for the Arts at
Gibbs High School, then studied fine art and anthropology at Eckerd College.
He has been a graphic designer and illustrator since 2007. He is also a
former stone carver who designed tombstones and marble inscriptions, and has
done high-end jewelry photography retouching.

His style is digital and traditional — pop-culture-centric work with dark
humor and surreal elements, watercolors, gouache, acrylics, colored pencils,
and found ephemera like vintage maps and sheet music. He reimagines
mythological figures into whimsical, irreverent character studies. He is a
non-binary, trans-masculine artist who found his artistic voice in his late
thirties after overcoming homelessness and single parenthood.

He is art director for Friend Club Records and designed the logo for Meatyard
Ybor, an affordable art studio complex in Ybor City. For SHINE 2023, he was
an invited participating artist.

muralDescription: |
A dreamy pastel mural inspired by classical sculpture and 1970s prog rock album art. Blue-toned Greek and Roman statues stand on pedestals within a colonnade of soft lavender arches. As the scene moves right, the temple begins to break apart — columns float upward and the ground fragments — while the furthest figure steps off her base and gazes into golden sun rays breaking through pink and tan clouds. String lights hang across the top of the building.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description

As the scene moves right, the temple begins to break apart — columns float upward and the ground fragments — while the furthest figure steps off her base and gazes into golden sun rays breaking through pink and tan clouds. String lights hang across the top of the building.

## Sara Sheppard — We Are Magic (2023)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 2336 5th Ave S
**Building:** New Dawn Coffee Co
**Instagram:** @sarah.g.sheppard

### Bio
Her father was her mother's college art teacher. Both parents are artists.

Sarah Sheppard grew up in Dallas, studied photography, drawing, painting,
graphic design, ceramics, and fabric art at the University of North Texas,
and has lived in St. Petersburg for over twelve years. She also studied
metalsmithing at the Creative Jewelry Academy, continuing work her late
father began.

Her style is colorful, high-contrast figurative work — new romanticism,
movement as metaphor for continuous loss and seeking. She started her mural
career painting for a spa in St. Petersburg after earlier work in Venetian
plaster and faux finishing for model homes. She has multiple SHINE murals
across the city, including "Life Reimagined" on the back wall of the Morean
Arts Center, commissioned by AARP.

For SHINE 2023, she returned to paint in St. Petersburg.

muralDescription: |
A wide mural on a black background centered on a pair of large, detailed
eyes gazing out from behind an ornate golden mask-like structure. The face
is flanked by crescent moons, a starburst crown, and colorful tropical
flowers — hibiscus in pink and teal. White feathered wings spread out on
both sides, and elaborate gold filigree scrollwork curls outward to fill
the edges of the wall. The words "We are all Magic" appear in script
across the lower center. The overall effect is mystical and symmetrical,
like a gilded altar piece reimagined as street art.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description

across the lower center. The overall effect is mystical and symmetrical,

## The Happy Mural Project — Girl Power (2023)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 2727 6th Ave South
**Building:** CrossFit 9
**Instagram:** @thehappymuralproject

### Bio
Their motto is "Honk if You're Happy." Their first mural was painted the
week the world shut down.

Alyssa Marie and Braden Everly are The Happy Mural Project — a married
couple who relocated from Colorado to St. Petersburg in 2017. Alyssa is a
fine artist who opened a gallery on Beach Drive in 2018 (closed January
2020). Braden has a degree in special education and worked as a house painter
in Colorado. Their first mural went up at Lolita's Catering in early March
2020, days before lockdown.

Their signature is oversized sunflower murals in vibrant color, plus
community-participatory "color by number" walls. Their SHINE 2020 piece
"Hands of Change" at the City Theatre featured hands holding sunflowers
representing racial and cultural diversity. For SHINE 2023, "Girl Power" was
a color-by-number mural completed by Girl Scouts of West Central Florida.
They are three-time SHINE artists — 2020, 2022, and 2023.

A young girl in a blue shirt stands face-to-face with a dappled gray horse, surrounded by giant golden sunflowers against a teal background. The Happy Mural Project logo is signed in the lower right corner.

## Chenlin Cai — The Path We Came / 我们来的路 (2023)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Philadelphia, PA
**Address:** 1927 4th Ave S
**Building:** Lantmännen Unibake
**Instagram:** @chenlin.cai

### Bio
She fuses traditional Chinese ink painting with X-ray imagery and
inside-bottle painting craft. She holds MFAs from both Tsinghua University in
Beijing and the Pennsylvania Academy of Fine Arts.

Chenlin Cai was born in 1984 in Fujian, China, and relocated to the United
States in 2014. Her work explores Chinese American identity through cellular
structures, non-traditional surfaces, and multi-media expression. Her SHINE
2023 "Bright Spot" mural, "The Path We Came," was a collaboration with the
James Museum — exploring the Chinese American experience and the
Transcontinental Railroad.

In Philadelphia, she worked with Mural Arts Philadelphia on a 21-story mural
on the Crane Building in Chinatown celebrating 150-plus years of community,
alongside Eurhi Jones and Yvonne Lung. She painted koi fish on the sidewalks
of 10th Street Plaza and murals inside Chinatown restaurants.

Her work is in the Copelouzos Family Art Museum of Contemporary Arts in
Athens, Greece. She has shown at the 12th Florence Biennale, Freeman's
Auction Gallery in Philadelphia, and 456 Gallery in New York.

searchBio: |

muralDescription: |
A wide mural on the Lantmännen Unibake building depicting three Chinese
American railroad laborers rendered in blue-gray tones against a dramatic
sunset of deep orange, gold, and purple. The three figures — one holding a
staff, one reading a letter, one planting a flower with his hat removed —
represent family longing, hope, and resilience. Behind them, golden mountain
peaks evoke the Sierra Nevada, with a setting sun and crescent moon
embodying the duality of aspiration and homesickness. A steam locomotive
and train cars wind along railroad tracks at right. The mural reimagines
Chinese laborers who built the First Transcontinental Railroad but were
excluded from its 1869 completion photographs. Created in partnership with
the James Museum's "From Far East to West" exhibition.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A wide mural on the Lantmännen Unibake building depicting three Chinese
American railroad laborers rendered in blue-gray tones against a dramatic
sunset of deep orange, gold, and purple. The three figures — one holding a
staff, one reading a letter, one planting a flower with his hat removed —
represent family longing, hope, and resilience. Behind them, golden mountain
peaks evoke the Sierra Nevada, with a setting sun and crescent moon
embodying the duality of aspiration and homesickness. A steam locomotive
and train cars wind along railroad tracks at right. The mural reimagines
Chinese laborers who built the First Transcontinental Railroad but were
excluded from its 1869 completion photographs. Created in partnership with
the James Museum's "From Far East to West" exhibition.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## 123Klan — Libre et Sauvage (2022)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Venice Beach, CA
**Address:** 128 20th St S
**Building:** Tri-J Towing
**Instagram:** @123klan

### Bio
They named the crew after their zip code — 59123, Bray-Dunes, France. That
was 1992.

Scien (Sebastien) and Klor (Carole) are 123Klan, a married graffiti duo who
started on the walls of northern France and ended up designing for Nike,
Adidas, Marvel, Netflix, Sony, MTV, ESPN, Stussy, and Vans. They moved from
France to Montreal in 2007, then to Venice, California. Along the way they
founded the BANDIT1SM streetwear brand and published three books.

Their style is what happens when street knowledge meets graphic design —
sharp-angled letters, vivid colors, razor-sharp color blocks, influenced by
Japanese cartoons, North American mascots, and pop art. They have exhibited
in over sixty shows globally, from Pow Wow Hawaii to Art Basel Miami to Urban
Art Fair Paris.

For SHINE 2022, they painted in St. Petersburg.

searchBio: |

muralDescription: |
A large mural on the side of Tri-J Towing in 123Klan's signature cartoon-graffiti style on a lavender background. The central figure is a cowboy outlaw character in a dark fedora with an arrow pierced through it, wearing an open Hawaiian shirt with a tropical print, rendered in their angular vector-meets-spraypaint aesthetic. To his left, a ghost-like figure with X'd-out eyes holds a flag, surrounded by cacti, a skull, and bold graffiti-style lettering reading "123" and "Go." The text "Libre et Sauvage" ("Free and Wild") runs across the bottom — a recurring 123Klan motif representing the untamed spirit of graffiti culture. Bullet hole details and crossed arrows anchor the composition at the base. The palette is muted pastels — lavender, sage green, rust, and navy — a departure from their typical acid brights.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A large mural on the side of Tri-J Towing in 123Klan's signature cartoon-graffiti style on a lavender background. The central figure is a cowboy outlaw character in a dark fedora with an arrow pierced through it, wearing an open Hawaiian shirt with a tropical print, rendered in their angular vector-meets-spraypaint aesthetic. To his left, a ghost-like figure with X'd-out eyes holds a flag, surrounded by cacti, a skull, and bold graffiti-style lettering reading "123" and "Go." The text "Libre et Sauvage" ("Free and Wild") runs across the bottom — a recurring 123Klan motif representing the untamed spirit of graffiti culture. Bullet hole details and crossed arrows anchor the composition at the base. The palette is muted pastels — lavender, sage green, rust, and navy — a departure from their typical acid brights.
