## Ashley Cantero (2022)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Tampa, FL
**Address:** 2421 Central Ave.
**Building:** Fonda Nails & Spa
**Instagram:** @ashleycantero

### Bio
She describes her work as "dark, intricate, sensual and, at times,
humorous." The 1960s psychedelia and Atomic Age design are deliberate.

Ashley Cantero is a Tampa native — visual artist, muralist, and illustrator.
Her style features precise line work and bold female characters with intense
emotional energy, drawing from retro charm, the supernatural, and sci-fi. At
FloridaRAMA she created immersive 3D diorama installations ("Drive-In,"
"Swamp Motel") and an eight-wall retro-futuristic mural, "Cosmic Dreams and
Sunshine Beams."

For SHINE 2022, she painted at 2421 Central Avenue.

muralDescription: |
A retro sci-fi scene spanning the full wall — three flying saucers beam up
figures against a teal background loaded with mid-century diamond shapes and
starbursts. A suited figure stands on a vanishing-point highway while a
green alien peers out from the right side. The palette is teal, pink, and
chartreuse with heavy black outlines giving it a vintage comic book feel.

muralInspiration: ""

starbursts. A suited figure stands on a vanishing-point highway while a
green alien peers out from the right side. The palette is teal, pink, and
chartreuse with heavy black outlines giving it a vintage comic book feel.

## Ben Johnston — Rise (2022)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Toronto, Canada
**Address:** 1010 Central Ave
**Instagram:** @ben_johnston

### Bio
He drew a 1,485-square-metre mural for Sierra Nevada Brewery with nothing
but Sharpies and scaffolding.

Ben Johnston grew up in Cape Town, South Africa, briefly studied industrial
design, and is otherwise self-taught. He is now based in Toronto. His work is
large-scale typographic murals with 3D illusory effects — bespoke typefaces,
signage, and illustration referencing Art Nouveau and pop art. The words are
often chosen with local communities: "Keep Going," "Relax," "Love,"
"Together."

Clients include Nike, Lululemon, Adobe, and Apple (where he also speaks). He
has won multiple Graphis Gold and Silver awards, Type Directors Club awards,
and ADC One Club Gold Cubes including Best of Discipline. He has painted for
MURAL Festival Montreal, BUMP Festival Calgary, and SeaWalls / Pangeaseed
with the UN. His "Wordplay" solo sculpture exhibit was featured by CBC Arts.

For SHINE 2022, he painted in St. Petersburg.

muralDescription: |
A towering multi-story mural on the side of an apartment building spelling "RISE" in massive 3D block letters. The isometric letterforms are rendered in graduating shades of cerulean, sky blue, and white against a deep blue background, with crisp shadows giving the typography a sculptural, floating quality. Signed "Ben" in white script at the lower left. The mural dominates the building's full height, visible from blocks away along Central Avenue.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description

The isometric letterforms are rendered in graduating shades of cerulean, sky blue, and white against a deep blue background, with crisp shadows giving the typography a sculptural, floating quality. Signed "Ben" in white script at the lower left. The mural dominates the building's full height, visible from blocks away along Central Avenue.

## Dream Weaver (2022)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 1510 1st Ave. N.
**Instagram:** @dream.weavin

### Bio
She painted her first mural at seven, on her aunt's backyard shed in Waco,
Texas. She has not stopped.

Taylor Smith — Dreamweaver — grew up between Portland, Oregon and just south
of Atlanta, born in Dallas. She studied communications and fine art at Georgia
College and State University, with a minor in graphic design, graduating in
2017. Before that, she took private painting lessons as a kid and apprenticed
at a tattoo shop at 16 — too young to actually tattoo under Georgia law, but
old enough to learn.

She spent time doing live painting at music festivals — Electric Daisy
Carnival in Vegas, Okeechobee, Electric Forest — before relocating to Florida
around 2019. She works almost entirely in spray paint, blending expressive
abstraction with realism. Nature runs through everything: wildlife, water,
birds, flora. She is left-handed.

She has painted an estimated 120 to 150 murals, 15 to 20 in St. Pete alone.
Other projects include the Clearwater Municipal Parking Garage and a sea
turtle mural at Coachman Park for Art Oasis 2024, and "The Garden Within" in
downtown Kissimmee for Osceola Arts. She is a resident artist at AER Tampa
and a member of Few and Far, an international women's graffiti crew.

For SHINE she painted in 2022 at 1510 1st Ave N and returned for SHINE
Origins 2025 at the Vernis Bowling building on the 600 block — where the
SHINE movement started in 2012.

searchBio: |
She painted her first mural at seven, on her aunt Carolyn's backyard shed in
Waco, Texas. She has not stopped.

Taylor Smith — professionally Dreamweaver — was born in Dallas, Texas, grew
up between Portland, Oregon and just south of Atlanta, Georgia. She earned a
BA in Communications and Fine Art with a minor in Graphic Design from Georgia
College and State University in 2017. She took private painting lessons
throughout elementary and middle school and did a tattoo apprenticeship at
16 — eight months, but Georgia age restrictions meant she couldn't actually
tattoo.

After college she started live painting at concerts and festivals in Atlanta
(2017), worked in restaurant service, graphic design, and creative event
production, then relocated to Florida around 2018-2019. She went full-time as
an artist around 2020. She works primarily in spray paint and aerosol,
blending expressive abstraction with realism. Nature is the constant —
wildlife, water, birds, flora and fauna. She also paints on canvas, airbrush,
skateboard decks, surfboards, shoes, and backpacks. She is left-handed.

She has painted an estimated 120 to 150 murals, with 15 to 20 in St. Pete.
Festival credits include SHINE 2022 (mural at 1510 1st Ave N, Yacht Power
Products building), SHINE Origins 2025 (Vernis Bowling building at 696 1st
Ave N), Art Oasis Mural Festival 2024 (Clearwater Municipal Parking Garage
and a marine-themed sea turtle mural at Coachman Park Dockmaster's Office),
Wynwood Mural Festival, Paint Memphis 2019, and Art Basel. She has done live
painting at Electric Daisy Carnival (Las Vegas), Okeechobee Music Festival,
and Electric Forest Festival.

Other projects include "The Garden Within" at 219-231 Broadway in downtown
Kissimmee for Osceola Arts' ARTisNOW Public Murals Project (2025). She was
Artist in Residence at Parc at Wesley Chapel, Tampa (2023) and is a resident
artist at AER Tampa. She is a contributor to Few and Far, an international
women's graffiti crew, and collaborates with fellow artist "Slim Gee" under
KindMinds Art (@kindmindsart). She teaches spray painting and mural techniques
to youth communities and is listed on Creative Pinellas as "an avid supporter
of improving our communities through public art and collaboration."

Profiled in Voyage MIA Magazine (January 2020) and The Artisan Magazine.
Contact: Dreamweavin.murals@gmail.com. Website: weavindreams.com.
Instagram: @dream.weavin.

muralDescription: |
A massive wall-sized explosion of color — a hybrid floral-phoenix form rendered in neon pinks, fiery oranges, deep purples, cerulean blues, and electric teals against a dark blue background. Feathered petals radiate outward from the center while swirling organic tendrils and tropical leaves frame the edges. Signed "Dreamweaver" in stylized script at the lower left. A SHINE festival plaque is visible at the base.

muralInspiration: ""

## Egypt Hagan (2022)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Arizona
**Address:** 299 Dr MLK Jr St N
**Building:** George F. Young
**Instagram:** @graffitiegypt

### Bio
She uses portraiture as storytelling — bold, vibrant colors carrying cultural
imagery and narratives that go untold elsewhere.

Egypt Hagan is originally from Los Angeles, now based in Tampa. Her first
solo exhibition was in New York in 2014. She has done international
commissions and community work, including "The Silent Symphony" — a mural
commissioned by Burlington City Arts and Vermont's Office of Racial Equity
for Juneteenth 2021. For SHINE 2022, she painted at 299 Dr. MLK Jr. Street
North.

muralDescription: |
Two faces in profile fill the multi-story wall of the George F. Young
building, surrounded by roses and soft washes of warm color. The
portraits are rendered with expressive, painterly brushwork — detailed
enough to read as specific individuals but loose enough to feel like
fine art rather than photorealism. Roses and floral elements weave
around and between the two figures.

muralInspiration: ""

## Imagine (2022)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Kathmandu, Nepal
**Address:** 240 Dr MLK Jr St N
**Building:** RetroTiques
**Instagram:** @imagine876

### Bio
She has a master's from Harvard. She founded Nepal's first Children's Art
Museum. She paints mindful mantras in Devanagari script with spray paint.

Sneha Shrestha — Imagine — was born in Nepal and is based in Boston, where
she works as Arts Program Manager at Harvard's Mittal South Asia Institute.
Her style fuses Nepali, Tibetan, and Japanese Buddhist mandalas with graffiti
aesthetics and American abstract expressionism. She uses a disciplined
breathing technique while painting.

She is the first contemporary Nepali artist in the collection of the Museum
of Fine Arts Boston. Her work is also in Harvard Art Museums, the Davis
Museum, and the Worcester Art Museum. She has painted at MIT, created a
monumental sculpture for the Isabella Stewart Gardner Museum, and won the
James and Audrey Foster Prize from the ICA Boston (2025). Other clients
include Google, Facebook, and Fidelity. Nepal's Cultural Council named her
one of the country's 100 most influential women.

For SHINE 2022, she painted at RetroTiques on Dr. MLK Jr. Street North.

muralDescription: |
A long parking-lot wall painted solid hot pink, covered with repeating columns of gold Sanskrit-inspired calligraphy. The metallic gold letterforms cascade downward in ornate clusters, with subtler red and pink swirls layered underneath. The effect is meditative and hypnotic — the same sacred text echoing across the entire wall like a visual chant.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A long parking-lot wall painted solid hot pink, covered with repeating columns of gold Sanskrit-inspired calligraphy. The metallic gold letterforms cascade downward in ornate clusters, with subtler red and pink swirls layered underneath. The effect is meditative and hypnotic — the same sacred text echoing across the entire wall like a visual chant.

muralInspiration: ""

## James Bullough (2022)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Berlin, Germany
**Address:** 2201 Central Ave.
**Building:** Central Neurology
**Instagram:** @james_bullough

### Bio
He was a middle-school teacher in Baltimore. He moved to Berlin in 2010 and
started painting buildings.

James Bullough is self-taught in realistic oil painting, trained by studying
the Old Masters of the Netherlands. His style is photorealistic portraiture
combined with graphic fragmentation — figures in mid-motion, dissected and
shattered, in oil, latex, acrylic, and spray paint. He works from his Berlin
studio.

He painted an eleven-story mural on Landsberger Allee in Berlin with
collaborator Addison Karl, facade murals for the URBAN NATION Museum, and
walls at Wynwood Walls, POW! WOW! Hawaii, and Lollapalooza. He is
represented by Thinkspace Projects in LA and Stolenspace Gallery in London.
He co-created VantagePoint Radio, an interview show on urban art. For SHINE
2022, he painted in St. Petersburg.

muralDescription: |
A multi-story mural on the side of a building depicting a woman in a crimson dress, her body fragmented into sweeping ribbon-like shards that scatter across a dark black background. The photorealistic figure — head tilted back, arms outstretched — appears to be dissolving or reassembling mid-leap. Deep navy blue, coral red, and muted lavender geometric strips weave through the composition, creating a sense of explosive movement against the void.

muralInspiration: ""

## Jeff Williams (2022)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 511 Dr. MLK Jr St. N.
**Building:** Myoblast Strength
**Instagram:** @sunsigns.stpete

### Bio
He was a leather engraver before he was a sign painter. He taught himself
lettering in 2012. The 1930s showcard aesthetic stuck.

Jeff Williams is a St. Petersburg native who founded Sun Signs & Lettering in
2015. His style draws from 1930s-40s showcard aesthetics, mid-century design,
1950s advertising, and American Traditional tattoo flash — clean, legible
letterforms with intentional negative space. He painted signage at Sunken
Gardens, working with the site's archivist, and a mini truck for Disney
Springs.

He has been featured on WEDU Arts Plus (PBS) and Fox 13. For SHINE 2022, he
painted at Myoblast Strength on Dr. MLK Jr. Street North.

muralDescription: |
A tall wall painted as a stack of vintage Florida tourism signs in
bold primary colors. From top to bottom: "Florida SOUVENIRS" with palm
trees, "ST. PETERSBURG" in large white block letters on black,
"the sun... the sea... you & me — THE SUNSHINE CITY" on red with
pin-up illustrations and oranges, "FLORIDA" in classic postcard
lettering, "Tampa Bay" in red script with a pelican mascot, and
"Sunshine 360 Days A Year" with a retro couple in green. Every detail
is hand-painted in authentic mid-century sign style.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description

bold primary colors. From top to bottom: "Florida SOUVENIRS" with palm
trees, "ST. PETERSBURG" in large white block letters on black,
"the sun... the sea... you & me — THE SUNSHINE CITY" on red with
pin-up illustrations and oranges, "FLORIDA" in classic postcard
lettering, "Tampa Bay" in red script with a pelican mascot, and
"Sunshine 360 Days A Year" with a retro couple in green. Every detail
is hand-painted in authentic mid-century sign style.

## MadC (2022)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Germany
**Address:** 1010 Central Ave.
**Instagram:** @mad_c1

### Bio
In 2010 she painted a 700-square-metre wall along the Berlin-Halle train
line. It is likely the largest graffiti mural ever painted by a single
person. She has broken her own record twice since.

Claudia Walde — MadC — was born in 1980 in Bautzen, Germany and raised in
Ethiopia. She studied at Burg Giebichenstein University of Art and Design,
Central Saint Martins in London, and earned a master's in graphic design. Her
style evolved from street lettering to transparent layered compositions in
spray paint — including her own "Spectra" transparent paint.

She has painted a control tower at Punta Cana airport, a permanent
installation at the Hessisches Landesmuseum Darmstadt, and glass works with
Maki and Associates architects. Her work appeared on a Deutsche Post stamp
(2023). She has published four books. Clients include the Dulwich Picture
Gallery in London, Mural Arts Philadelphia, the City of Abu Dhabi, and
Wynwood Walls. For SHINE 2022, she painted in St. Petersburg.

muralDescription: |
A multi-story abstract mural on the side of an apartment building. Sweeping translucent ribbons of hot pink, cyan, orange, and white crisscross at sharp angles over a vivid yellow background. Where the color bands overlap they create new hues — purple where pink meets blue, warm peach where orange meets white. The effect is like looking through stacked sheets of colored glass. White paint splatter near the top right adds a calligraphic flourish.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A multi-story abstract mural on the side of an apartment building. Sweeping translucent ribbons of hot pink, cyan, orange, and white crisscross at sharp angles over a vivid yellow background. Where the color bands overlap they create new hues — purple where pink meets blue, warm peach where orange meets white. The effect is like looking through stacked sheets of colored glass. White paint splatter near the top right adds a calligraphic flourish.

## Marina Capdevila (2022)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Barcelona, Spain
**Address:** 601 1st Ave. N.
**Building:** The Sage
**Instagram:** @marinacapdevila

### Bio
Her grandmother is her muse. She paints the vitality of old age — beauty
outside the usual aesthetic canons.

Marina Capdevila holds a degree in art from the University of Barcelona and a
postgraduate in artistic illustration from Eina School. She spent time at the
Willem de Kooning Academy in Amsterdam. Her style uses powerful color with
soft shading inspired by Renaissance sfumato technique, rendered through
exaggeration and irony — elderly people rendered as vibrant, vital, alive.

She has painted in fifteen-plus countries. Festival credits include The
Crystal Ship in Belgium, Vision Art Festival in Switzerland, and Wynwood
Embassy in Miami. She won the Junceda Public Space Award (Spain, 2022) and
the Pinzell Cuixart artistic career recognition (2019). For SHINE 2022, she
painted in St. Petersburg.

muralDescription: |
A two-story wall dominated by a crowd of surreal orange-skinned characters in various states of mischief. On the left, a figure in a beret and sunglasses clutches a boombox. In the center, an enormous face wearing oversized reflective shades holds a lobster. On the right, a figure in a bucket hat dangles a fish on a line while a pale ghost-like creature peeks out between them. The palette is almost entirely warm oranges, creams, and golds against a yellow-gold upper band with tiny palm tree silhouettes. Signed "Marina Capdevila" at the upper left.

muralInspiration: ""

## Reginald O'Neal (2022)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Miami, FL
**Address:** 701 3rd St. S.
**Building:** USF Piano Man Building
**Instagram:** @reginaldo.neal

### Bio
He is self-taught. His mentor taught him to study Joaquin Sorolla. Artsy
named him Vanguard 2021.

Reginald O'Neal paints in oil on linen — figurative work depicting
musicians, family, and community members from Miami's Overtown neighborhood.
He began classical training in 2012 under Alejandro Dorda, who also
introduced him to his other mentor, Spanish-born graffiti artist Axel Void.
His work evolved from broad political subjects to intimate portrayals of the
people closest to him.

He has shown at the Rubell Museum ("American Vignettes," 2024), ICA Miami,
The Bass, and Perez Art Museum Miami. His solo "Souled Out" opened at
Vielmetter Los Angeles in 2024. He was nominated for the 2023 Florida Prize
in Contemporary Art and holds residencies at Oolite Arts and Anderson Ranch.
His SHINE 2022 mural depicted historian John Henrik Clarke overlaid on the
Mundari tribe of South Sudan.

muralDescription: |
A sweeping black-and-white mural spanning the full wall. A suited man
— historian John Henrik Clarke — sits as the central figure, painted
with photorealistic precision. Behind him, silhouetted scenes show
Mundari tribespeople tending long-horned cattle and casting fishing
nets. The monochromatic palette and classical brushwork give the piece
the weight of a museum painting at building scale.

muralInspiration: ""

## Sydney Prusso — Go Outside, Look Inside (2022)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 533 28th St. S.
**Building:** Seriously Strong Training
**Instagram:** @sydneyprusso

### Bio
She graduated from MICA and has painted fifty-plus murals across six states,
installations up to 75 feet.

Sydney Prusso is an illustrator, lettering artist, and muralist based in the
Tampa Bay area. Her style is contemporary botanical — flat illustration
rooted in graphic design, warm 1960s and 70s color palettes, flowing poster
lettering. Nature, the outdoors, and mental health run through the work. She
has done a Reef Organization project in the Florida Keys.

Clients include Salesforce, Adobe, Dickies, PepsiCo, John Deere, Stihl, the
NFL, and AstraZeneca. For SHINE 2022, she painted two murals in St.
Petersburg.

Petersburg.

muralDescription: |
The entire side of a peaked-roof building wrapped in retro psychedelic lettering spelling "Outside" and "Look Inside" in chunky, rounded '70s-style typography. The letters are layered in pinks, oranges, chartreuse, white, and maroon against a purple background. Colorful butterflies — a large pink-and-red one at center, smaller ones floating above — are scattered between the words. Sparkle stars dot the upper peak. The overall vibe is a vintage concert poster blown up to building scale.

muralInspiration: ""

## Tasko (2022)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Tampa Bay, FL
**Address:** 1960 5th Ave S.
**Building:** Kozuba & Sons
**Instagram:** @the_one_of_many_

### Bio
A friend handed him a spray can while skating. He has been writing for
fifteen years.

Jake Jacquillard — Tasko — is a Tampa Bay graffiti writer and wildstyle
specialist. Self-taught, no formal education. His work is "loud, colorful and
in your face" — flashy lettering in spray paint with fifteen-plus years of
practice. He wants people to see graffiti as art, not vandalism.

He organized "Letter Headz," a writer event in Pinellas. For SHINE 2018, he
was selected as an Open Call artist and painted "St Pete" in 3D graffiti
letters. He returned for SHINE 2022 with "The Collective."

## The Happy Mural Project — Wild Ride (2022)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 3001 Central Ave.
**Building:** St. Petersbark
**Instagram:** @alyssa.marie.of.the.sea

### Bio
Their motto is "Honk if You're Happy." Their first mural was painted the
week the world shut down.

Alyssa Marie and Braden Everly are The Happy Mural Project — a married
couple who relocated from Colorado to St. Petersburg in 2017. Alyssa is a
fine artist who opened a gallery on Beach Drive in 2018 (closed January
2020). Braden has a degree in special education and worked as a house
painter in Colorado. Their first mural went up at Lolita's Catering in
early March 2020, days before lockdown.

Their signature is oversized sunflower murals in vibrant color, plus
community-participatory "color by number" walls. Florida wildlife —
manatees, gators, birds — runs through a lot of the work. They were
commissioned by the Florida Wildlife Corridor Foundation for a mural
celebrating the state's ecological connectivity efforts.

They are three-time SHINE artists. For SHINE 2022 they painted "Wild Ride"
at St. Petersbark on Central Avenue — a full-wall American alligator in
rich browns against a field of golden sunflowers and teal sky.

searchBio: |
Their motto is "Honk if You're Happy." Their first mural was painted the
week the world shut down.

Alyssa Marie and Braden Everly are The Happy Mural Project — a married
couple who relocated from Colorado to St. Petersburg in 2017. Alyssa is a

2020). Braden has a degree in special education and worked as a house

Their signature is oversized sunflower murals in vibrant color, plus
community-participatory "color by number" walls. Florida wildlife —
manatees, gators, birds — runs through a lot of the work. They were
commissioned by the Florida Wildlife Corridor Foundation for a mural
celebrating the state's ecological connectivity efforts.

They are three-time SHINE artists (2020, 2022, 2023). For SHINE 2022 they
painted "Wild Ride" at St. Petersbark on Central Avenue — a full-wall
American alligator in rich browns against a field of golden sunflowers and
teal sky. For SHINE 2020 they painted "Hands of Change" at the St.
Petersburg City Theatre — hands holding sunflowers representing racial and
cultural diversity. For SHINE 2023 they painted "Girl Power," a
color-by-number mural completed by Girl Scouts of West Central Florida.

muralDescription: |
A full-wall painting of an American alligator in profile, stretching from
snout to tail across the entire building. The gator is rendered in rich
browns, tans, and dark chocolate with detailed scale texturing throughout.
Behind it, oversized golden sunflowers on green stems fill the background
against a teal sky. The composition makes the gator feel massive — its
head alone takes up about a third of the wall. The color contrast between
the warm gator tones and the cool teal background gives it a Florida
postcard quality.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A full-wall painting of an American alligator in profile, stretching from
snout to tail across the entire building. The gator is rendered in rich
browns, tans, and dark chocolate with detailed scale texturing throughout.
Behind it, oversized golden sunflowers on green stems fill the background
against a teal sky. The composition makes the gator feel massive — its
head alone takes up about a third of the wall. The color contrast between
the warm gator tones and the cool teal background gives it a Florida
postcard quality.

## Van Der Luc — Tank School (2022)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Bogota, Colombia
**Address:** 602 24th St S
**Building:** Allums Auto
**Instagram:** @van_der_luc_

### Bio
Polar bears hauling robots through sunny landscapes. Drones delivering art
supplies. Bird skeletons wandering past boxes of honey. This is how he
talks about the environment.

Lucas Franco — Van Der Luc — is a self-taught painter and multimedia artist
from Bogota, Colombia, now based in St. Petersburg. He works out of Studios
at 5663 in Pinellas Park's Pinellas Arts Village. His subjects are big —
climate, nature, geopolitics, technology — but his delivery is whimsical
and surrealist, playful rather than preachy.

He has shown in group exhibitions and museum-curated shows across the
Tampa Bay region. In 2019 he was selected as an emerging artist for
SHINE's REVEAL: EMERGE live painting event at Station House. By 2022 he
was a full SHINE mural artist.

For SHINE 2022 he painted "Tank School" on the side of Allums Auto in the
Warehouse Arts District — a white polar bear striding across a green
landscape with a robot on its back, an umbrella studded with stars, and a
drone delivering art supplies in the corner.

searchBio: |
Polar bears hauling robots through sunny landscapes. Drones delivering art
supplies. Bird skeletons wandering past boxes of honey. This is how he
talks about the environment.

Lucas Franco — Van Der Luc — is a self-taught painter and multimedia artist

He has shown in group exhibitions and museum-curated shows across the
Tampa Bay region. In 2019 he was selected as an emerging artist for

For SHINE 2022 he painted "Tank School" on the side of Allums Auto in the

muralDescription: |
A large whimsical mural on a bright blue sky background covering the full side of Allums Auto. A massive white polar bear strides across a green grassy landscape, carrying a robot passenger who holds a black umbrella studded with stars. The robot sits atop the bear with a first-aid kit satchel, a potted plant, and a box labeled "Honey" strapped to the bear's back. A skeletal bird — just bones and a long beak — perches behind the robot. A swarm of bees buzzes on the right side, and in the upper left, a drone delivers a cardboard box labeled "Art Supplies." Power lines and distant utility poles dot the horizon. Signed "VAN DER LUC" in angular hand-lettered type in the upper right corner. The piece blends environmental and technological themes with a storybook charm.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A large whimsical mural on a bright blue sky background covering the full side of Allums Auto. A massive white polar bear strides across a green grassy landscape, carrying a robot passenger who holds a black umbrella studded with stars. The robot sits atop the bear with a first-aid kit satchel, a potted plant, and a box labeled "Honey" strapped to the bear's back. A skeletal bird — just bones and a long beak — perches behind the robot. A swarm of bees buzzes on the right side, and in the upper left, a drone delivers a cardboard box labeled "Art Supplies." Power lines and distant utility poles dot the horizon. Signed "VAN DER LUC" in angular hand-lettered type in the upper right corner. The piece blends environmental and technological themes with a storybook charm.

## Vitale Bros (2022)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 694 Arlington Ave N
**Building:** Parc
**Instagram:** @vitalebros

### Bio
Their first mural came from a sketch on a paper napkin, handed to a
restaurant owner. That was 1992.

Johnny Vitale and his brother Paul started Vitale Bros in Tampa Bay
hand-lettering signs and painting storefront windows. Their parents are
artists. So were their grandparents. No formal training — just family
tradition and thirty-plus years of practice. Their late aunt Nancy, who had
Down syndrome and attended Parc Center for Disabilities, turned out to be an
artist too, discovered in her final decade.

In the late 1990s they opened a warehouse studio that hosted group art,
music, and fashion shows that helped birth St. Pete's emerging art scene.
They collaborated on set design for Harmony Korine's Spring Breakers. They
have painted for the Tampa Bay Rays, the Buccaneers, L'Oreal, and dozens of
local businesses. They call themselves the "mural police" — restoring damaged
walls around the city for free.

In 2023, Johnny launched Overspray, a street art shop and graffiti school now
based in Gulfport, teaching tagging, block letters, Wildstyle, and advanced
mural technique to classes of up to twenty students. After Hurricane Milton,
they painted free signs for businesses that lost theirs.

They have painted SHINE every year since its inception — "The Cool Kids" in
2015, "Bait and Switch" in 2019, and multiple walls since. For SHINE Origins
2025, Johnny painted two solo murals: one on the 600 block where it all
began, and another at The Factory on Fairfield Ave South with his daughter
Sionna.

searchBio: |
Their first mural came from a sketch on a paper napkin, handed to a
restaurant owner. That was 1992.

Johnny Vitale and his brother Paul founded Vitale Bros in 1992 in Tampa Bay,
starting with hand-lettered signs and painted storefront windows. Youngest
brother Joey later joined. Their parents are artists — the family artistic
tradition goes back to their grandparents. No formal art education has been
documented; the craft appears entirely self-taught and family-transmitted.
Their late aunt Nancy had Down syndrome and attended Parc Center for
Disabilities, where staff discovered her artistic talent in her final decade
of life.

In the late 1990s and early 2000s, they opened a warehouse studio that hosted
legendary group art, music and fashion shows credited with helping birth St.
Pete's emerging art scene. They collaborated in set design for Harmony
Korine's Spring Breakers (filmed in the St. Pete area). Their style blends
pop art, graffiti, and traditional mural techniques — fine art meets pop art
sensibility, incorporating local culture, historical elements, and visual
stories reflecting community identity.

Clients include the Tampa Bay Rays, Tampa Bay Buccaneers, L'Oreal /
SalonCentric (national locations), Anchor Skate Supply, and Great Bay
Distributors, plus numerous local businesses and private residences. Notable
murals include the Marilyn Monroe on the side of the historic Playhouse
Theater on Central Ave, a Virgin Mary at St. Pete Catholic High School,
Power to the Patients giant fist at the Morean Arts Center, and the Nueva
Cantina restaurant mural in Tampa. They describe themselves as the mural
police — restoring damaged murals around the city for free.

The current Vitale Bros team includes Johnny, Paul, Nick and Jason Kekllas,
Bruce Statton, and Jake Gerow. They have also collaborated under the name
Up and Over with Tes One, and painted behind the State Theatre with Tes One
and Palehorse.

In July 2023, Johnny launched Overspray — a retail street art shop and
graffiti school, initially on the Pinellas Trail, relocated to 5013 20th Ave
S. in Gulfport. Overspray teaches graffiti, tagging, block and bubble
letters, Wildstyle, and advanced mural classes to groups of up to 20
students. The five-artist teaching team includes John Vitale, Nicole Salgar,
Hinson Thehealer, Digital (LA), and Jujmo. After Hurricane Milton, they
painted free signs for businesses that lost theirs. They host events like
Hot Walls during SHINE.

SHINE participation: The Cool Kids (2015, 2063 Central Ave — faces of the
artists' children), Bait and Switch (2019 — red snapper and sea turtle),
SHINE 2022, SHINE 2024 (mural at Anchor Skate Supply with pro skateboarder
Kris Markovich, inspired by Rays City Connect uniforms; limited-edition
skateboard L'Orange). For SHINE Origins 2025, Johnny painted two solo
walls: 600 1st Ave N (the 600 block where SHINE began) and The Factory at
2606 Fairfield Ave S (collaboration with Sionna Sio Macleish, celebrating
St. Pete history and landmarks).

Studio: 5013 20th Ave S., Gulfport, FL. Website: vitalebrothers.com.
Instagram: @johnvitale (solo), @vitalebros (collective).

muralDescription: |
A row of SpongeBob SquarePants characters fills the wall in the Vitale
Brothers' bold, graphic mural style. The familiar cartoon figures are
painted at building scale with clean outlines and flat, saturated colors
that pop against the building exterior. It's become one of the more
popular photo-op walls in the area.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A row of SpongeBob SquarePants characters fills the wall in the Vitale
Brothers' bold, graphic mural style. The familiar cartoon figures are
painted at building scale with clean outlines and flat, saturated colors
that pop against the building exterior. It's become one of the more
popular photo-op walls in the area.

## Aurailieus Artist (2021)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 600 27th St. S.
**Building:** Zen Glass Studio
**Instagram:** @aurailieus_artist

### Bio
His robot character is named Bob. He paints him everywhere.

Mark Stevens — Aurailieus Artist — blends fine art with urban street art,
inspired by graffiti and Jean-Michel Basquiat. He works in mixed media —
paintings, spray cans, canvas prints, wood cutouts. He was an Open Call
winner and painted for SHINE in 2021.

### Mural Description
A vibrant mixed-style piece on a teal cinder block wall. On the left, a
female portrait with flowers in her hair and golden earrings gazes outward,
rendered in a clean illustrative style. To her right, a burst of graffiti
lettering in pinks, reds, purples, and blacks fills the center of the wall,
with angular black wing-like shapes exploding outward. Scattered throughout
are colorful elements — green and pink splashes, circular dots, and what
appears to be a small yellow-green character (Bob the Robot). The piece has
a collaborative jam feel, blending portraiture with wildstyle graffiti.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Bakpak Durden (2021)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Detroit, MI
**Address:** 919 1st Ave. N.
**Building:** The Bends
**Instagram:** @bakpakdurden

### Bio
His first solo museum exhibition sold out at Cranbrook Art Museum. He is a
Black trans postgender artist painting in the tradition of Dutch Baroque.

Bakpak Durden is a self-taught artist from Detroit. His style is
hyperrealistic and conceptual — Afro-Surrealist work exploring human emotion,
identity, consciousness, and spiritual existence, inspired by Baroque
painting and cinematic language. He works in oil, acrylic, graphite, and fine
art photography.

His "Eye of Horus" at Cranbrook was the inaugural Fresh Paint exhibition
series. He has shown at the Muskegon Museum of Art, Arab American National
Museum, Heron Arts in San Francisco, and Paradigm Gallery in Philadelphia.
He won the 2021 Gilda Award and a Modern Ancient Brown Foundation Grant. He
is represented by B&A Reps. For SHINE 2021, he painted a vanitas-style mural
in St. Petersburg.

### Mural Description
A dark, moody mural stretches across the long wall of The Bends. On
a near-black background, a skull and exposed jawbone with visible
teeth emerge from tangled, organic forms in deep greens and grays.
The text "TELL ME HOW YOU FEEL" appears in the upper right corner.
The hyperrealistic rendering and somber palette give it the gravity
of a classical vanitas painting at massive scale.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Case Maclaim (2021)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Frankfurt am Main, Germany
**Address:** 2641 Central Ave.
**Building:** 2641 Central Ave Condos
**Instagram:** @case_maclaim

### Bio
He earned a diploma in art restoration. Then he picked up a spray can and
started painting hands the size of buildings.

Andreas von Chrzanowski — Case Maclaim — was born in 1979 in Frankfurt am
Main and raised in East Germany. He studied art restoration and conservation
at Erfurt University of Applied Sciences. He started spraying in 1995 and
co-founded Ma'Claim, an East German crew that pioneered photorealistic
aerosol art.

His signature is overlapping, intertwined hands symbolizing unity and power —
freehand spray can work combining photorealism with surrealism. He has
painted in twenty-plus countries: Eastern Market in Detroit, Historic Third
Ward in Milwaukee, Bushwick in Brooklyn, Wynwood in Miami, and URBAN NATION
in Berlin. For SHINE 2021, he painted in St. Petersburg.

### Mural Description
A massive photorealistic portrait of a healthcare worker in teal scrubs and
a surgical cap, one gloved hand reaching directly toward the viewer. To the
right, another pair of hands gently holds a small teddy bear sitting in a
red toy car — a warm, personal touch against the clinical setting. The
dark background makes the figures pop off the wall, and the skin and fabric
detail is rendered at a level that reads as a photograph from across the
parking lot.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Emily Ding (2021)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Houston, TX
**Address:** 100 7th St. S.
**Building:** Rob Graham Enterprise
**Instagram:** @_emilyding

### Bio
She draws inspiration from flora, fauna, and human temperament — wild
creatures telling stories about emotions we recognize but cannot name.

Emily Ding is based in Los Angeles and represented by Thinkspace Projects.
Her expressive animals and figures are rendered in bold color gradients with a
fluid, painterly style — acrylics on canvas, wood panels, and cradled wood.
She has painted murals in Shanghai, Salt Lake City, Sacramento, Washington
DC, Oakland, Austin, Flint, Harrisburg, and Houston.

For SHINE 2021, she painted in St. Petersburg.

### Mural Description
A young fawn sleeps curled in a gentle pose, its spotted fur rendered
with painterly realism. White flowers bloom from the deer's back and
around its body, growing up toward a window that's incorporated into
the design. The deep emerald-green background gives the whole wall
the feeling of a forest clearing. Emily Ding's signature is visible
in the lower right.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Gleo (2021)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Colombia
**Address:** 2336 5th Ave. S.
**Building:** New Dawn Coffee Co
**Instagram:** @gleo_co

### Bio
She painted a 50,000-square-foot grain elevator in Wichita, Kansas. It broke
the record for the largest acrylic mural by a single artist.

Nathalia Gallego Sanchez — Gleo — was born in 1992 in Cali, Colombia. She
studied chemistry by day and fine arts at a night institute before earning
her bachelor's in visual arts and graphic design from Universidad del Valle.
She found formal study too theoretical and went her own way. She uses
acrylics, brushes, and rollers — no spray cans. "I let myself be guided by
the moment."

Her murals feature mystical beings, pre-Columbian figures, women with
patterned face-paint and floral-decorated hair, and signature bright yellow
orbs. She draws from Colombian folklore and ancestral cosmology. She has
painted across France, Portugal, Morocco, Brazil, Mexico, Belgium, the
Netherlands, and the US. Her name is wordplay — derived from her surname
GALLEGO.

For SHINE 2021, she painted in St. Petersburg.

### Mural Description
A wide panoramic mural across the full building wall showing multiple
overlapping faces and hands rendered in soft pinks and mauves. The figures
intertwine with ornate mandala-like patterns in warm golds and oranges,
with sunflowers and botanical details woven between the portraits. The
color palette is dreamy — mostly blush pink, sage green, and amber against
a pale background. The faces gaze in different directions, creating a
layered, contemplative composition.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Greg Mike (2021)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Atlanta, GA
**Address:** 915 1st Ave. N.
**Building:** Goldman Wetzel
**Instagram:** @gregmike

### Bio
Justin Bieber collects him. So does Diplo. His "MAD CANS" NFT series
generated three million dollars.

Greg Mike — Gregory Michael Mensching — started graffiti at thirteen in
Atlanta and became a muralist in his late twenties. His style is Surrealist
Pop Art — vivid explosions of color, gleefully twisted characters inspired by
mid-century American cartoons and skate culture. His signature character
Larry Loudmouf is everywhere.

He founded ABV Gallery and Agency in Atlanta in 2008 and launched the
OuterSpace Project in 2015, producing over a hundred murals across the city.
Clients include Nike, Coca-Cola, Google, Spotify, BMW, Adidas, Converse, the
Atlanta Braves and Falcons, Ford Mustang, and Topgolf. He received the Key
to the City of Worcester, Massachusetts, and was named Best Established
Visual Artist and a Notable Georgian by Georgia Trend.

For SHINE 2021, he painted in St. Petersburg.

### Mural Description
A massive wall covered edge-to-edge with Greg Mike's signature cartoon
characters — bug-eyed, grinning figures in electric pinks, greens, and
yellows. The word "SMILE" runs across the center in bold lettering,
surrounded by the crowd of manic, playful characters that make the
building impossible to walk past without looking.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Jared Wright — Harmony (2021)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Tampa Bay, FL
**Address:** 670 Central Ave.
**Building:** Colony Grill
**Instagram:** @jaredwright

### Bio
His first mural ended up in a Tampa Bay Rays commercial. He designed a
skateboard for Tony Hawk.

Jared Wright is a self-taught painter and illustrator from Connecticut, raised
in the Tampa Bay area. His style is surreal, dreamlike, hallucinatory — fun
worlds with loose narratives, influenced by Dali, classic rock, aliens, and
nature. His first painting commission came around 2007, based on Wes
Anderson's The Life Aquatic.

He has collaborated with Birdhouse Skateboards (Tony Hawk), Asphalt Yacht
Club, and Pyramid Skateboards. For SHINE 2021, he painted "Harmony" at 670
Central Avenue — his first-ever mural.

### Mural Description
A surreal scene fills the Colony Grill wall with smooth, rounded
organic forms in muted corals, sage, and teal. An owl with large
dark eyes perches near a crescent moon while a snake-like creature
winds through the composition. Leaf shapes, clouds, and celestial
elements float through the scene, all rendered in soft, flat color
fields with clean outlines.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Jason Harvin (2021)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Tampa, FL
**Address:** 1211 1st Ave. N.
**Building:** Rick Noll Properties
**Instagram:** @waywardwalls

### Bio
He quit Oracle to paint walls. He started painting in 2018. He was featured
on HGTV's 100 Day Dream Home.

Jason Harvin grew up in a military family in Germany, graduated from
Heidelberg American High School, and earned a degree in hospitality
management from the University of Central Florida. He worked at Oracle for
years before leaving corporate in early 2022 to paint full-time.

He founded Wayward Walls in 2019. His style is geometric abstraction with
bold color palettes — painter's tape for crisp designs that transform blank
walls into vibrant landmarks. During the pandemic he created "Zoom Walls" —
custom accent walls for virtual meeting backgrounds. He also runs Wayworld,
an event-driven art space in St. Pete's Warehouse Arts District.

For SHINE 2021 and 2022, he painted in St. Petersburg.

### Mural Description
A wide commercial building wrapped in interlocking geometric shapes — overlapping triangles, concentric circles, and bold diagonal stripes in saturated blue, orange, red, yellow, black, and white. A large red triangle dominates the center, framed by concentric blue-and-orange rings. A bright yellow half-circle anchors the bottom edge. Black-and-white striped bands slice through the composition at sharp angles, while smaller geometric motifs fill every remaining gap. Palm trees along the base add scale to the Op Art explosion.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Jenipher Chandley — Be-you-tiful! (2021)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 625 23rd St S
**Building:** Cimco Tile
**Instagram:** @jenipherchandley

### Bio
She started painting outside Sake Bomb on Central Avenue around 2011. She is
a survivor — of economic hardship, of domestic abuse — and she paints that
survival into everything.

Jenipher Chandley was born and raised in St. Petersburg. She earned degrees
in interior design and graphic design from IADT Tampa, and is a mother of
four who recently became a grandmother. She works predominantly in oils —
representational figurative artwork infused with surrealism and symbolism.
The female form recurs, embodying fragility and strength. Her central theme
is "the story of love" — seeking, experiencing, losing, rediscovering.

For over twelve years she has worked with nonprofits and community
initiatives. She is lead curator at Bar@548 in downtown St. Pete, hosts free
weekly Wednesday night art meetups (no commission taken), and curates monthly
emerging artist shows. She has co-curated and exhibited in hundreds of shows
throughout Tampa Bay. Her studio is at the ArtsXchange, Studio Gallery 104.

She received a 2023 Emerging Artist Grant from Creative Pinellas and has been
a Local Artist Spotlight at the Gasparilla Festival of the Arts and American
Stage Theater. For SHINE 2021 she painted her first festival mural, and for
SHINE Origins 2025 she returned with "Stronger Together" at 31st Street and
7th Avenue South — assisted by her daughter Becka.

muralDescription: |
A vibrant mural on the side of Cimco Tile featuring oversized lemons, halved citrus slices, and white orange blossoms scattered across a deep navy blue background. The lemons glow in warm yellows and oranges, their green leaves curling between the fruit in multiple shades of sage and emerald. A large cross-section of a lemon dominates the center of the wall, painted around a small door. The words "Be-you-tiful!" appear in flowing white script on the left side. Signed "@JenipherChand" in the lower right corner. The bold, graphic botanical style and uplifting message make it a standout on the 23rd Street corridor.

muralInspiration: ""

The lemons glow in warm yellows and oranges, their green leaves curling between the fruit in multiple shades of sage and emerald. Signed "@JenipherChand" in the lower right corner. The bold, graphic botanical style and uplifting message make it a standout on the 23rd Street corridor.

## Leo Gomez — Can't Thank You Enough (2021)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 744 6th Ave. S.
**Building:** Bayfront Health St. Pete
**Instagram:** @leogomezstudio

### Bio
He grew up drawing alongside his grandmother in Bogota. She was a painter.
Hip hop got him into graffiti as a teenager.

Leo Gomez is from Bogota, Colombia, based in St. Petersburg since around
2016. He studied graphic design and trained in hand lettering and typography.
His style is bold, clean typography with dreamy color palettes — all
hand-drawn before going digital. "Handcrafted Lettering with Soul and
Purpose."

Clients include Disney Springs, Starbucks, Publix, the Tampa Bay Lightning,
Green Bench Brewing, and Tombolo Books. He designed the Tampa Bay Lightning's
Hockey Talks Night limited-edition puck for mental health awareness (2024)
and painted signage at the St. Pete/Clearwater International Airport.

For SHINE, he painted in both 2019 ("Sunshine on my Mind") and 2021.

### Mural Description
Elegant gold script lettering spelling "Can't Thank You Enough" fills
the entire wall of a building adjacent to Bayfront Health. The flowing
cursive letterforms are painted on a rich navy blue background, with
decorative swashes and flourishes extending from each word. "Leo Gomez
Studio" is signed in the upper right corner.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Michael Fatutoa (2021)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Tampa Bay, FL
**Address:** 2060 1st Ave. S.
**Building:** Grayspeed
**Instagram:** @samoan_mike

### Bio
He tattoos the traditional Samoan Pe'a — waist to knees, encoding genealogy
and cultural identity. His daughters are learning the craft.

Michael Fatutoa — Samoan Mike — was born on the island of Tutuila in eastern
Samoa. He grew up in Kalihi, Hawaii within the Samoan community. He started
tattooing informally at fifteen for friends, entered the profession formally
at nineteen after moving to Las Vegas, and eventually relocated to Tampa. He
is self-taught. His mural work extends traditional Polynesian geometric
patterns — drawn from carvings and tapestries — to walls.

He tattooed WWE's Roman Reigns. Two of his daughters apprentice under him.
For SHINE 2021, he painted in St. Petersburg.

### Mural Description
Two massive sea creatures face each other on a flat gray wall. On the left, a hammerhead shark rendered in teal and black, its entire body filled with intricate Polynesian tatau patterns — spirals, chevrons, and layered geometric bands that follow the contours of its form. On the right, a sea turtle in black and white, its shell packed with bold angular motifs including step patterns and diamond shapes, with small teal accents at the flippers. The two animals appear to be circling each other, filling the wall from edge to edge.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Miss Crit — Nowness is the Portal (2021)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 3000 Central Ave.
**Building:** Absolutely Beautiful Flowers
**Instagram:** @miss_crit

### Bio
Her illustration was chosen for the global Creative Mornings theme "Rise" —
displayed across 238 cities in 69 countries.

Laura Spencer — Miss Crit — was born and raised in St. Petersburg. She
graduated from the Pinellas County Center for the Arts at Gibbs High School,
then earned a BFA in Illustration from Ringling College of Art and Design
with a semester exchange at Sint Lucas Antwerpen in Belgium. She calls her
style "a little bit spooky and a little bit psychedelic."

She worked as art director at Bealls Department Stores and as an illustrator
at Creative Arts Unlimited before going full-time. She co-created the
Sunshine City Mosaic — a $50,000 public art installation at Sunset Park.
She received the Creative Pinellas Emerging Artist award (2019) and leads
SHINE's 10th anniversary trolley mural tour.

For SHINE 2021, she painted in St. Petersburg.

### Mural Description
The phrase "NOWNESS IS THE PORTAL" stretches across the wall in large cream-
colored serif lettering with a slight Art Nouveau curve to the type. Behind
and around the text, a dense arrangement of tropical foliage fills the
entire surface — deep green monstera leaves, burgundy ti plants, broad
tropical leaves in teal and dark green, and a large white magnolia bloom
opening in the upper right. The background is a moody dark teal that makes
the plants and flower pop. The whole piece wraps around two second-story
windows on the building face.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Mwanel Pierre-Louis (2021)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Miami, FL
**Address:** 1100 1st Ave. N.
**Building:** Green Bench Brewing
**Instagram:** @mwanel1_l

### Bio
Mwanel Pierre-Louis is a Miami-based painter born in New York to
Haitian parents. He studied at New World School of the Arts in Miami
and the Art Center College of Design in Pasadena, and has since built a
client roster that includes Adidas, Jack Daniel's, Foot Locker, Puma,
the Miami Dolphins, and the Florida Panthers. His paintings have shown
at Scope Art Fair during Art Basel and at POW!WOW! Hawaii.

His 2021 SHINE portrait on the side of Green Bench Brewing features a
woman with flowing locs against layered washes of warm color. The piece
shows his signature approach — photorealistic faces dissolving into
loose, expressive abstraction around the edges.

### Mural Description
A striking portrait of a woman with long locs dominates the side of
Green Bench Brewing. The face is rendered with photorealistic detail
while the background dissolves into expressive washes of warm oranges,
reds, and earth tones. The contrast between the precise portrait work
and the loose, painterly surroundings gives the piece real presence
from across the street.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Nicole Salgar — The Commuter (2021)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 615 23rd St S
**Building:** Cimco Tile
**Instagram:** @nmsalgar

### Bio
She spent seven years designing for Ralph Lauren before she started painting
walls.

Nicole Salgar graduated from Design and Architecture Senior High in Miami
and earned her degree from the Fashion Institute of Technology in New York,
specializing in tailoring. She left fashion and started painting. Her first
mural was a 2013 collaboration with Chuck Berrett on Manhattan's Lower East
Side.

Her style blends Latin folk art influences with horror, sci-fi, and cinema
aesthetics — vivid colors, high contrast, constantly evolving. She was
selected for the Dali Museum's "Outside In" exhibition (2025). Her largest
wall is a 2.5-story mural in Wynwood with the Pangeaseed foundation on
rising sea levels. She has painted in Israel, Mexico, Spain, Cuba, Puerto
Rico, and across the US. Clients include Starbucks, Absolut, Lululemon,
Steve Madden, and Bacardi.

For SHINE 2021, she painted in St. Petersburg.

### Mural Description
A large-scale mural covering the entire side wall of Cimco Tile in a striking limited palette of muted reds, teals, greens, and pinks. The left side features a photorealistic portrait of a young woman gazing directly at the viewer with an intense, contemplative expression. Her face is rendered in soft green and warm tones, with horizontal red striped shadows — like light through blinds — falling across her forehead and the wall behind her. Lush tropical leaves in deep reds and teals frame her face and extend across the right side of the wall, interwoven with chain-link fence imagery and bold geometric red shapes. A large flower blooms on the far right, wrapping around the building's roll-up door. Signed "ns" on the left side. The piece explores themes of parallel dimensions and transformation, with the subject serving as a gatekeeper between worlds.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Reid Jenkins (2021)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 800 31st St. S.
**Building:** St. Pete Distillery
**Instagram:** @reidmjenkins

### Bio
He paints the people seldom seen in the 'Burg. He also tattoos them.

Reid Jenkins is a St. Petersburg native. He graduated from the Pinellas
County Center for the Arts at Gibbs High School — the same program that
produced Derek Donnelly — then earned a BFA from the Maryland Institute
College of Art. He tattoos at Fabled Trade Tattoo Company, a queer-owned
studio in the Lealman area, and keeps a studio at the ArtsXchange in the
Warehouse Arts District.

His murals tend toward photorealistic portraiture and botanicals — his SHINE
2021 piece at the St. Pete Distillery featured portraits of his two daughters
surrounded by sunflowers and greenery. He has noted that roughly a third of
mural pay goes to supplies alone. He collaborated with Iboms and Zulu Painter
on "Imagine Gas Plant & Laurel Park" at Campbell Park Recreational Center,
and painted the cheetah with geometric 90s-inspired colors at Star Booty
Salon on 16th Street South.

For SHINE Origins 2025, he painted a botanical piece at Founders Pro using a
projection method for the outline.

searchBio: |
He paints the people seldom seen in the 'Burg. He also tattoos them.

Reid Jenkins is a St. Petersburg native. He graduated from the Pinellas
County Center for the Arts (PCCA) program at Gibbs High School, then earned
a BFA from the Maryland Institute College of Art (MICA). He is a muralist,
painter, illustrator, and tattoo artist. He tattoos at Fabled Trade Tattoo
Company (formerly Ink Werkz Tattoo Studio), a queer-owned studio at 4195
54th Ave N in the Lealman area of St. Petersburg. He keeps a studio at the
ArtsXchange in the Warehouse Arts District.

His aim is to "paint the human experience and the experience of those seldom
seen in the 'Burg," focusing on capturing small, overlooked moments. His work
tends toward photorealistic portraiture and botanicals. He has noted that
roughly 33% of mural pay goes to supplies alone. He supports MIZE Gallery,
COVE in Pinellas Park, Woodfield Fine Art, and d-gallerie.

For SHINE 2021, he painted at the St. Pete Distillery — portraits of his two
daughters with sunflowers and greenery. He collaborated with Iboms and Zulu
Painter on "Imagine Gas Plant & Laurel Park" at Campbell Park Recreational
Center. Other murals include the large cheetah with geometric 90s-inspired
colors at Star Booty Salon (1741 16th St S). He exhibited in "Renew," a
group show with Bekky Beukes at Mergeculture, Tampa (February 2022).

For SHINE Origins 2025, he painted a botanical piece at Founders Pro, using
a projection method for the outline. Instagram: @reidmjenkins.

muralDescription: |
Two large-scale portraits of young women — the artist's daughters — surrounded by lush greenery, golden sunflowers, and cascading white jasmine vines against a deep purple and teal background. A yellow butterfly floats between the figures. Painted across the facade of St. Petersburg Distillery.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
Two large-scale portraits of young women — the artist's daughters — surrounded by lush greenery, golden sunflowers, and cascading white jasmine vines against a deep purple and teal background. A yellow butterfly floats between the figures. Painted across the facade of St. Petersburg Distillery.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Ricky Watts (2021)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** San Francisco, CA
**Address:** 2622 Fairfield Ave. S.
**Building:** The Factory St. Pete
**Instagram:** @rickywatts

### Bio
Google made him their Artist in Residence. He painted the lobby of Building
42 in Mountain View.

Ricky Watts was born in 1980 in San Francisco. He studied graphic design at
the Art Institute of California in San Diego for two years, then left to
pursue fine art. In 2011 he developed his signature "hard-edge gradients" —
abstract, fluid shapes with psychedelic color gradients that evolved from
letter-based canvas work.

He has painted fifty-plus large-scale murals since 2004 and mounted over a
hundred group exhibitions and twenty solo shows, including at Sotheby's
Oakland and Modern Eden. Clients include Google, Louis Vuitton, YouTube,
Mercedes Benz, IBM, and Outside Lands. He has painted for POW! WOW!, the Las
Vegas Circus Center, and Sonic Temple Festival.

For SHINE 2021, he painted in St. Petersburg.

### Mural Description
A massive abstract mural wrapping around The Factory St. Pete building.
Enormous organic, fluid shapes in bold saturated colors — orange, teal,
yellow, deep blue, green, and coral — flow and overlap across the facade
like giant brushstrokes frozen mid-motion. The forms are rounded and bulbous,
somewhere between liquid ribbons and inflated tubes, creating a sense of
constant movement. Palm trees in front of the building interact with the
composition. The "Ricky Watts" signature and address "2622 Fairfield Ave S"
are visible on the right side. The scale is impressive — the flowing shapes
span two stories.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Woes Martin (2021)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Los Angeles, CA
**Address:** 2343 Emerson Ave. S.
**Building:** New Dawn Coffee Co 2
**Instagram:** @woes

### Bio
His signature character is a stressed-out panda. It represents struggle and
perseverance. There is also a hipster Chewbacca.

Aaron "Angry Woebots" Martin — Woes — was born and raised on Oahu, Hawaii.
Self-taught through graffiti, hip hop, and comics. He paints acrylic on wood
and canvas in minimal color palettes with detailed character design, plus
aerosol murals, resin sculptures, and vinyl toys.

Clients include Kidrobot, Disney, Converse Red, Samsung, New Era, and Fitted
Hawaii. He has shown at Urban Nation in Berlin, Comic-Con, Designer Con, and
Singapore Toy Con. Festival credits include POW! WOW! Hawaii, Murals in the
Market in Detroit, and the Heineken Mural Project.

For SHINE 2021, he painted in St. Petersburg.

### Mural Description
A long warehouse wall covered in a pack of oversized panda bears piled on top of each other against a black background. The pandas are rendered in gritty black and white with exaggerated features — bloodshot pink eyes, bared fangs, and massive claws. Some snarl at the viewer while others sleep with one eye cracked open. Bright green bamboo leaves poke through the tangle of fur and limbs. The scale is enormous — each panda is taller than a parked truck.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Ya La'Ford — Love Shines (2021)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Tampa, FL
**Address:** 330 Beach Dr NE
**Building:** Smith & Associates Real Estate
**Instagram:** @yalaford

### Bio
She has an MFA and a law degree. She represented the United States at the
Dakar Biennale in Senegal.

Ya La'Ford is a first-generation American of Jamaican heritage, from the
Bronx. She earned her MFA from the Art Institute of Boston (now Lesley
University) and her Juris Doctor from the University of Florida. She
maintains studios in St. Petersburg, the Bronx, Jamaica, and Maine.

Her work spans painting, sculpture, sound, print, video, and immersive
installations using light, water, sound, and motion. Sharp geometric lines,
bold contrasting colors, vibrant circles and triangles. She sits on the Board
of Trustees of the Museum of Fine Arts in St. Petersburg. She designed an
Arrow McLaren SP IndyCar and was a featured artist for Super Bowl 2021.

Clients include the Tampa Bay Rays, Toronto Blue Jays, and Nike. Her work is
in the collections of Tropicana Field, Al Lang Stadium, the Morean Arts
Center, and the Boston Public Library. She has shown at The James Museum, The
Ringling Museum of Art, and Florida CraftArt.

For SHINE 2021, she painted in St. Petersburg.

### Mural Description
A bold graphic mural on a charcoal-gray wall featuring a large heart filled with Ya La'Ford's signature intricate geometric line pattern — dense, angular, interlocking shapes in bright orange-red. The words "Love Shines" are written across the heart in flowing white neon-style script, with a small illuminated element at the top. The heart is centered on the wall with clean negative space around it, creating a striking contrast between the dark background and the vibrant pattern. Signed "Ya La'ford @yalaford" in white at the bottom. An orange Smith & Associates plaque with the mural's backstory is mounted to the lower right. The piece was part of "The Love Line Project," one of three SHINE 2021 Bright Spots community projects — the phrase "Love Shines" was voted on by the public.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Brian Butler (2020)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Miami, Florida
**Address:** 1101 1st Ave N
**Building:** MUV Dispensary
**Instagram:** @upperhandart

### Bio
He drew over two thousand live concert performances from the audience. He
calls the series "Show Drawn."

Brian Butler is based in Miami. He studied at the Massachusetts College of
Art and Design. His style is playful, illustrative, bold color — driven by
music. He adapts to darker, grittier work for serious subjects. He founded
The Miami Palmist independent music publication and toured colleges with
Microsoft and Cut & Paste.

Festival credits include SXSW, Bonnaroo, Governors Ball, Riot Fest, Ultra,
and the Motorhead Motorboat. Clients include Vice, Target, Tumblr, Red Bull,
Converse, the Miami Heat, Pabst Blue Ribbon, Vans, Live Nation, and Liquid
Death. Music clients: Wu Tang Clan and The Mighty Mighty Bosstones.

For SHINE 2020 / Sea Walls, he painted "Carbon Punishment" in the Edge
District — an enviro-METAL multimedia project combining a mural with
grindcore music tracks.

### Mural Description
A large-scale nature scene wraps the MUV Dispensary building, featuring
dense tropical foliage, birds in flight, and flowing organic linework.
The composition covers multiple faces of the building, creating an
immersive green canopy effect. Bold outlines define the plant forms
while softer color fills give the whole piece a hand-illustrated,
almost storybook quality.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Happy Mural Project (2020)

**Category:** shine
**Address:** 4025 31st Street S
**Building:** The St. Pete City Theatre
**Instagram:** @thehappymuralproject

### Bio
Their motto is "Honk if You're Happy." Their first mural was painted the
week the world shut down.

Alyssa Marie and Braden Everly are The Happy Mural Project — a married
couple who relocated from Colorado to St. Petersburg in 2017. Alyssa is a
fine artist who opened a gallery on Beach Drive in 2018 (closed January
2020). Braden has a degree in special education and worked as a house painter
in Colorado. Their first mural went up at Lolita's Catering in early March
2020, days before lockdown.

Their signature is oversized sunflower murals in vibrant color, plus
community-participatory "color by number" walls. Their SHINE 2020 piece
"Hands of Change" at the City Theatre featured hands holding sunflowers
representing racial and cultural diversity. For SHINE 2023, "Girl Power" was
a color-by-number mural completed by Girl Scouts of West Central Florida.
They are three-time SHINE artists — 2020, 2022, and 2023.

## IBOMS — Where the Bloom Begins (2020)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 2324 Central Ave
**Building:** Grand Central Brewhouse
**Instagram:** @i.b.o.m.s

### Bio
His solo show "Ego Death" at Mize Gallery sold out in 48 hours. IBOMS stands
for "illegal. Beauty. On. My. Streets."

Jabari Jahi Babatu Reed-Diop — IBOMS — is a St. Petersburg native. He
attended Douglas Anderson School of the Arts in Jacksonville and the Gibbs
High PCCA Magnet Program. His signature character "Ashi" — a young Black
male — was drawn from his childhood in south St. Pete. He uses the "iceberg
theory": celebratory surface, deeper narratives underneath.

He works in 2D illustration, 3D sculpture, and digital design. His recent
work features mushroom-headed characters exploring "Ego Death." He has shown
at d-Gallerie and Mize Gallery, designed a Tampa Bay Rays Artist Print
Series (2019), and co-created a PSTA bus wrap with St. Pete Youth Farm.

For SHINE 2020, he painted "Where the Bloom Begins" at Grand Central
Brewhouse. He returned as a SHINE 2021 Bright Spots artist.

### Mural Description
A large cartoon-style character dominates the bright yellow wall — a
tattooed figure with blue and gray braided hair, gritted teeth, and an
intense sideways glare. The word "BLOOMS" is tattooed across its chest.
The character is surrounded by bulbous, cloud-like brown shapes that pile
up around the base, with a wavy white line suggesting water and small fish
swimming along the bottom edge. The IBOMS tag sits in the upper right
corner. Stars and swirl details float above the character's head.

muralInspiration: "Created in partnership with the Pangeaseed Foundation's Sea Walls program to address Florida's red tide and harmful algal bloom crisis."

---

## Kenny Coil and Marc Berenguer (2020)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, Florida
**Address:** 1000 3rd St S
**Building:** USFSP Harbor Hall
**Instagram:** @kennycoil

### Bio
Marc designed for brands in Barcelona before crossing the Atlantic. Kenny is
a USF graduate. They opened Break Maiden together in 2018.

Kenny Coil and Marc Berenguer run Break Maiden, a branding and design studio
at 33 6th Street South in St. Petersburg. Both are USF graduates. Their work
spans brand identity, packaging, illustration, and murals. Marc moved from
Barcelona to the US in 2009, bringing a love of typography and minimal design.

Clients include Green Bench Brewery (Webb City Cellar branding) and MADE
Coffee. For SHINE 2020 / Sea Walls, they painted a mangrove and coastal
wetlands mural on Harbor Hall at USF St. Pete, with embedded information
viewable via the Pixelstix app.

### Mural Description
A full-building mural in a flat, graphic illustration style depicting
Florida nature and neighborhood life. The composition is divided into
loose grid-like sections filled with stylized herons, egrets, pelicans,
tropical fish, palm trees, mangroves, and colorful downtown buildings in
teal, pink, yellow, orange, and sage green. The shapes are rounded and
geometric with a mid-century modern feel — puffy trees with scalloped
edges, simplified houses with bright facades, and birds rendered as
elegant silhouettes. A large white egret dominates the lower right. The
overall palette is warm and coastal, giving the building a cheerful,
welcoming presence.

---

## Lili Yuan (2020)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Jacksonville, FL
**Address:** Grand Central District
**Building:** ARTE
**Instagram:** @oliooolio

### Bio
She left Hangzhou, China at thirteen for education. Each of her paintings
takes over a hundred hours.

Lili Yuan is based in Jacksonville, Florida. She studied economics in China,
then took art classes at Florida State College at Jacksonville after
immigrating. She is largely self-taught. Her style is hyperrealism in oil —
rich tones and strong physical presence, exploring the dichotomy of Eastern
and Western cultures. Her "water series" is a recurring body of work.

For SHINE 2020 / Sea Walls, she painted "Gift" at 1661 Central Avenue — a
woman in a red dress floating in blue water with the Chinese idiom meaning
"Be like water."

### Mural Description
A large circular composition on a cream-colored wall depicting a woman
with long dark hair floating in turquoise water, seen from above. She
wears a flowing red dress with deep pleats that billow and spread around
her like a blooming flower. The water is rendered with photorealistic
light caustics and ripples. Her hands gently push against the fabric as
her hair trails out beyond the circle's edge. To the left, bold Chinese
calligraphy reads "shang shan ruo shui" (the highest good is like water)
— a passage from the Tao Te Ching. A small red seal stamp appears at the
bottom left corner. The @oliooolio tag is visible near the circle's edge.

---

## Nneka Jones and Bianca Burrows (2020)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Tampa, Florida
**Address:** 1957 1st Ave S
**Building:** Goodyear Rubber Products
**Instagram:** @artyouhungry

### Bio
TIME Magazine used her embroidered American flag on its cover. She was
twenty.

Nneka Jones was born in Trinidad and Tobago, where she placed first in the
Caribbean for Art and Design Examinations before moving to the United States
in 2016. She earned her BFA with a minor in marketing from the University of
Tampa in 2020. A professor challenged her to "paint without paint" — she
turned to embroidery, a skill learned from her mother in Trinidad.

Her work addresses systemic injustice, sexual abuse, and human trafficking
through realistic embroidered portraits and mixed media. Her "Targets" series
incorporates condoms. TIME commissioned her embroidered flag for its August
2020 cover on "The New American Revolution." She keynoted Adobe MAX that
October. She won the Gasparilla Emerging Artist Award (2019) and Best of Show
with a $15,000 prize at Gasparilla (2022). For SHINE 2022, she painted in
St. Petersburg.

searchBio: |
TIME Magazine used her embroidered American flag on its cover. She was
twenty.

Nneka Jones was born in Trinidad and Tobago, where she placed first in the
Caribbean for Art and Design Examinations before moving to the United States
in 2016. She earned her BFA with a minor in marketing from the University of
Tampa in 2020. A professor challenged her to "paint without paint" — she
turned to embroidery, a skill learned from her mother in Trinidad.

Her work addresses systemic injustice, sexual abuse, and human trafficking
through realistic embroidered portraits and mixed media. Her "Targets" series
incorporates condoms. TIME commissioned her embroidered flag for its August
2020 cover on "The New American Revolution." She keynoted Adobe MAX that
October. She won the Gasparilla Emerging Artist Award (2019) and Best of Show
with a $15,000 prize at Gasparilla (2022). For SHINE 2022, she painted in
St. Petersburg.

## Tatiana Suarez — Mangroves and Manatees (2020)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Miami, FL
**Address:** 250 2nd Ave SE
**Building:** St. Petersburg Sailing Center
**Instagram:** @tatunga

### Bio
Her mother is Brazilian. Her father is Salvadoran. The tropical palette of
greens and yellows comes from both.

Tatiana Suarez earned her BFA in Illustration and Design from the University
of Miami in 2005. Her style is pop surrealism — ethereal feminine creatures
with doe-eyes surrounded by flora and fauna. She has painted at Wynwood
Walls, in Okinawa, Hawaii, Detroit, Mexico, and Sweden.

Clients include Netflix, L'Oreal, Reebok, and JanSport. She is represented
by Spoke Art in San Francisco and Corey Helford Gallery in Los Angeles. For
SHINE 2020, she painted in St. Petersburg.

searchBio: |
Her mother is Brazilian. Her father is Salvadoran. The tropical palette of
greens and yellows comes from both.

Tatiana Suarez earned her BFA in Illustration and Design from the University
of Miami in 2005. Her style is pop surrealism — ethereal feminine creatures
with doe-eyes surrounded by flora and fauna. She has painted at Wynwood

SHINE 2020, she painted in St. Petersburg.

muralDescription: |
A building-wrap mural covering the entire facade of the St. Petersburg Sailing Center in muted greens, teals, and warm browns. A young woman with flowing auburn hair swims among Florida manatees, her hand resting gently on a large manatee in the foreground. Schools of striped sheepshead fish swim to the right, while teal mangrove leaves and sea plants fill the left side and edges. Bubbles drift upward throughout the composition. The palette is naturalistic and subdued — deep greens, sage, and sandy tones — giving the whole piece an underwater, dreamlike quality. Signed "Tati 2020 @tatunga" in the lower right corner. Painted for SHINE x Sea Walls 2020, sponsored by Ocean Conservancy, the mural highlights the fragile mangrove and manatee ecosystems of Florida's coast.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A building-wrap mural covering the entire facade of the St. Petersburg Sailing Center in muted greens, teals, and warm browns. A young woman with flowing auburn hair swims among Florida manatees, her hand resting gently on a large manatee in the foreground. Schools of striped sheepshead fish swim to the right, while teal mangrove leaves and sea plants fill the left side and edges. Bubbles drift upward throughout the composition. The palette is naturalistic and subdued — deep greens, sage, and sandy tones — giving the whole piece an underwater, dreamlike quality. Signed "Tati 2020 @tatunga" in the lower right corner. Painted for SHINE x Sea Walls 2020, sponsored by Ocean Conservancy, the mural highlights the fragile mangrove and manatee ecosystems of Florida's coast.
