## Melanie Posner

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** Los Angeles, CA
**Address:** 421 4th Ave N
**Building:** Hollander Hotel
**Instagram:** @therealmelpoz

### Bio
She painted the woman in the red headscarf on the back of the Hollander
Hotel in under five days, then went straight to Walt Disney World to paint
scenic art for Star Wars: Galaxy's Edge.

Melanie Posner was born around 1993 in a small town outside Philadelphia.
She moved to Tampa in 2013 and earned her BFA in Painting from the
University of Tampa in 2016. Her work is photorealistic portraits of
women — oversized, expressive eyes set against rainbow palettes,
psychedelic paisley backgrounds, and florals. "Painting females and
exploring how complex they are kind of helped me figure out myself, and
who I was."

She was a cast member on Amazon Prime's docuseries Tampa Baes (2021), a
show about a Tampa Bay lesbian friend group. Amazon commissioned her to
paint a mural on a bus that toured the city promoting the show. Her
painting "Passion" appears on screen.

In 2024 she painted a forty-foot mural on the LGBT Center of Los Angeles
for TikTok's "Create the Future" campaign. She is now based in Los Angeles.

Her SHINE 2018 mural "New Beginnings" is a twenty-three by sixty-five foot
photorealistic woman lying on her side, one eye peeking through her
fingers, on 7th Terrace South.

searchBio: |
She painted the woman in the red headscarf on the back of the Hollander
Hotel in under five days, then went straight to Walt Disney World to paint
scenic art for Star Wars: Galaxy's Edge.

Melanie "Mel" Posner was born around 1993 in a small town just outside
Philadelphia. She moved to Tampa in 2013 and earned her BFA in Painting
from the University of Tampa in 2016, where she also studied art therapy.
She was previously based in Gulfport, Florida, and is now in Los Angeles.

Her style is photorealistic portraits of women — oversized, expressive
eyes, vibrant rainbow color palettes, psychedelic paisley backgrounds,
floral elements. She blends lifelike detail with touches of dreamy
surrealism. She works in oil on canvas, collage, prints, and large-scale
murals. She sources her subjects from Pinterest, Google, and social media,
looking for eyes, story, and confidence. "Working large is just really
exciting," she says. "I feel like I'm a little bit more free doing murals."
Her stated philosophy: paint strong, confident, independent women to be
inspired by them.

For SHINE 2018, she was one of five local "Open Call" artists. Her mural
"New Beginnings" at 4139 7th Terrace South is twenty-three feet high by
sixty-five feet long — a photorealistic woman lying horizontally on her
side, her hand cupping the right side of her face, one eye peeking through
her fingers. She completed it in October 2018.

Her commercial clients include Walt Disney World (scenic artwork for Star
Wars: Galaxy's Edge at Hollywood Studios, Orlando), New Belgium Brewing
(the eighteen-foot Hollander Hotel mural of a woman in a red headscarf
with a glass of Mural Agua Fresca Cerveza, completed in under five days),
Amazon Prime Video (a mural on a promotional bus for Tampa Baes), and
TikTok. For TikTok's 2024 "Create the Future" campaign, she painted a
forty-foot mural on the LGBT Center of Los Angeles at 8745 Santa Monica
Blvd in West Hollywood.

She was a cast member on all eight episodes of Tampa Baes (Amazon Prime
Video, 2021), a docuseries about a Tampa Bay lesbian friend group produced
by 3 Ball Productions and filmed over roughly ten weeks starting April
2021. She described herself as "neutral" and avoiding drama on the show.
"My anxiety during filming was up to the roof, my mental health totally
depleted. It was very stressful and I will say, I love how the show came
out." Her oil painting "Passion" (also called "Summer and Marissa")
appears on screen. She worked for years as a bartender at The Honey Pot,
a landmark LGBTQ+ club in Ybor City, Tampa.

She has exhibited at Enigma gallery in downtown St. Petersburg.
Instagram: @therealmelpoz. Website: melanieposner.com.

muralDescription: |
An 18-foot-tall commercial mural on the back entrance of the Hollander Hotel
near the swimming pool and tiki bar. A confident woman wearing a red head
scarf and dark sunglasses holds a tall glass of beer, rendered in
photorealistic style. The background features a bold psychedelic paisley
pattern in mint green, yellow, and pink with black and white teardrop motifs.
Signed "Melanie Posner" in the upper right, with "@muralcerveza" and
"@therealmelpoz" at the bottom. Commissioned by New Belgium Brewing to
promote their Mural Agua Fresca Cerveza summer beer.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
An 18-foot-tall commercial mural on the back entrance of the Hollander Hotel
near the swimming pool and tiki bar. A confident woman wearing a red head

## Stephen Palladino (2017)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Los Angeles / New York
**Address:** 276 Dr. Martin Luther King Jr. Blvd N
**Building:** Benson Worley Architecture
**Instagram:** @goodandshiddy

### Bio
He started painting large-scale murals across New York City as a teenager.
Tiffany and Lady Gaga are clients.

Stephen Palladino was born in 1986 in the Bronx. He attended a magnet art
program at age ten for classical technique, but his real education was on the
streets. His style merges street culture, fine art, and raw emotional
storytelling — cartoon and graffiti-rooted characters that are "slick and
gritty, dark and lowbrow" with hidden layers of popular imagery.

Clients include Tiffany & Co., Playboy, Lady Gaga, Reebok, American Express,
and Capital One. He created "The Late Night Drawing Show" on social media and
is a regular at Art Basel. He is based between Los Angeles and New York.

For SHINE 2017, he painted in St. Petersburg.

### Mural Description
A 17-by-95-foot mural on the south wall of Benson Worley Architecture
featuring a dense composition of distorted human figures and faces in bright
fluorescent colors — aqua, orange, lime green, purple, blue, and rosy red.
From a distance the forms read as a cheery abstract puzzle, but up close bold
black line drawings reveal Palladino's expressive, German Expressionism-
influenced style. Characters include an orange woman, a blue-suited man, and
a red woman in profile, interspersed with phrases like "LOVE ME," "AIN'T,"
"NOBODY but YOU," "LOOKIN NEAT," "GAS," "YO!," and "NOT TRUE" scrawled in
handwritten text across the wall.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Chad Mize — Twiggy

**Category:** shine-legacy
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 653 Central Ave (alley)
**Building:** Behind Bluelucy Gallery

### Bio
He created the "Paris/Tokyo/London/St. Pete" shirt in 2010. It is still
everywhere.

Chad Mize was born in 1974 in New Orleans, grew up in Tampa Bay, and has
been in St. Petersburg for over seventeen years. He earned a BFA in Visual
Communications from the University of Mississippi. His early career included
drawing sidewalk portraits at Faneuil Hall in Boston and illustrating Flash
animations for a digital textbook company. His style is bold, cartoon-like
imagery commenting on politics, religion, and contemporary culture —
humorous, tongue-in-cheek, influenced by Keith Haring's belief that art
should be accessible.

He has painted over 120 murals. He co-founded BLUELUCY gallery on Central
Avenue in 2010 (representing 450-plus artists before closing in 2015), then
opened MIZE Gallery on MLK Jr. Street (2018-2022), then SPACE Gallery, and
now curates pop-ups at Green Bench Brewery and runs "Side Spot," a
mini-gallery next to his husband Dan's restaurant Uptown Eats. He served on
SHINE's organizing committee for six-plus years.

He is a featured artist at the James Museum (2025) and was included in
"Outside In: New Murals Inspired by Dali" at the Dali Museum (2025). They
call him "St. Pete's Godfather of Art." He has earned it.

muralDescription: |
A stencilled black-and-white mural of 1960s fashion icon Twiggy's face in
the alley behind the 600 block of Central Avenue. The image references the
iconic 1966 photograph by Barry Lategan, showing Twiggy's signature doe eyes
with stars, rendered with an iridescent sheen against a faded light blue wall.
The deliberately muted, pastel-hued palette contrasts with the bolder murals
in the surrounding area.

muralInspiration: ""

## Shark Toof (2015)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Los Angeles, CA
**Address:** 687 Central Ave
**Building:** State Theater
**Instagram:** @shark__toof

### Bio
Samsung put his shark in a commercial with LeBron James. Apple put it in an
iPad ad.

David Lew — Shark Toof — studied at the Art Center College of Design in
Pasadena. His iconic image is a hand-drawn wheat-pasted great white shark
that has appeared on walls from Wynwood to Cambodia. His other series
include "Guilty Pleasures" (cigarette packs and slot machines) and "Mayday"
(pulp-comic romance).

Clients include Apple, Samsung, Ritz Carlton, Heineken, Burton, Toyota,
Adidas, and Axl Rose (a personal tour jacket). He has shown at Corey Helford
Gallery, Thinkspace, Inner State Gallery in Detroit, and GO Gallery in
Amsterdam. His work is in the permanent collection of the Chinese American
Museum in Los Angeles. He painted the Heavy Metal Magazine cover (Issue 288).

For SHINE 2015, he painted in St. Petersburg.

### Mural Description
A massive great white shark painted in dark gray and black across the east
wall of the State Theater, a pink/coral building with arched windows on
Central Avenue. The shark's head dives downward with its mouth wide open,
baring rows of white teeth, positioned above a red metal-grate door. The
piece was completed over four days using approximately 100 cans of aerosol
paint on the 55-by-33-foot wall. The shark's menacing form contrasts with
the ornate architectural details of the historic theater building.

muralInspiration: ""

## Sara Sheppard — Life Reimagined (2017)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 719 Central Ave
**Building:** Morean Arts Center
**Instagram:** @sarah.g.sheppard

### Bio
Her father was her mother's college art teacher. Both parents are artists.

Sarah Sheppard grew up in Dallas, studied photography, drawing, painting,
graphic design, ceramics, and fabric art at the University of North Texas,
and has lived in St. Petersburg for over twelve years. She also studied
metalsmithing at the Creative Jewelry Academy, continuing work her late
father began.

Her style is colorful, high-contrast figurative work — new romanticism,
movement as metaphor for continuous loss and seeking. She started her mural
career painting for a spa in St. Petersburg after earlier work in Venetian
plaster and faux finishing for model homes. She has multiple SHINE murals
across the city, including "Life Reimagined" on the back wall of the Morean
Arts Center, commissioned by AARP.

For SHINE 2023, she returned to paint in St. Petersburg.

muralDescription: |
A mural on the back wall of the Morean Arts Center framed with a navy blue
border and ornate gold trim, giving it the appearance of a steampunk painting.
At center, a woman merges with a banyan tree — her body becoming the trunk,
her roots spreading into the ground. She reaches out to a red phoenix bird
at left, symbolizing rebirth. The right side features massive interlocking
bronze and gold gears representing the passage of time and the mechanics of
working life. Teal water fills the base. Commissioned by AARP as part of the
"Age Friendly St. Pete" SHINE All Year program in 2017.

muralInspiration: ""

## Alex Pardee — Brightmares (2016)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Los Angeles, CA
**Address:** 251 10th St N
**Instagram:** @alexpardee

### Bio
He designed creatures for Zack Snyder's Sucker Punch. He made the album art
for The Used. He calls his monsters "Brightmares."

Alex Pardee was born in 1976 in Antioch, California. He is self-taught — no
formal art education. His style is dark, fantastical worlds filled with
ghoulish creatures in unexpectedly vibrant, saturated colors, inspired by
Garbage Pail Kids and Tales From the Crypt.

He was art director, executive producer, and creature designer for Digging Up
The Marrow (2014, directed by Adam Green). He has designed album art for The
Used, In Flames, Aesop Rock, and Cage. Clients include Hurley International,
KidRobot, and Upper Playground. He shows at Spoke Art, Corey Helford
Gallery, and Gallery 1988.

For SHINE 2016, he painted in St. Petersburg.

### Mural Description
A 46-by-19-foot mural depicting an emaciated, skeletal gray figure hunched
beneath an enormous dripping concentric-circle orb in pink, blue, green,
yellow, and orange — like a deflating rainbow balloon where the figure's head
should be. The creature has elongated bony limbs and stands on bulbous
white and tan blobs that spurt upward from the ground. A swirling black
vortex fills the background. Colors drip downward from the orb as if
melting in the Florida heat. This was the largest mural Pardee had ever
attempted at the time, completed with assistance from fellow artist Ricky
Watts.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Morning Breath (2019)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Brooklyn, NY
**Address:** 250 Dr. Martin Luther King Jr. St N
**Building:** Coney Island Sandwich Shop
**Instagram:** @morningbreathinc

### Bio
They met at Think Skateboards in San Francisco in 1996. They have designed
over a hundred album covers since.

Doug Cunningham and Jason Noto are Morning Breath Inc., a Brooklyn-based
design studio they formalized in 2002. Cunningham studied at the Academy of
Art University in San Francisco. Their style pulls from 1950s-1980s
Americana — comics, Mad magazine, punk rock, hip-hop, heavy metal, and
skateboard graphics — rendered in limited color palettes with bold lines.

The client list is absurd: Foo Fighters, Kanye West, Jay-Z, Eminem, Slayer,
Queens of the Stone Age, AFI. Brands: Vans, Adidas, Ray-Ban, Absolut Vodka,
MTV, KFC (a full restaurant redesign). They won the Grammy for Best Recording
Package in 2021 for Desert Sessions Vols. 11 and 12 — a Josh Homme project
with a mix-and-match booklet and sticker set. They have published two
monographs: The Early Bird (2007) and By the Skin of Our Teeth
(HarperCollins, 2016).

Their SHINE 2015 mural on Central Avenue is mostly hidden behind new
construction — only a narrow vertical slice survives. They returned for SHINE
2019 with a 101-foot-wide piece at 250 Dr. MLK Jr. Street North in their
signature black-white-and-red style: open lips, aviator sunglasses, a
smiling devil face.

muralDescription: |
A 101-by-15-foot brush-painted mural in black, white, and red on a pale
blue background, designed to look like a vintage comic book pamphlet held
open wide. At left, the Steeplechase "Funny Face" from Coney Island grins
with a missing tooth. The center features a sinister figure in a red jacket
holding a "Welcome to Fear City" pamphlet — a reference to the real 1975
New York City scare pamphlet. Phrases like "GHASTLY," "STARTLING SECRETS of
Mystery," "JITTERY NERVES," and "Picture You NERVOUS" are scattered across
the composition in retro horror-comic typography. At far right, a large red
devil face with curling mustache and horns looms above the wall. The mural
was inspired by the Coney Island Sandwich Shop (operating since 1926) and
contains subtle nods to New York City throughout.

muralInspiration: ""

## J&S Signs — Point of Pines (2018)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Portland, OR (originally St. Petersburg, FL)
**Address:** 553 9th St N
**Instagram:** @jandssigns

### Bio
They met as ceramics majors at the University of Florida. Their clients
include Nike, Adidas, Patagonia, and the Portland Trail Blazers.

Jeffrey Sincich (born 1990, Belleair, FL) and Josh Stover founded J&S
Signs in 2013 in St. Petersburg after graduating from UF in 2012. They
started by painting positive-message murals and transitioned into
professional sign painting — their style draws from 1930s-1950s lettering,
bold and simple with a signature mint-and-red palette. They relocated to
Portland, Oregon around 2015 and rebranded as "Variety Shop" in 2021.

Their St. Pete murals include "You Are My Sunshine" at the Morean Arts
Center and "Love Your Neighborhood" on Central Avenue. For SHINE 2018
they painted "Point of Pines" — a retro children's-book scene of vintage
St. Pete buildings named for the Spanish meaning of Pinellas.

searchBio: |
They met as ceramics majors at the University of Florida. Their clients
include Nike, Adidas, Patagonia, and the Portland Trail Blazers.

Jeffrey Sincich (born 1990, Belleair, FL) and Josh Stover — J&S Signs —
met as ceramics majors at the University of Florida and graduated in 2012.
They began painting positive-message murals in St. Petersburg and
transitioned into professional sign painting, founding J&S Signs in 2013.
Their style draws from 1930s-1950s hand lettering — bold, simple, with
distinctive letter shapes and a signature mint-and-red palette. They
relocated to Portland, Oregon around 2015 and rebranded as "Variety Shop"
in 2021. Clients include Nike, Adidas, Patagonia, Airbnb, and the Portland
Trail Blazers.

St. Pete murals include "You Are My Sunshine" at the Morean Arts Center
and "Love Your Neighborhood" on Central Avenue. For SHINE 2018 they
painted "Point of Pines" at 553 9th St N — a 15-by-60-foot retro mural
in muted pink, mustard, sea green, and orange depicting three vintage St.
Pete buildings. The title references the Spanish meaning of "Pinellas" —
the county's original name. Period details include a green bench, palm
trees, and a mid-century skyline.

Instagram: @jandssigns.

muralDescription: |
A 15-by-60-foot mural in a retro, bold children's-book style using muted
pink, mustard brown, sea green, and orange. The composition depicts three
vintage St. Pete buildings: a green one-story motel at left with a 1950s
neon "Motel" sign and an "All Are Welcome" placard; a two-story mustard
post office at center with archways and a vintage "Oranges $1.99" sign;
and a pink two-story apartment at right with orange and brown columns. The
title "Point of Pines" references the Spanish meaning of "Pinellas" — the
county's original name. Period details include a green bench, palm trees,
and a layered skyline evoking mid-century Florida.

muralInspiration: ""

## Derek Donnelly — 365 Million Things To Do (2018)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 54 7th St N
**Building:** Morean Arts Center (east wall)
**Instagram:** @saintpaintarts

### Bio
He painted a T-Rex in a Hawaiian shirt going fishing — for AARP. That tells
you most of what you need to know about Derek Donnelly.

A Pinellas County native, Donnelly grew up in Pinellas Park, graduated from
the Center for the Arts at Gibbs High School in 2001, and studied graphic
design and digital illustration at IADT Tampa. He went full-time on murals in
2010. A year later he opened Saint Paint Arts gallery on the 600 block of
Central Avenue — not as a vanity project, but as a launchpad. Several artists
got their first real exposure there before he closed it in 2014 to focus on
walls.

His style sits at the intersection of illustration and realism — playful
without being childish, bold without being loud. His son Syre shows up in
murals across the city. So does Florida wildlife. He doesn't separate the
personal from the public work. In 2012, when mentor Bill "Woo" Correira died,
Donnelly organized the Woomorial — a 40-artist tribute mural on the 600 block
that became a landmark. That instinct to build things for other people keeps
showing up. He founded COVE gallery in Pinellas Park, helped establish the
Pinellas Arts Village district, and started Public Art Project Inc., a
nonprofit that pushes public art into places it wouldn't otherwise reach and
raised pandemic relief funds for local artists.

His client list runs from Fender Guitars to the Tampa Bay Rays to Sirata Beach
Resort — the first major St. Pete Beach resort to commission a local artist.
Multiple Best of the Bay wins. Inaugural Artmaker 2026 at Ruth Eckerd Hall.
For SHINE 2025, he wasn't just painting — he ran operations and served as
artist liaison for the entire festival while producing two murals of his own.

searchBio: |
He painted a T-Rex in a Hawaiian shirt going fishing — for AARP. That tells
you most of what you need to know about Derek Donnelly.

A Pinellas County native who grew up in Pinellas Park, Donnelly graduated from
the Pinellas County Center for the Arts at Gibbs High School in 2001 and
studied graphic design and digital illustration at IADT Tampa. He has 20-plus
years of painting experience and went full-time on murals in 2010. Within a
year he opened Saint Paint Arts gallery on the 600 block of Central Avenue in
downtown St. Petersburg — not as a vanity project, but as a launchpad for
other artists. Several got their first real exposure there before he closed it
in 2014 to focus entirely on walls.

In the early 2010s, Donnelly and collaborator Sebastian Coolidge co-founded
the downtown St. Pete mural movement when the area was, by his own account,
"drab and virtually art-free." Together they painted "A Moment to Reflect" on
the Florida Craftsmen building — at the time the largest mural in St.
Petersburg, four stories tall, completed in ten days. It depicts a businessman
removing his shoes to embrace creativity, with a green tie nodding to sponsor
Regions Bank.

In November 2012, mentor Bill "Woo" Correira died from complications of brain
surgery. Correira was known for his fish paintings and his fierce support of
the 600 block arts community. Donnelly organized the Woomorial, a tribute
mural on the side of the Saint Paint gallery where 40-plus artists
contributed — Donnelly painted Woo's portrait while others added marine life.
It became a 600 block landmark.

His style blends illustration and realism — bold character work, signature
gradients, hand-drawn detail, playful without being childish. His son Syre
appears in murals across the city, including the Outshine project at Planet
Retro Records on Dr. MLK Jr. Street N, where he collaborated with Hinson the
Healer, R2 Romero, and Artist Slim. Florida wildlife is a constant. He has
painted portraits of Prince, Kurt Cobain, and Tupac Shakur. His canvases
featuring Syre were exhibited in the "Son-Shine" show at the Wayward Goose.

Donnelly's client list includes AARP, Hilton Hotels, the Tampa Bay Rays, MLB,
Fender Guitars, St. Petersburg College, Sirata Beach Resort (two murals as
part of a $15 million renovation — the first major St. Pete Beach resort to
commission a local artist), Rococo Steak (a ceiling mural), Station House, and
Smith & Associates Real Estate. His work spans Clearwater Beach, Gulfport,
Treasure Island, Madeira Beach, Largo, Dunedin, Tampa, Seminole, North Ft.
Myers, and Anna Maria Island.

That instinct to build things for other people keeps showing up. He founded
COVE gallery (Creative Opportunities & Visual Entertainment) in late 2016 in
the Pinellas Arts Village — a city-funded project using rehabbed shipping
containers at 5705 Park Blvd in Pinellas Park. He helped establish the
Pinellas Arts Village district from the beginning and hosts ArtWalk events
there every fourth Saturday. He started Public Art Project Inc., a nonprofit
that creates large-scale beautification projects, educates the public on the
social and economic impact of the arts, and raised pandemic relief funds for
local artists. He is a member of the Bloom Collective and a participant in the
Carmada art car movement alongside Sebastian Coolidge, James Oleson, Daniel
Barojas, and Zulu Painter.

He helped originate the SHINE Mural Festival and in 2018 curated five "Open
Call" walls for the festival. Multiple Best of the Bay wins from Creative
Loafing Tampa Bay. Selected as the inaugural Artmaker 2026 at Ruth Eckerd
Hall's Marcia P. Hoffman School of the Arts — a year-long exhibition in the
hall's galleries with a featured artwork on the annual holiday ornament. He
appeared on The Catalyst Sessions (May 2020) and RHStv's "Jabari's Beautiful
People: Featuring St. Pete Murals."

For SHINE 2025, executive director Helen French brought him on as Operations
Lead and artist liaison — the person with "the pulse" of the city. It was his
first time as a featured SHINE artist after a decade of painting city walls.
He produced two murals: a vintage postcard piece reading "Greetings from
Hollander Hotel" at 421 4th Ave N, and a panoramic ocean scene with his
signature pelican-riding boy at 1847 1st Ave N.

The dinosaur in the Hawaiian shirt fishing off the waterfront? That's his AARP
"Age Friendly St. Pete" series on the east wall of the Morean Arts Center —
the older generation, he says, enjoying life to the fullest.

muralDescription: |
A whimsical mural on the east wall of the Morean Arts Center depicting
an anthropomorphic T-Rex dinosaur wearing a brown fedora, round glasses,
and a blue-and-white Hawaiian shirt, holding a fishing rod over the
waterfront. A pink-and-blue sunset sky frames a distant city skyline
across the bay. The mural is part of Donnelly's AARP "Age Friendly St.
Pete" series — the T-Rex going fishing represents "the older generation
of St. Pete enjoying life to the fullest." Signed "Donnelly @saintpaintarts."

## Chad Mize

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 421 4th Ave N
**Building:** Hollander Hotel
**Instagram:** @chadmize

### Bio
Chad Mize is a multimedia artist often called "St. Pete's Godfather of Art."
He founded MIZE Gallery (formerly Bluelucy Gallery) and was a member of
SHINE's organizing committee. His bold, colorful illustration style draws
from pop art, comics, and Florida culture. He has multiple murals across
St. Petersburg including the iconic Twiggy portrait behind his former
gallery on Central Avenue.

muralDescription: |
A large three-story commercial mural on the side of the Hollander Hotel
in Chad Mize's signature bold-line illustration style. On a white background,
colorful vignettes in orange, teal, yellow, and pink depict Florida
icons — flamingos, a pink dolphin, an orange slice, a mermaid, a seahorse,
beach chairs, palm trees, and a retro couple in swimwear splashing in
the surf. "St. Pete" appears in playful lettering at top, "HH Hollander
Hotel" in gold at center, and "Florida" in a retro script below. NÜTRL
Vodka Seltzer branding appears in the upper right. Signed "@CHADMIZE."

muralInspiration: ""

## Palehorse — Saraswati Goddess (2020)

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Instagram:** @palehorsedesign

### Bio
He co-founded SHINE with his wife Michelle. He has also designed gig posters
for Foo Fighters, Queens of the Stone Age, Iggy Pop, and Jimi Hendrix.

Chris Parks — Palehorse — earned a BA in Graphic and Interactive
Communication from Ringling College of Art and Design. His style is intricate
digital illustration blending Eastern mysticism, sacred iconography, esoteric
symbolism, and global mythologies. His practice is rooted in Kriya Yoga — he
hosts weekly meditation circles called "Seekers of the Eternal."

Clients include National Geographic, Nike, Mini Cooper, FX, World of
Warcraft, Hasbro, Under Armour, WWE, Harley Davidson, Red Bull, Wired
Magazine, and DC Comics. He was featured in the Dali Museum and co-created
the FloridaRAMA immersive art experience.

muralDescription: |
A massive black-and-gold mural depicting Saraswati, the Hindu goddess
of knowledge and the arts. The multi-armed figure plays a veena
(stringed instrument) at center, surrounded by extraordinary
ornamental detail — a swan on the left, a peacock on the right, an
Om symbol above, and the word "सरस्वती" (Saraswati) in Devanagari
script. The entire composition is rendered in white and gold linework
on a jet-black background, giving it the quality of an illuminated
manuscript at building scale.

muralInspiration: ""

## Elizabeth Barenis — Healing Palms (2025)

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 744 6th Ave. S.
**Building:** Bayfront Health St. Pete

### Bio
In 2018, she was one of ten painters invited to the Mark Rothko Art Center in
Latvia. She grew up in the Mississippi Delta, where her parents owned a music
store.

Elizabeth Barenis studied piano with her mother, took private art lessons
throughout childhood, attended the Mississippi School for Math and Science,
and graduated from Sarah Lawrence College in 2004. A summer at the Chautauqua
Institute for Art that same year sealed it — she committed to painting
full-time.

She calls her approach "Abstract Precisionism" — generalized shapes rendered
with clean, precise lines that recall Cubism. Palm fronds, tropical flora,
seascapes, forests, and foggy scenes dominate. She works from her own
photography, particularly nighttime reference shots where she reconstructs
pattern and form. She also has a background in medical illustration, which
shows in the detail.

She moved to St. Petersburg in 2015 for family reasons and stayed for the
arts community. Since then: a 2017 Creative Pinellas Emerging Artist Grant, a
2023 Professional Artist Grant, solo shows at the Morean Arts Center
("Tropical Splendor") and Art Center Sarasota ("Palmistry"), and a mural on
the Bayfront Hospital ambulance bay — a diptych painting photographed and
enlarged for the louvered exterior wall. Her first public mural was "Super
Palm" in Dunedin in 2022, 300 square feet on a Parks and Recreation building.

For SHINE Origins 2025, she painted alongside Karel Garcia, Amy Ilic-Volpe,
Brain The Genius, and others at 31st Street and 7th Avenue South.

searchBio: |
In 2018, she was one of ten painters from seven countries invited to the
International Painting Symposium at the Mark Rothko Art Center in Daugavpils,
Latvia, marking Rothko's 115th anniversary. She grew up in the Mississippi
Delta, where her parents owned a music store.

Elizabeth Barenis was born and raised in Greenville, Mississippi. She studied
piano with her mother and later learned guitar. She enrolled in private art
lessons throughout childhood. She attended the Mississippi School for Math and
Science in Columbus, Mississippi, then graduated from Sarah Lawrence College
in Bronxville, New York in 2004. A summer at the Chautauqua Institute for Art
that same year sealed it — she committed to painting full-time.

She calls her approach "Abstract Precisionism" — generalized shapes rendered
with clean, precise lines reminiscent of Cubism. Her primary subjects are palm
fronds, tropical flora, seascapes, forests, rainbows, and foggy scenes. She
works from her own photography, particularly nighttime reference photos where
she reconstructs pattern and form. She also has a background in medical
illustration, which informs her sensitivity to detail. She works in acrylic
and oil. Her philosophy: beauty is "an indication of a greater force at work
in our world, one of harmony and well-being."

She relocated to St. Petersburg, Florida in 2015 for family reasons and
stayed for the arts community. Awards include a 2017 Creative Pinellas
Emerging Artist Grant, a 2023 Creative Pinellas Professional Artist Grant, a
2025 Award of Excellence at Art Festival Beth-El, and the Margaret Murphy
Steward Best of Show at a Morean Arts Center members exhibition (which led to
her solo show there). Solo exhibitions include "Palmistry" at Art Center
Sarasota (October-November 2022) and "Tropical Splendor" at the Morean Arts
Center (2024, with gallery talk August 8). Group exhibitions include
"Perspectives" at Studio@620 (with Vincent Mancuso), ARTicles Art Gallery in
the Uptown Arts District, SixStar Art Studios, and Art Festival Beth-El
(2025, 2026).

Public art includes "Super Palm" — a 300 sq ft mural on the Parks and
Recreation building at 1700 Solon Ave in Dunedin, Florida (November 2022, her
first public mural), signal box designs in Clearwater, and an ambulance bay
mural at Bayfront Hospital (now Orlando Health Bayfront) in St. Petersburg —
based on a 48 by 144 inch diptych painting, photographed and enlarged for the
louvered exterior wall. Healthcare clients include Moffitt Cancer Center
(artwork acquisition), Bayfront Health St. Petersburg (mural and painting
commissions), and Premier Community Healthcare in New Port Richey (extensive
series of prints).

For SHINE Origins 2025, she was one of approximately 17 local artists
selected. She painted at 31st Street and 7th Avenue South alongside Karel
Garcia, Amy Ilic-Volpe, Brain The Genius, Jenipher Chandley, Zulu Painter,
and Rebekah Lazaridis. Website: elizabethbarenis.com.
Instagram: @elizabethbarenis.

muralDescription: |
Overlapping angular planes in shades of green and blue fill the
louvered exterior wall of the Bayfront Health ambulance entrance. The
geometric shapes suggest tropical palm fronds abstracted into faceted,
crystalline forms — a diptych painting enlarged to building scale. The
cool palette and precise geometry complement the medical campus
architecture while adding a distinctly tropical warmth.

## Matt Kress

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 1st St N & Dr. MLK Jr St N
**Building:** Animal Hospital
**Instagram:** @mattkressart

### Bio
Air traffic controllers call his airplane "Skittles." He spray-painted
a 1967 Mooney in forty-five colors — it headlined Sun 'n Fun and
Oshkosh and won Best Unique Paint Scheme.

Matt Kress grew up in Minerva, Ohio, studied painting and graphic design
at Kent State, then spent nearly sixteen years in a desk job because he
was afraid art wasn't a real career. In 2014 he visited St. Petersburg on
a business trip, found a town alive with murals, and moved. His friend
Christian McAlhaney of the band Anberlin told him: "I love the art you're
doing, but don't forget you're nothing right now. Get your work out there
and do some murals."

He applied to SHINE 2018 and got his first mural — a pirate ship sponsored
by the Tampa Bay Buccaneers. It paid a thousand dollars for two weeks of
work. After that the phone started ringing. He has since painted over
thirty murals including the seventy-foot sea turtle at Channel Club in
Tampa — the tallest mural in the city at the time.

searchBio: |
Air traffic controllers call his airplane "Skittles." He spray-painted
a 1967 Mooney in forty-five colors — it headlined Sun 'n Fun and
Oshkosh and won Best Unique Paint Scheme.

Matt Kress was born and raised in Minerva, Ohio (1994 Minerva High School
graduate). He studied art and graphic design at Kent State University,
originally enrolling as an architecture student before switching to
painting. Inspired by Klimt, Chuck Close, Dali, Picasso, Warhol, and
Mondrian. He spent nearly sixteen years in business (ten years at Fastenal
in sales, plus process engineering) before moving to St. Petersburg in 2014
and going full-time as an artist at age 43 in 2018.

His friend Christian McAlhaney (lead guitarist of Anberlin) encouraged him
to pursue murals and referenced the SHINE festival. His first mural was
"The Voyage" for SHINE 2018 — a 25-foot pirate ship sponsored by the Tampa
Bay Buccaneers at 128 20th St S. It paid $1,000 for two weeks. He has
since painted 30-plus murals nationwide including the 70-foot sea turtle
"Gaspar's Direction" at Channel Club Apartments in Tampa (tallest mural in
Tampa at the time, 2022), a Roman goddess Minerva mural in his hometown,
and "PiCOWso" — a Picasso-inspired 14-foot fiberglass cow for Minerva
Dairy (possibly the world's largest painted art cow).

"The Mooney Anomaly" is a 1967 Mooney M20F airplane (N3205F) owned by
pilot Liam Hawkins, based at Albert Whitted Airport in downtown St. Pete.
Kress spray-painted it in 40-45 Montana Colors graffiti cans with an
asymmetrical design — no two parts alike. It won Best Unique Paint Scheme
at Sun 'n Fun Aerospace Expo in Lakeland and appeared at EAA AirVenture
Oshkosh (world's largest airshow, 677,000 attendees). Air traffic
controllers nicknamed it "Skittles." Featured in AOPA, Flying Magazine,
General Aviation News, and Axios Tampa Bay.

He exhibits at Red Dot Miami (Art Basel) and Art Santa Fe. Original
paintings range $5,000-$20,000. Revenue is approximately 75% murals, 25%
originals. He cycles 100 miles weekly on the Pinellas Trail and mentors
young artists in Tampa Bay. His motto: "The eye is the lamp of the body."

Instagram: @mattkressart. Website: mattkress.art.

muralDescription: |
A long wall covered with oversized portraits of dogs and cats in a
colorful geometric pop-art style. A Boston terrier, a large dark dog,
and a tabby cat with prominent whiskers are painted in bold, faceted
color blocks — blues, oranges, greens, reds — set against a
kaleidoscopic background of intersecting diamond patterns. The style
is vibrant and eye-catching from the parking lot.

muralInspiration: ""

## Melanie Posner (2023)

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** Los Angeles, CA
**Building:** instagram: therealmelpoz
**Instagram:** @therealmelpoz

### Bio
She painted the woman in the red headscarf on the back of the Hollander
Hotel in under five days, then went straight to Walt Disney World to paint
scenic art for Star Wars: Galaxy's Edge.

Melanie Posner was born around 1993 in a small town outside Philadelphia.
She moved to Tampa in 2013 and earned her BFA in Painting from the
University of Tampa in 2016. Her work is photorealistic portraits of
women — oversized, expressive eyes set against rainbow palettes,
psychedelic paisley backgrounds, and florals. "Painting females and
exploring how complex they are kind of helped me figure out myself, and
who I was."

She was a cast member on Amazon Prime's docuseries Tampa Baes (2021), a
show about a Tampa Bay lesbian friend group. Amazon commissioned her to
paint a mural on a bus that toured the city promoting the show. Her
painting "Passion" appears on screen.

In 2024 she painted a forty-foot mural on the LGBT Center of Los Angeles
for TikTok's "Create the Future" campaign. She is now based in Los Angeles.

Her SHINE 2018 mural "New Beginnings" is a twenty-three by sixty-five foot
photorealistic woman lying on her side, one eye peeking through her
fingers, on 7th Terrace South.

searchBio: |
She painted the woman in the red headscarf on the back of the Hollander
Hotel in under five days, then went straight to Walt Disney World to paint
scenic art for Star Wars: Galaxy's Edge.

Melanie "Mel" Posner was born around 1993 in a small town just outside
Philadelphia. She moved to Tampa in 2013 and earned her BFA in Painting
from the University of Tampa in 2016, where she also studied art therapy.
She was previously based in Gulfport, Florida, and is now in Los Angeles.

Her style is photorealistic portraits of women — oversized, expressive
eyes, vibrant rainbow color palettes, psychedelic paisley backgrounds,
floral elements. She blends lifelike detail with touches of dreamy
surrealism. She works in oil on canvas, collage, prints, and large-scale
murals. She sources her subjects from Pinterest, Google, and social media,
looking for eyes, story, and confidence. "Working large is just really
exciting," she says. "I feel like I'm a little bit more free doing murals."
Her stated philosophy: paint strong, confident, independent women to be
inspired by them.

For SHINE 2018, she was one of five local "Open Call" artists. Her mural
"New Beginnings" at 4139 7th Terrace South is twenty-three feet high by
sixty-five feet long — a photorealistic woman lying horizontally on her
side, her hand cupping the right side of her face, one eye peeking through
her fingers. She completed it in October 2018.

Her commercial clients include Walt Disney World (scenic artwork for Star
Wars: Galaxy's Edge at Hollywood Studios, Orlando), New Belgium Brewing
(the eighteen-foot Hollander Hotel mural of a woman in a red headscarf
with a glass of Mural Agua Fresca Cerveza, completed in under five days),
Amazon Prime Video (a mural on a promotional bus for Tampa Baes), and
TikTok. For TikTok's 2024 "Create the Future" campaign, she painted a
forty-foot mural on the LGBT Center of Los Angeles at 8745 Santa Monica
Blvd in West Hollywood.

She was a cast member on all eight episodes of Tampa Baes (Amazon Prime
Video, 2021), a docuseries about a Tampa Bay lesbian friend group produced
by 3 Ball Productions and filmed over roughly ten weeks starting April
2021. She described herself as "neutral" and avoiding drama on the show.
"My anxiety during filming was up to the roof, my mental health totally
depleted. It was very stressful and I will say, I love how the show came
out." Her oil painting "Passion" (also called "Summer and Marissa")
appears on screen. She worked for years as a bartender at The Honey Pot,
a landmark LGBTQ+ club in Ybor City, Tampa.

She has exhibited at Enigma gallery in downtown St. Petersburg.
Instagram: @therealmelpoz. Website: melanieposner.com.

muralDescription: |
A photorealistic woman's face fills the wall, wearing large reflective
sunglasses that mirror a waterfront skyline scene. Bright pink lips
and warm skin tones contrast with the cool teal background, which is
dotted with oversized polka dots in pink, gold, red, and light blue.
The piece wraps around windows that are incorporated into the design.
Palm trees frame the mural on either side.

muralDescription: |
An intricate Southeast Asian temple composition on a jet-black background.
A central Thai-style pagoda structure with layered flame-shaped spires
rises in the middle, flanked by two elaborate Naga serpent-dragons that
coil outward in ornamental detail. The entire piece is rendered in fine
linework using teal, red, gold, and white — giving it the look of a
traditional temple painting scaled up to cover a freestanding wall. A
thin white circle frames the central temple like a halo, and a trident
symbol sits in the upper right corner. The level of detail is remarkable
at this scale, with every scale, flame tip, and architectural ornament
individually rendered.

muralInspiration: "Inspired by the Sanskrit concept of 'Sat Chit Ananda' — existence, consciousness, bliss. Painted in collaboration with brother Matt Parks."

### Mural Description
An intricate Southeast Asian temple composition on a jet-black background.
A central Thai-style pagoda structure with layered flame-shaped spires
rises in the middle, flanked by two elaborate Naga serpent-dragons that
coil outward in ornamental detail. The entire piece is rendered in fine
linework using teal, red, gold, and white — giving it the look of a
traditional temple painting scaled up to cover a freestanding wall. A
thin white circle frames the central temple like a halo, and a trident

## Sara Sheppard — Stronger Together

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Instagram:** @sarah.g.sheppard

### Bio
Her father was her mother's college art teacher. Both parents are artists.

Sarah Sheppard grew up in Dallas, studied photography, drawing, painting,
graphic design, ceramics, and fabric art at the University of North Texas,
and has lived in St. Petersburg for over twelve years. She also studied
metalsmithing at the Creative Jewelry Academy, continuing work her late
father began.

Her style is colorful, high-contrast figurative work — new romanticism,
movement as metaphor for continuous loss and seeking. She started her mural
career painting for a spa in St. Petersburg after earlier work in Venetian
plaster and faux finishing for model homes. She has multiple SHINE murals
across the city, including "Life Reimagined" on the back wall of the Morean
Arts Center, commissioned by AARP.

For SHINE 2023, she returned to paint in St. Petersburg.

muralDescription: |
A wide panoramic mural showing a row of stylized faces in profile, each
rendered in different bold color blocks — greens, yellows, purples, pinks,
blues, and oranges. The faces overlap and interlock like puzzle pieces,
surrounded by tropical leaves, palm fronds, and simple white flowers. The
words "Stronger Together!" appear in gold script on the right side. The
style is flat and graphic with clean geometric shapes making up each face,
giving the whole piece a modern, poster-art quality. It reads as a
celebration of community and diversity.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description

## DAAS — We Are Going to Be Friends (2018)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Austin, TX
**Address:** 128 20th St S
**Building:** Tri-J Towing
**Instagram:** @daas

### Bio
He apprenticed six years under a muralist in Daytona Beach. Then he moved to
Japan and worked for Universal Studios Osaka.

DAAS — his legal name — grew up in Jacksonville and St. Petersburg. He earned
an associate's in graphic design from Pinellas Technical College. He spent
roughly a decade in Osaka, fell in love with the city, and developed a style
based on Japanese origami crease patterns — bold, geometrically-inspired
murals in vivid color, precision-based.

He has painted across Japan, China, Nepal, Portugal, Jordan, and Canada.
Recent clients include Amazon, PayPal, and Kimpton Hotels. He worked on the
Nippon Paint "Color Way of Love" school revitalization project in Shanghai.
He is now based in Austin, Texas.

For SHINE 2018, he painted "We Are Going to Be Friends" — 25 feet by 103
feet, spray paint only, seven days.

### Mural Description
A full building-side mural depicting a group of rabbits rendered in DAAS's
signature geometric faceted style. The bunnies are built from interlocking
angular planes of soft grays, whites, lavenders, and pale blues, giving
them a fractured, cubist look while remaining clearly recognizable. They
sit in green grass along the base of the wall. The background is a
patchwork of large triangles in warm tones — golds, olive greens, corals,
and tans — creating a quilt-like geometric pattern behind the animals.
The DAAS tag appears in blocky letters in the upper right corner. The
piece covers the entire side of the Tri-J Towing building on 20th St.

muralInspiration: "Fourth in an ongoing narrative series featuring a young girl interacting with nature and animals. The previous three murals in the series were painted in Italy and China. The title references the White Stripes song. Completed in 7 days using only spray paint."

---

## George Rose (2019)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Melbourne, Australia
**Address:** 601 14th St S
**Building:** Campbell Park Handball Courts
**Instagram:** @george_rose

### Bio
Disney, Instagram, TikTok, Facebook, Nike. He paints endangered indigenous
flora at macro scale.

George Rose studied at the ANU School of Art in Canberra and is based in
Melbourne, Australia. His style is gestural mark-making with endemic
botanicals — bright surreal colors, signature gradients, hard edges with bold
undulating texture. He has painted for the STRAAT Museum in Amsterdam and the
Musee Pierre-Noel in France.

He won the Resene Total Colour Visual Art Award (2020), the Resene Total
Colour Award (2023), and "Best Mural" at the Street Art Australia Prize
(2019). For SHINE 2019, he painted all eight walls of a handball court at
Campbell Park — solo, brushes and paint sprayer, eight days.

### Mural Description
A sprawling botanical mural covering all eight walls of two back-to-back
handball courts at Campbell Park. Large curved bell-shaped flowers big
enough to climb into sit alongside massive fringed petals and tall
striated leaves stretching floor to ceiling. The color palette transitions
from golden orange at the top through pink in the middle to electric blue
at the base, all outlined in smoky blue linework. Half the flowers are
Florida natives and half are Australian natives, connecting Rose's home
country with the mural's location.

muralInspiration: "Half the flowers are Florida natives and half are native to Australia, connecting the artist's home country with St. Pete. Painted solo in 8 days on 8 walls using only buckets, brushes, and a paint sprayer."

---

## Illsol — Notice Us (2018)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Tampa, FL
**Address:** 2010 1st Ave S
**Instagram:** @_illsol_

### Bio
The name means "illuminated soul" — sol is sun in Spanish, a nod to
Michelle's half-Cuban heritage.

Michelle Sawyer and Tony Krol are Illsol, a husband-and-wife team based in
Tampa. Michelle completed a traditional tattoo apprenticeship and has a
teaching background. Tony is a self-taught stencil artist. They have worked
as a duo since 2014, with Michelle going solo since 2021. Their style blends
clean-lined, often symmetrical imagery with distressed, layered textures —
stencil work, graffiti, aerosol portraits, and solid color blocks.

They founded Mergeculture Gallery in Tampa Heights (2017) and co-founded the
Heights Walls community mural program. Clients include T-Mobile, the City of
Tampa, and the Tampa Convention Center. Their SHINE 2018 mural "Notice Us"
depicted an endangered Florida panther.

searchBio: |
The name means "illuminated soul" — sol is sun in Spanish, a nod to
Michelle's half-Cuban heritage.

Michelle Sawyer and Tony Krol are Illsol, a husband-and-wife team based in

muralDescription: |
A massive Florida panther portrait dominates the wall, its face rendered
in photorealistic detail with piercing gold-green eyes, whiskers, and
tawny fur blending into the surrounding abstract elements. The panther
looms 22 feet high over the intersection. Behind and around the big cat,
concentric rings in turquoise and coral create a haloed effect, while
scattered teal dots, monstera leaf silhouettes, and fragmented geometric
patterns fill the background. The palette balances the warm realism of
the panther against cool abstract elements in teals, corals, and greens.

muralInspiration: "A plea on behalf of Florida's endangered wildlife. The Florida Panther was nearly declared extinct in the 1990s, with only 120-230 adults remaining. 'Notice Us' asks humans to recognize the impact of development on Florida's fragile ecosystem."

### Mural Description
A massive Florida panther portrait dominates the wall, its face rendered
in photorealistic detail with piercing gold-green eyes, whiskers, and
tawny fur blending into the surrounding abstract elements. The panther
looms 22 feet high over the intersection. Behind and around the big cat,

**Building:** Zen Glass Studio
**Instagram:** @sarah.g.sheppard

### Bio
Her father was her mother's college art teacher. Both parents are artists.

Sarah Sheppard grew up in Dallas, studied photography, drawing, painting,

muralDescription: |
A nautical-themed piece centered on a golden porthole frame containing a
mermaid/siren figure with flowing dark hair and pale skin, entwined with
bright red octopus tentacles that curl through and around the circular
frame. The porthole is set within an elaborate compass rose or ship's
wheel pattern in gold, teal, and white, with geometric Art Deco-style
rays radiating outward. The background alternates between teal triangles
and white cross-hatched sections. The title "THE SIREN" appears in gold
lettering on a dark plaque at the bottom center. The composition has a
strong symmetrical, heraldic quality.

muralInspiration: "Commissioned by Josh of Zen Glass Studio, who gave Sheppard free reign. The octopus and mermaid reflect his glassblowing subjects. The tentacle inside the circle creates a yin-yang incorporating the 'zen' aspect, while the compass poses a question about temptation and direction."

### Mural Description

mermaid/siren figure with flowing dark hair and pale skin, entwined with
bright red octopus tentacles that curl through and around the circular
frame. The porthole is set within an elaborate compass rose or ship's
wheel pattern in gold, teal, and white, with geometric Art Deco-style
rays radiating outward. The background alternates between teal triangles
and white cross-hatched sections. The title "THE SIREN" appears in gold
lettering on a dark plaque at the bottom center. The composition has a
strong symmetrical, heraldic quality.

muralInspiration: "Commissioned by Josh of Zen Glass Studio, who gave Sheppard free reign. The octopus and mermaid reflect his glassblowing subjects. The tentacle inside the circle creates a yin-yang incorporating the 'zen' aspect, while the compass poses a question about temptation and direction."

## Ernesto Maranje

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** Miami, FL
**Address:** 2606 Fairfield Ave S
**Building:** Florida Wildlife Corridor Foundation HQ
**Instagram:** @ernestomaranje

### Bio
He discovered painting at twenty-seven because a knee injury kept him off
Coast Guard boats. His first attempt was a California flag on a mechanic's
shop window. Four years later he was painting at Wynwood Walls.

Ernesto Maranje is a Cuban-American artist born in Chicago in 1983, raised
in Miami's Hialeah neighborhood. He served seven years in the U.S. Coast
Guard and is entirely self-taught. By 2015 he had painted his first
large-scale mural in Little Havana and his career took off. His style
builds detailed, nature-immersive compositions — animals emerging from lush
organic backgrounds of flowers, foliage, and painterly brushstrokes.

He was selected for Wynwood Walls' ten-year anniversary in 2019, where he
painted "Sentries." Clients include Johnnie Walker (Blue Label Miami
Edition bottle), Disney Springs, Starbucks, Chase, and Chewy. He has
painted walls in Beirut, Sulaymaniyah (Iraq, for UNHCR/World Refugee Day),
a refugee camp in Athens, and three walls in Kyiv, Ukraine.

This piece at the Florida Wildlife Corridor Foundation headquarters shows
a great blue heron, a Florida black bear, and a swallowtail kite — all
moving in the same direction, symbolizing wildlife connectivity across
Florida.

searchBio: |
He discovered painting at twenty-seven because a knee injury kept him off
Coast Guard boats. His first attempt was a California flag on a mechanic's
shop window. Four years later he was painting at Wynwood Walls.

Ernesto Maranje is a Cuban-American artist born in Chicago in 1983 and
raised in Miami's Hialeah neighborhood. He served seven years in the U.S.
Coast Guard and is entirely self-taught — he discovered painting during
his service when a knee injury kept him off boats during his last year. By
2015 he had painted his first large-scale mural in Little Havana and his
career took off. His style builds detailed, nature-immersive compositions
where animals emerge from lush organic backgrounds of flowers, foliage,
and painterly brushstrokes.

He was selected to paint at Wynwood Walls for its prestigious ten-year
anniversary in 2019, where he painted "Sentries." Commercial clients
include Johnnie Walker (the Blue Label Miami Edition bottle design), Disney
Springs (Art Walk commission), Starbucks, Chase, and Chewy. International
work includes murals in Beirut, Lebanon; Sulaymaniyah, Iraq (with aptART
for UNHCR/World Refugee Day); a refugee camp in Athens; and three walls in
Kyiv, Ukraine. He has also painted at Beyond Walls in Lynn, Massachusetts.
Environmental work includes murals for PangeaSeed Sea Walls and Florida
Atlantic University.

This mural at the Florida Wildlife Corridor Foundation headquarters (2606
Fairfield Ave S, at The Factory) depicts a great blue heron with wings
spread, a Florida black bear, and a swallowtail kite — all animals moving
in the same direction, symbolizing the corridor concept of wildlife
movement and ecological connectivity across Florida. The palette is moody
and atmospheric — deep blues, greens, and stormy grays.

Instagram: @ernestomaranje. Website: maranje.com.

muralDescription: |
A richly detailed nature scene on the facade of the Florida Wildlife
Corridor Foundation headquarters at The Factory. A great blue heron
dominates the center, wings spread mid-stride, rendered in naturalistic
grays, blues, and whites with remarkable feather detail. A Florida black
bear peers from the left side among lush green foliage. A swallowtail
kite swoops through the upper portion while the lower background suggests
dark water and vegetation. The palette is moody and atmospheric — deep
blues, greens, and stormy grays — giving it a more serious, conservation-
minded tone than typical mural work. All the animals face the same
direction, symbolizing the corridor concept of wildlife movement and
connectivity.

muralInspiration: "Commissioned by the Florida Wildlife Corridor Foundation for their headquarters. All animals are depicted moving in the same direction, symbolizing the need for wildlife movement and ecological connectivity across Florida."

### Mural Description
A richly detailed nature scene on the facade of the Florida Wildlife
Corridor Foundation headquarters at The Factory. A great blue heron
dominates the center, wings spread mid-stride, rendered in naturalistic
grays, blues, and whites with remarkable feather detail. A Florida black
bear peers from the left side among lush green foliage. A swallowtail
kite swoops through the upper portion while the lower background suggests
dark water and vegetation. The palette is moody and atmospheric — deep
blues, greens, and stormy grays — giving it a more serious, conservation-
minded tone than typical mural work. All the animals face the same
direction, symbolizing the corridor concept of wildlife movement and
connectivity.

muralInspiration: "Commissioned by the Florida Wildlife Corridor Foundation for their headquarters. All animals are depicted moving in the same direction, symbolizing the need for wildlife movement and ecological connectivity across Florida."

## Raisa Nosova — Woman of Light (2025)

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** Hoboken, NJ
**Address:** 2606 Fairfield Ave S
**Building:** Museum of Motherhood (The Factory)
**Instagram:** @raisanosova

### Bio
Raisa Nosova is a Russian-born multidisciplinary artist working in painting,
murals, blown glass sculpture, drawing, animation, and performance art. She
earned her MFA from NYU Steinhardt and her BFA from the Fashion Institute
of Technology, and studied blown glass at Urban Glass in Brooklyn. She also
attended Vesalius College in Brussels and the Art Students League of New
York. Her experience of becoming a mother during her MFA profoundly shifted
her practice toward themes of motherhood, trauma, resilience, displacement,
and the invisible labor of caregiving — she was two months pregnant when
Russia bombed Kyiv, and with close family in Ukraine, that event also shaped
her work. She's best known for blown glass breast sculptures exploring
postpartum experience, and her anti-war mural "Uprooted" on a Ukrainian-
owned gas station in Jersey City. She's exhibited at Theodore Art Gallery
in Manhattan, La Galerie Emeric Hahn in Paris, and Mana Contemporary in
Jersey City, and has held residencies at the Berlin Art Institute and the
Ramona Residency in Houston (the first artist-mother residency in Texas).
She has over 15 years of teaching experience, including adjunct
professorships at NYU and FIT.

muralDescription: |
A dynamic, warm-toned mural on the facade of the Museum of Motherhood at
The Factory. A female figure is depicted in a crouching or seated position,
rendered in expressive brushwork in reds, golds, pinks, and deep magentas.
Radiating golden light beams and architectural fragments burst outward from
behind the figure, creating a sense of energy and movement. The composition
has a stained-glass quality with bold color blocks and strong outlines. The
"MoM" (Museum of Motherhood) text is visible in the upper left. The piece
wraps around the building entrance, framing the doorway.

muralInspiration: "Painted for the Museum of Motherhood after Nosova met the founder and felt both the urge and responsibility to contribute to raising awareness for the museum, the vital institution in a society that so often feels isolating for mothers."

### Mural Description
A dynamic, warm-toned mural on the facade of the Museum of Motherhood at
The Factory. A female figure is depicted in a crouching or seated position,
rendered in expressive brushwork in reds, golds, pinks, and deep magentas.
Radiating golden light beams and architectural fragments burst outward from
behind the figure, creating a sense of energy and movement. The composition
has a stained-glass quality with bold color blocks and strong outlines. The
"MoM" (Museum of Motherhood) text is visible in the upper left. The piece
wraps around the building entrance, framing the doorway.

muralInspiration: "Painted for the Museum of Motherhood after Nosova met the founder and felt both the urge and responsibility to contribute to raising awareness for the museum, the vital institution in a society that so often feels isolating for mothers."

## Angela Faustina — Citrus Medley (2018)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Atlanta, GA
**Address:** 1133 Baum Ave N
**Building:** Green Bench Brewing Company
**Instagram:** @angelafaustina

### Bio
She lives with Lupus. She paints fruit at macro scale — beauty, fragility,
strength, resilience, all in one orange.

Angela Faustina was born in 1986 and is based in Atlanta. She graduated
summa cum laude with a BA in Liberal Arts (fine art and history) from New
College of Florida in Sarasota, with additional coursework at Parsons and
Ringling. Her style is hyperrealistic contemporary oil painting of fruit in
extreme close-up — bridging realism and idealism beyond traditional still
life.

She has had 80-plus exhibitions since 2006 across the US, Italy, Portugal,
the UK, Singapore, Australia, and Hong Kong. She is represented by Signet
Contemporary Art in London. She won a Georgia Visual Artists Grant (2020) and
Best Book Cover of 2023 from Electric Literature. Her "Flourishing Florence"
mural in Florence, Alabama spans 246 feet.

For SHINE 2018, she painted "Citrus Medley" in collaboration with Green Bench
Brewing — two seven-by-sixteen-foot panels, brush only, nine days.

### Mural Description
Two matching wall sections flanking a sliding metal gate at Green Bench Brewing Company, each approximately seven feet high by sixteen feet long. The mural features oversized cross-section slices of citrus fruits — blood oranges, grapefruits, lemons, limes, and navel oranges — painted in vivid reds, purples, magentas, yellows, greens, and oranges against a warm background. The fruit slices are rendered in Faustina's signature hyperrealistic style, showing the detailed membrane structure, seeds, and juice of each variety. The left wall leans toward warmer tones with more blood orange and grapefruit, while the right wall balances warm and cool with prominent lemon and lime slices. Both walls are signed "ANGELA FAUSTINA" at the bottom. She completed the piece in nine days using only a brush — it was her largest work at the time.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Jimmy Breen & Anthony Freese — Lucky You (2019)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 1701 Central Ave
**Building:** 1701 Central
**Instagram:** @waxandhive

### Bio
Jimmy Breen designed album art for the Grateful Dead, Bruno Mars, and Green
Day. He died unexpectedly in June 2022. He was 37.

Breen and Anthony Freese co-founded Wax & Hive, a design studio in St.
Petersburg. Both graduated from the USF St. Petersburg graphic design
program. Together they had over two decades of experience in illustration
and graphic design. Breen was a Senior Illustrator at Warner Music Group.
Freese kept the studio going.

Their style is bold, graphic, pop-art-influenced — tattoo culture meets
vintage signage meets clean design. Their client list ran from Cardi B and
Blake Shelton to Bloomin' Brands and Hunger & Thirst Group. They were
fixtures in St. Pete's creative scene.

For SHINE 2019 they painted "Lucky You" on the parking garage of the 1701
Central apartment building, facing I-275 — eighteen by forty-two feet,
fifteen good luck symbols from cultures around the world. The premise: "we're
all lucky to live in Saint Pete." They followed it with an interior courtyard
mural for the building's 2020 grand opening.

Breen is remembered as one of St. Pete's most prolific and beloved muralists.

searchBio: |
Jimmy Breen designed album art for the Grateful Dead, Bruno Mars, and Green
Day. He died unexpectedly in June 2022. He was 37.

Breen and Anthony Freese co-founded Wax & Hive, a design studio in St.
Petersburg. Both graduated from the USF St. Petersburg graphic design
program. Together they had over two decades of experience in illustration
and graphic design. Breen was a Senior Illustrator at Warner Music Group.

Breen is remembered as one of St. Pete's most prolific and beloved muralists.

muralDescription: |
An 18-by-42-foot mural on the parking garage of the 1701 Central apartment building, facing I-275 North and visible to highway traffic. The piece is a 3-by-5 grid of panels depicting good luck symbols from cultures around the world, inspired by the idea that "we're all lucky to live in Saint Pete." Each panel features a bold graphic illustration rendered in a pink, blue, yellow, black, and red color scheme. The symbols include: "Good Luck" in black cursive script, a Lincoln penny, a pair of dice, crossed fingers, an anchor, a ladybug, an Italian cornicello on a chain, a Japanese maneki-neko (beckoning cat), a yellow smiley face, a Chinese lucky bat, an anatomical heart, a horseshoe pointing up, a shooting star, a bird on a branch, a hamsa hand with an evil eye, and a four-leaf clover. The style is clean, graphic, and pop-art-influenced with bold black outlines and limited flat color fills.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
An 18-by-42-foot mural on the parking garage of the 1701 Central apartment building, facing I-275 North and visible to highway traffic. The piece is a 3-by-5 grid of panels depicting good luck symbols from cultures around the world, inspired by the idea that "we're all lucky to live in Saint Pete." Each panel features a bold graphic illustration rendered in a pink, blue, yellow, black, and red color scheme. The symbols include: "Good Luck" in black cursive script, a Lincoln penny, a pair of dice, crossed fingers, an anchor, a ladybug, an Italian cornicello on a chain, a Japanese maneki-neko (beckoning cat), a yellow smiley face, a Chinese lucky bat, an anatomical heart, a horseshoe pointing up, a shooting star, a bird on a branch, a hamsa hand with an evil eye, and a four-leaf clover. The style is clean, graphic, and pop-art-influenced with bold black outlines and limited flat color fills.

## Dasic Fernández — Multicolored Lady (2016)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** New York, NY
**Address:** 1720 Central Ave
**Building:** FRSTeam by Rogers
**Instagram:** @dasicfernandez

### Bio
He painted a 27-story building for the Super Bowl. He was the only Latin
American artist selected.

Dasic Fernandez was born in Rancagua, Chile. He studied architecture at the
University of Chile for four years, leaving in his final year to pursue art
full-time. His style is portraiture with an "inverse drip" — rivulets of
color floating upward from subjects. Light impressionism with political
undertones, influenced by graffiti, Van Gogh, Kandinsky, and Roberto Matta.

He co-filmed Pan-American Graffiti, a documentary tracing a 100-day journey
from New York to Santiago with thirteen large-scale murals. He worked with
the Rebel Diaz Arts Collective on a 70-foot rooftop protest mural reading
"No human being is illegal." He has painted at Wynwood Walls and was
recognized by The New York Times and Americas Quarterly as an Arts Innovator.

For SHINE 2016, he painted in St. Petersburg.

### Mural Description
A 90-by-17-foot mural on the east side of the FRSTeam by Rogers building, facing 17th Street. On a black background, a woman tilts her face upward with her eyes closed and arms spread wide, receiving multicolored rain. Her skin and flowing hair are rendered in dripping layers of blue, purple, magenta, orange, green, and gold — Dasic's signature "inverse drip" style that gives the colors a sense of upward motion. She wears a pale translucent blue sleeveless top that contrasts with the rich colors streaming across her body. White clouds float in the dark sky and white puddles frame the base of the composition. Near each shoulder, a hummingbird hovers — the one on the left sipping a colored raindrop as it falls.

muralInspiration: ""

---

## Michael Reeder — Beneath the Shell (2016)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Los Angeles, CA
**Address:** 1701 1st Ave S
**Building:** FRSTeam by Rogers
**Instagram:** @reederone

### Bio
He did a residency at Red Bull House of Art in Detroit. His work has been
featured in Forbes and Juxtapoz.

Michael Reeder was born in 1982 in Dallas and earned his BFA in Painting
from the School of Visual Arts in New York in 2007. His figurative work
mixes realism with flat graphic space — vibrant colors, geometric patterns,
lifelike imagery exploring identity, ambiguity, ego, and mortality. Skate
and street culture run through everything.

He is represented by Allouche Gallery (LA and NY), Hashimoto Contemporary
(SF), and Thinkspace Gallery. He has shown at the Fort Wayne Museum of Art,
Long Beach Museum of Art, Mesa Contemporary Arts Museum, and internationally
in London, Basel, Melbourne, and Honolulu. For SHINE 2016, he painted in St.
Petersburg.

searchBio: |
He did a residency at Red Bull House of Art in Detroit. His work has been
featured in Forbes and Juxtapoz.

Michael Reeder was born in 1982 in Dallas and earned his BFA in Painting
from the School of Visual Arts in New York in 2007. His figurative work
mixes realism with flat graphic space — vibrant colors, geometric patterns,
lifelike imagery exploring identity, ambiguity, ego, and mortality. Skate
and street culture run through everything.

He is represented by Allouche Gallery (LA and NY), Hashimoto Contemporary
(SF), and Thinkspace Gallery. He has shown at the Fort Wayne Museum of Art,
Long Beach Museum of Art, Mesa Contemporary Arts Museum, and internationally
in London, Basel, Melbourne, and Honolulu. For SHINE 2016, he painted in St.
Petersburg.

muralDescription: |
A mural on the north side of the FRSTeam by Rogers building, facing the alley between 1st Avenue South and Central Avenue. The left half of the wall is covered in a repeating three-dimensional cube pattern in pink, teal, and gold on a salmon-pink background — an optical illusion that appears to project outward from the wall. The pattern extends over a roll-up garage door. On the right, a photorealistic greyscale human skull emerges from behind the cubes, its jaw and cranium rendered in precise detail. To the far right, a fragment of a living face — just the nose, eyes, and brow — peeks from behind the skull in warm flesh tones. The composition moves left to right from abstraction to death to life, illustrating the title's idea of what exists beneath outward appearances.

muralInspiration: ""

## BASK (2014)

**Category:** shine-legacy
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 648 Central Ave
**Building:** Times Square Properties
**Instagram:** @knownasbask

### Bio
He fled communist Czechoslovakia as a child and grew up in Florida. His work
explores the parallels between state propaganda and American consumerism.

Ales Bask Hostomsky — BASK (Because Art Should Kill) — was born in
Czechoslovakia (birth year disputed: 1977 or 1984) and is self-taught. He
works in mixed media on wood and canvas — rich textures and "anti-iconic"
imagery deconstructing media. His art appeared in Iron Man 3.

His work is in the permanent collection of the Jacksonville Museum of Modern
Art and the Florida International Museum. He shows at Parlor Gallery in
Asbury Park and Signari Gallery. For SHINE, he painted in both 2014 and 2020.

### Mural Description
A roughly 100-foot-long black-and-white collage mural on the east-facing wall of 648 Central Avenue. The piece is a dense retrospective of BASK's graphic vocabulary: overlapping panels of greyscale portraits, faces, figures, and icons rendered in his signature distressed, wheat-paste-influenced style. A salmon-pink geometric panel near the top right provides the only color. On the right side, "BASK" is repeated five times in stacked block letters — alternating black-on-white and white-on-black — with a single red "S" breaking the monochrome. Below the stacked name, "AMERICAN ANIMAL" appears in bold white type. The overall effect is a visual manifesto — layered, gritty, and unapologetically personal.

muralInspiration: ""

## LOOK the Weird — Existence is Absurd (2018)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Berlin, Germany
**Address:** 1114 Central Ave
**Building:** Central Arcade
**Instagram:** @looktheweird

### Bio
He co-founded The Weird — an international graffiti crew alongside Nychos,
Low Bros, and DXTR. He has been painting walls since he was fifteen in East
Germany.

Lars Wunderlich — LOOK the Weird — was born in 1981 in Neubrandenburg, East
Germany. He studied art at Ernst Moritz Arndt University in Greifswald and
earned a graphic design degree from HTW Berlin. His current work uses
algorithm-generated portraits with distortions and glitches — post-graffiti
visual collages exploring technology and art, parallel to the New Leipzig
School but centered on AI aesthetics.

He co-founded PEACHBEACH design company (2007-2019) and KIDZONCOMPUTERS
animation studio (2020-present). His "AM I JUST A CODE FOR YOU?" showed at
Urban Nation Museum in Berlin (2022-2024). He has done residencies in Denver,
Belgrade, and Chennai.

For SHINE 2018, he painted in St. Petersburg.

### Mural Description
A wide mural on the west-facing wall of the Central Arcade building at 1114 Central Avenue, visible to eastbound traffic on 1st Avenue South. The piece is a surreal, dreamlike panorama painted in Wunderlich's signature distortion-collage style. Flowing organic shapes in pink, magenta, teal, mint green, and cream swirl across the wall, evoking melting landscapes and wavy glass. Among the abstract forms, recognizable elements emerge: a figure reading or holding books, rippling striped surfaces, plant-like tendrils, and architectural fragments. The composition references Florida's environment — the piece was inspired by hurricane damage, red tide, and the contradictions of contemporary life in the state. The overall effect is of something familiar viewed through a funhouse mirror: colorful and inviting at a distance, unsettling and thought-provoking up close.

muralInspiration: |
"My piece in St. Pete is all about Florida and the environment. I love to provoke questions." — LOOK the Weird

muralAwards: ""

## Ya La'Ford (2022)

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 569 22nd St S
**Building:** Power Moves Electric
**Instagram:** @yalaford

### Bio
She has an MFA and a law degree. She represented the United States at the
Dakar Biennale in Senegal.

Ya La'Ford is a first-generation American of Jamaican heritage, from the
Bronx. She earned her MFA from the Art Institute of Boston (now Lesley
University) and her Juris Doctor from the University of Florida. She
maintains studios in St. Petersburg, the Bronx, Jamaica, and Maine.

Her work spans painting, sculpture, sound, print, video, and immersive
installations using light, water, sound, and motion. Sharp geometric lines,
bold contrasting colors, vibrant circles and triangles. She sits on the Board
of Trustees of the Museum of Fine Arts in St. Petersburg. She designed an
Arrow McLaren SP IndyCar and was a featured artist for Super Bowl 2021.

Clients include the Tampa Bay Rays, Toronto Blue Jays, and Nike. Her work is
in the collections of Tropicana Field, Al Lang Stadium, the Morean Arts
Center, and the Boston Public Library. She has shown at The James Museum, The
Ringling Museum of Art, and Florida CraftArt.

For SHINE 2021, she painted in St. Petersburg.

### Mural Description
A mural covering the front facade of the Power Moves Electric warehouse on 22nd Street South. On a solid black cinder-block wall, three geometric compositions are arranged symmetrically: two matching half-circle forms flanking the sides and a full circle centered above a metal door. Each form is filled with angular, crystalline white linework — interlocking straight-line segments that create a stained-glass or shattered-ice pattern. Subtle accents of pastel blue, pink, and warm gold highlight select segments within the white network. The black background gives the piece a nighttime quality, making the white geometry glow. "Ya La'ford" is signed in white script in the lower right corner. The style is consistent with Ya La'Ford's broader body of work exploring sacred geometry and light refraction.

muralInspiration: ""

## Sydney Prusso (2021)

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 2606 Fairfield Ave S
**Building:** Fairgrounds St. Pete (FloridaRAMA)
**Instagram:** @sydbloomstudio

### Bio
She graduated from MICA and has painted fifty-plus murals across six states,
installations up to 75 feet.

Sydney Prusso is an illustrator, lettering artist, and muralist based in the
Tampa Bay area. Her style is contemporary botanical — flat illustration
rooted in graphic design, warm 1960s and 70s color palettes, flowing poster
lettering. Nature, the outdoors, and mental health run through the work. She
has done a Reef Organization project in the Florida Keys.

Clients include Salesforce, Adobe, Dickies, PepsiCo, John Deere, Stihl, the
NFL, and AstraZeneca. For SHINE 2022, she painted two murals in St.
Petersburg.

muralDescription: |
A long horizontal mural covering the side of a low warehouse building in the Fairgrounds / Factory St. Pete complex. The piece is a bold, graphic composition of abstract geometric shapes with hard edges and flat fills in teal, pink, black, maroon, and blue. A large six-pointed teal star dominates the center. To its left, a black field is filled with salmon-pink polka dots. Flowing red and maroon organic shapes weave between diagonal teal stripes and chevrons. Blue teardrop forms, pink arches, and a spiral motif round out the composition on the right. Two small windows are integrated into the design. The style is pop-art influenced with strong graphic sensibility — reminiscent of Memphis Design or 1980s postmodern illustration.

muralInspiration: ""
