## PHYBR (2024)

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** Kansas City, MO
**Address:** 1756 Central Ave
**Building:** The Neon Lunchbox
**Instagram:** @phybr

### Bio
He spent two weeks as A$AP Rocky's creative director. He has a background in
horticulture.

Ken Dushane III — PHYBR — grew up in Toledo, Ohio, started in graffiti and
skateboarding, got into lowrider culture in high school, and learned
airbrushing and pinstriping along the way. He studied IT and computer
networking, spent three years on a graphic design degree, and played in bands
before rediscovering art around age thirty after a divorce. He started
painting graffiti seriously in Detroit around 2013.

He has painted over 250 murals in twelve-plus years — hyperrealistic
portraits, animals, objects, creatures, and the "pop-esque tropical foliage"
that his horticulture background feeds. Clients include Universal Pictures
(advertising for the movie Night Swim), rapper Tee Grizzley, the Truman
music venue and Lyric Opera House in Kansas City.

He relocated to Kansas City in 2022 and holds a residency at Upper Level
Gallery. He has worked with youth through the Young Artists at Work program
in Toledo and after-school programs at the College for Creative Studies in
Detroit.

searchBio: |
He spent two weeks as A$AP Rocky's creative director. He has a background in
horticulture.

Kenneth "Ken" Dushane III — PHYBR — grew up in Toledo, Ohio, originally
getting into art through graffiti and skateboarding culture. In high school he
discovered lowrider culture and learned airbrushing and pinstriping. He earned
an associate's degree in IT / Computer Networking Systems and spent three
years studying graphic design toward a bachelor's. He played in bands and
rediscovered visual art around age thirty after a divorce, painting graffiti
seriously in Detroit starting around 2013-2014.

He has painted over 250 murals professionally in twelve-plus years. His style
fuses hyperrealistic portraits, animals, objects, and creatures with
"pop-esque tropical foliage" informed by his background in horticulture. He
is expanding into fine art and 3D sculptural work, debuting with his "Poison
Garden" show. Specializations include landscape, figurative, and typography.

Clients include A$AP Rocky (creative director for two weeks, multiple
projects), Universal Pictures (advertising for Night Swim), rapper Tee
Grizzley, the Truman music venue (Kansas City), the Lyric Opera House (Kansas
City), and From The Earth Dispensary. He has shown at Epiphany Center for the
Arts in Chicago ("Foliage #3") and holds a residency at Upper Level Gallery
in Kansas City.

He has worked with youth through the Young Artists at Work program in Toledo,
Ohio and after-school art programs at the College for Creative Studies in
Detroit. He relocated to Kansas City in 2022 from Toledo via Detroit.

Festival credits include SpraySeeMO Mural Festival (participated every year
2017-2021+) and work in Detroit, Chicago, Kalamazoo, Kansas City, Toledo,
Miami, Los Angeles, and St. Petersburg. His Neon Lunchbox mural on Central
Avenue is a commercial commission, not a SHINE piece.

Instagram: @phybr. Website: phybrart.com.

muralDescription: |
An exterior mural on the side of The Neon Lunchbox, an 80s/90s-themed restaurant and cocktail bar at 1756 Central Avenue. The piece is a swirling optical-illusion vortex of hyper-realistic 80s and 90s toys and pop culture icons against a dark background. Items spiral inward toward a central vanishing point: Tom and Jerry mid-chase, a Rubik's cube, a pink-and-black Furby with glowing blue eyes, a grey Beanie Baby elephant with its red TY heart tag, a Game Boy, porcelain dolls, a Tamagotchi, and other childhood artifacts of the era. The rendering is photorealistic — each toy casts its own shadow and catches light as if tumbling through space. The vortex effect creates a sense of being pulled into a nostalgic time warp, matching the restaurant's retro theme.

muralInspiration: ""

## Melanie Posner — Iryna (2026)

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** Los Angeles, CA
**Address:** 2200 block Central Ave
**Building:** instagram: therealmelpoz
**Instagram:** @therealmelpoz

### Bio
She painted the woman in the red headscarf on the back of the Hollander
Hotel in under five days, then went straight to Walt Disney World to paint
scenic art for Star Wars: Galaxy's Edge.

Melanie Posner was born around 1993 in a small town outside Philadelphia.
She moved to Tampa in 2013 and earned her BFA in Painting from the
University of Tampa in 2016. Her work is photorealistic portraits of
women — oversized, expressive eyes set against rainbow palettes,
psychedelic paisley backgrounds, and florals. "Painting females and
exploring how complex they are kind of helped me figure out myself, and
who I was."

She was a cast member on Amazon Prime's docuseries Tampa Baes (2021), a
show about a Tampa Bay lesbian friend group. Amazon commissioned her to
paint a mural on a bus that toured the city promoting the show. Her
painting "Passion" appears on screen.

In 2024 she painted a forty-foot mural on the LGBT Center of Los Angeles
for TikTok's "Create the Future" campaign. She is now based in Los Angeles.

Her SHINE 2018 mural "New Beginnings" is a twenty-three by sixty-five foot
photorealistic woman lying on her side, one eye peeking through her
fingers, on 7th Terrace South.

searchBio: |
She painted the woman in the red headscarf on the back of the Hollander
Hotel in under five days, then went straight to Walt Disney World to paint
scenic art for Star Wars: Galaxy's Edge.

Melanie "Mel" Posner was born around 1993 in a small town just outside
Philadelphia. She moved to Tampa in 2013 and earned her BFA in Painting
from the University of Tampa in 2016, where she also studied art therapy.
She was previously based in Gulfport, Florida, and is now in Los Angeles.

Her style is photorealistic portraits of women — oversized, expressive
eyes, vibrant rainbow color palettes, psychedelic paisley backgrounds,
floral elements. She blends lifelike detail with touches of dreamy
surrealism. She works in oil on canvas, collage, prints, and large-scale
murals. She sources her subjects from Pinterest, Google, and social media,
looking for eyes, story, and confidence. "Working large is just really
exciting," she says. "I feel like I'm a little bit more free doing murals."
Her stated philosophy: paint strong, confident, independent women to be
inspired by them.

For SHINE 2018, she was one of five local "Open Call" artists. Her mural
"New Beginnings" at 4139 7th Terrace South is twenty-three feet high by
sixty-five feet long — a photorealistic woman lying horizontally on her
side, her hand cupping the right side of her face, one eye peeking through
her fingers. She completed it in October 2018.

Her commercial clients include Walt Disney World (scenic artwork for Star
Wars: Galaxy's Edge at Hollywood Studios, Orlando), New Belgium Brewing
(the eighteen-foot Hollander Hotel mural of a woman in a red headscarf
with a glass of Mural Agua Fresca Cerveza, completed in under five days),
Amazon Prime Video (a mural on a promotional bus for Tampa Baes), and
TikTok. For TikTok's 2024 "Create the Future" campaign, she painted a
forty-foot mural on the LGBT Center of Los Angeles at 8745 Santa Monica
Blvd in West Hollywood.

She was a cast member on all eight episodes of Tampa Baes (Amazon Prime
Video, 2021), a docuseries about a Tampa Bay lesbian friend group produced
by 3 Ball Productions and filmed over roughly ten weeks starting April
2021. She described herself as "neutral" and avoiding drama on the show.
"My anxiety during filming was up to the roof, my mental health totally
depleted. It was very stressful and I will say, I love how the show came
out." Her oil painting "Passion" (also called "Summer and Marissa")
appears on screen. She worked for years as a bartender at The Honey Pot,
a landmark LGBTQ+ club in Ybor City, Tampa.

She has exhibited at Enigma gallery in downtown St. Petersburg.
Instagram: @therealmelpoz. Website: melanieposner.com.

muralDescription: |
The mural depicts Iryna Zarutska, a23-year-old Ukrainian refugee who was stabbed and killed on the Charlotte,
North Carolina light rail on August 22, 2025, in a random attack. The portrait
shows her with flowing golden-blonde hair, gazing gently toward the viewer
with a soft, warm expression. A pink hibiscus flower blooms near her shoulder.
The background transitions from pale mint green on the left to warm golden
yellow on the right. The piece is rendered in Posner's signature photorealistic
style with warm, luminous skin tones. Posner was seen painting from a green
scissor lift on the day the photo was taken.

muralInspiration: |
Iryna Zarutska fled Ukraine during the Russian invasion and resettled in
Charlotte, North Carolina. On August 22, 2025, she was stabbed from behind
while seated on the Lynx Blue Line light rail at East/West Boulevard station.
She was 23 years old. Her death drew international attention and prompted
North Carolina to pass "Iryna's Law," a criminal justice package addressing
bail and mental health evaluations.

### Mural Description
The mural depicts Iryna Zarutska, a23-year-old Ukrainian refugee who was stabbed and killed on the Charlotte,
North Carolina light rail on August 22, 2025, in a random attack. The portrait
shows her with flowing golden-blonde hair, gazing gently toward the viewer
with a soft, warm expression. A pink hibiscus flower blooms near her shoulder.
The background transitions from pale mint green on the left to warm golden
yellow on the right. The piece is rendered in Posner's signature photorealistic
style with warm, luminous skin tones. Posner was seen painting from a green
scissor lift on the day the photo was taken.

muralInspiration: |

## Cecilia Lueza — Encounter (2018)

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 2612 22nd St S
**Building:** ArtsXchange
**Instagram:** @cecilialuezaap

### Bio
She painted an entire intersection. Fifty volunteers helped. Then a storm
washed it away, so they painted it again.

Cecilia Lueza was born in 1971 in Posadas, in Argentina's subtropical
northeast — a landscape of dense flora and fauna that still runs through
everything she makes. She earned her MA in Visual Arts from the National
University of La Plata in Buenos Aires, held her first solo exhibition there
in 1998, and moved to the United States shortly after. She settled in
Pinellas County in 2007.

Her work spans murals, monumental sculptures, and mixed-media installations,
all built on bold geometric patterns, clean lines, and jewel-tone color.
Birds, women, and children recur. She was originally a monochromatic painter
in Argentina — Florida's light changed that.

The public art resume is staggering: nearly 60 installations since 2013.
Starbucks, the North Carolina Museum of Art, the Atlanta BeltLine, the James
Museum, a kinetic angel sculpture in Chattanooga acquired for permanent public
display. Her work sits in the Bass Museum of Art, the Museum of Contemporary
Art Miami, and the Florida Department of State collection.

She is a four-time SHINE artist — "Common Ground" in 2016 (the intersection),
"Encounter" and "The Blue Hour" in 2018, "The Sky Is the Limit" in 2019, and
"Radiance" at the Haddy Factory for SHINE 2025 Origins.

searchBio: |
She painted an entire intersection. Fifty volunteers helped. Then a storm
washed it away, so they painted it again.

Cecilia Lueza was born in 1971 in Posadas, Misiones Province, Argentina —
subtropical northeast, dense flora and fauna, a landscape that still runs
through everything she makes. She earned her MA in Visual Arts (Painting) from
the National University of La Plata in Buenos Aires (1990-1994), held her
first solo exhibition in Buenos Aires in 1998, and moved to the United States
in 1999 after meeting Miami-based artists. She lived in Miami before settling
in Pinellas County, Florida in 2007. She cites Georgia O'Keeffe and Francis
Bacon as influences.

Her work spans murals, monumental sculptures, and mixed-media installations —
bold geometric patterns, clean lines, jewel-tone color gradations. Birds,
women, and children recur. She was originally a monochromatic painter in
Argentina; Florida's light and vibrancy changed that. Nature is her primary
subject. She calls her art an attempt to embody "universal meaning such as
innocence, beauty, freedom."

The public art resume is staggering: nearly 60 installations since 2013, over
20 years as a working public artist. Major murals include "I Am My Own Muse"
at the Marriott City Center in Durham NC (2019), "Exuberance" at HCC Dale
Mabry Campus in Tampa (2020), "Summertime" in Clearwater (2021, selected from
190+ nationwide applicants), "Chromascape" in Jersey City (2021), "The Legend
of Ondine" in Lakeland (2022), "The Starbucks Siren" at 3939 4th St N in St.
Petersburg (2023), "Life Force" at Arya Residences in St. Petersburg (2024),
and "Morning Ritual" at Joffrey's Coffee HQ in Tampa (2026).

Public sculptures include "Winds of Change" — a kinetic stainless steel angel
acquired by the Chattanooga Public Art Commission for permanent display —
"Luminarium" at the Arts and Innovation Center in Rockville MD (interactive
wall sculptures with fiber optics and LEDs activated by sound), "Time Flies
Sundial" at River Terrace Education Campus in Washington DC, multiple school
commissions for the DC Department of General Services, "Delta" and "Nova" in
Jacksonville, "Dual Nature" in Reno, "Journey of Hope" in Fort Myers, "Nexus"
in Tampa, "Caleidoscopio" in Toledo, "Sea Orb" in Jacksonville, and "Golden
Hour" at the Orange County Administration Center in Orlando (2026).

Clients and commissions include Starbucks, the North Carolina Museum of Art
(mural for a Frida Kahlo/Diego Rivera exhibition), the Atlanta BeltLine, the
City of Atlanta Mayor's Office of Cultural Affairs, the Chattanooga Public Art
Commission, Hillsborough Community College, and municipalities in
Indianapolis, Clearwater, Fort Myers, and Jacksonville. Her work is in the
collections of the Bass Museum of Art Miami, the Museum of Contemporary Art
Miami, the Florida Department of State, and 13+ institutional collections.
Gallery representation includes Kavachnina Contemporary, Onessimo Fine Art
(Palm Beach Gardens), Mary Martin Art Gallery (Naples), and Naomi Silva
Gallery (Atlanta).

Awards include a Tourist Development Council Grant (Miami-Dade, 2011),
Professional Development Individual Artist Grant (Pinellas County, 2008),
Florida Artist Enhancement Grant (2008), Puffin Foundation Grant (2006),
Karrvaz Artist-in-Residency in Spain (2006), and Sculpture Key West People's
Choice Award (2006).

She is a four-time SHINE artist: "Common Ground" (2016) — a street mural at
Central Ave and 5th Street outside Florida CraftArt, painted with 50+
volunteers, washed away by a storm, painted again; "Encounter" (2018) at the
ArtsXchange in the Warehouse Arts District; "The Blue Hour" (2018) at ONE St.
Petersburg; "The Sky Is the Limit" (2019) — a 164 x 11 foot ground mural at
North Shore Park pier; and "Radiance" (2025) at the Haddy Factory for SHINE
Origins. Instagram: @cecilialuezaap. Website: lueza.com.

muralDescription: |
Two stylized birds face each other on a cinder-block wall at the entrance
to the ArtsXchange campus. On the left, a red bird with a dark head faces
right, its fanned tail and wing feathers radiating outward in warm geometric
wedges of red, orange, pink, magenta, and yellow. On the right, a blue bird
faces left, its feathers spreading in cool geometric wedges of deep navy,
royal blue, teal, and green. The two birds are silhouetted against a pale
sky-blue background, their mirrored poses creating a sense of dialogue or
greeting. "LUEZA" is signed in blue at the lower left. The style is Lueza's
signature geometric abstraction — each feather rendered as a clean, flat
color wedge radiating from a central spiral.

muralInspiration: |
Originally painted in 2018, the mural deteriorated over time from fading
and nearby construction damage. In April 2023, Lueza restored the piece
with funding from a National Endowment for the Arts grant administered
through Creative Pinellas. She and ArtsXchange leadership chose restoration
over a new design because they remained "very fond of this one."

muralAwards: ""

## Daniel Mrgan — A Picnic (2016)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 1701 1st Ave S
**Building:** FRSTeam by Rogers
**Instagram:** @danielmrgan

### Bio
He immigrated to the US at twenty-one as a refugee. His family fled the
Yugoslav conflict from a contested city near the Serbian border, first to
Berlin, then to America.

Daniel Mrgan is originally from Croatia, now based in St. Petersburg. He
earned a graphic design degree from St. Petersburg College and also studied
at Schiller International University. His fine art is whimsical and
illustrative — introspective characters, wood burnings, immersive narrative
works combining illustrations, sculptures, and texts. He treats fine art as
personal passion alongside his graphic design career.

His solo show "Sir Dr. Admiral Rupert Xavier's Paper Plane Flights to Exotic
Mind Destinations" at Bluelucy captured the tone. For SHINE 2016, he painted
"A Picnic" at 1701 1st Avenue South.

searchBio: |
He immigrated to the US at twenty-one as a refugee. His family fled the
Yugoslav conflict from a contested city near the Serbian border, first to
Berlin, then to America.

Daniel Mrgan is originally from Croatia, now based in St. Petersburg. He
earned a graphic design degree from St. Petersburg College and also studied
at Schiller International University. His fine art is whimsical and
illustrative — introspective characters, wood burnings, immersive narrative
works combining illustrations, sculptures, and texts. He treats fine art as
personal passion alongside his graphic design career.

His solo show "Sir Dr. Admiral Rupert Xavier's Paper Plane Flights to Exotic
Mind Destinations" at Bluelucy captured the tone. For SHINE 2016, he painted
"A Picnic" at 1701 1st Avenue South.

muralDescription: |
A 25-by-12-foot mural on the south side of the FRSTeam by Rogers building at 1701 1st Ave S, facing the sidewalk. The piece depicts a whimsical picnic scene starring two cartoon characters named Leon and Olive, rendered in a flat, graphic illustration style using a limited palette of green, gold, red, dark navy, and white. Against a rich green grass background bordered by red trees, Leon reclines reading a book while Olive daydreams at a table with coffee and art supplies. A white text bubble in the upper left reads: "Meanwhile, in a city park, Leon was reading a library book of Latino poetry while Olive daydreamed about her next business venture. They both wondered whether to have another piece of cheese." Playful details fill the scene — a smiling log, a worm in a derby hat, bicycle wheels, a butterfly, and scattered food. The building number "1701" is visible in red at the lower right. The style recalls vintage children's book illustration with bold outlines, flat color fills, and a gentle sense of humor.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A 25-by-12-foot mural on the south side of the FRSTeam by Rogers building at 1701 1st Ave S, facing the sidewalk. Against a rich green grass background bordered by red trees, Leon reclines reading a book while Olive daydreams at a table with coffee and art supplies. ## Vitale Bros — Bait & Switch (2019)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 2010 1st Ave S
**Instagram:** @vitalebros

### Bio
Their first mural came from a sketch on a paper napkin, handed to a
restaurant owner. That was 1992.

Johnny Vitale and his brother Paul started Vitale Bros in Tampa Bay
hand-lettering signs and painting storefront windows. Their parents are
artists. So were their grandparents. No formal training — just family
tradition and thirty-plus years of practice. Their late aunt Nancy, who had
Down syndrome and attended Parc Center for Disabilities, turned out to be an
artist too, discovered in her final decade.

In the late 1990s they opened a warehouse studio that hosted group art,
music, and fashion shows that helped birth St. Pete's emerging art scene.
They collaborated on set design for Harmony Korine's Spring Breakers. They
have painted for the Tampa Bay Rays, the Buccaneers, L'Oreal, and dozens of
local businesses. They call themselves the "mural police" — restoring damaged
walls around the city for free.

In 2023, Johnny launched Overspray, a street art shop and graffiti school now
based in Gulfport, teaching tagging, block letters, Wildstyle, and advanced
mural technique to classes of up to twenty students. After Hurricane Milton,
they painted free signs for businesses that lost theirs.

They have painted SHINE every year since its inception — "The Cool Kids" in
2015, "Bait and Switch" in 2019, and multiple walls since. For SHINE Origins
2025, Johnny painted two solo murals: one on the 600 block where it all
began, and another at The Factory on Fairfield Ave South with his daughter
Sionna.

searchBio: |
Their first mural came from a sketch on a paper napkin, handed to a
restaurant owner. That was 1992.

Johnny Vitale and his brother Paul founded Vitale Bros in 1992 in Tampa Bay,
starting with hand-lettered signs and painted storefront windows. Youngest
brother Joey later joined. Their parents are artists — the family artistic
tradition goes back to their grandparents. No formal art education has been
documented; the craft appears entirely self-taught and family-transmitted.
Their late aunt Nancy had Down syndrome and attended Parc Center for
Disabilities, where staff discovered her artistic talent in her final decade
of life.

In the late 1990s and early 2000s, they opened a warehouse studio that hosted
legendary group art, music and fashion shows credited with helping birth St.
Pete's emerging art scene. They collaborated in set design for Harmony
Korine's Spring Breakers (filmed in the St. Pete area). Their style blends
pop art, graffiti, and traditional mural techniques — fine art meets pop art
sensibility, incorporating local culture, historical elements, and visual
stories reflecting community identity.

Clients include the Tampa Bay Rays, Tampa Bay Buccaneers, L'Oreal /
SalonCentric (national locations), Anchor Skate Supply, and Great Bay
Distributors, plus numerous local businesses and private residences. Notable
murals include the Marilyn Monroe on the side of the historic Playhouse
Theater on Central Ave, a Virgin Mary at St. Pete Catholic High School,
Power to the Patients giant fist at the Morean Arts Center, and the Nueva
Cantina restaurant mural in Tampa. They describe themselves as the mural
police — restoring damaged murals around the city for free.

The current Vitale Bros team includes Johnny, Paul, Nick and Jason Kekllas,
Bruce Statton, and Jake Gerow. They have also collaborated under the name
Up and Over with Tes One, and painted behind the State Theatre with Tes One
and Palehorse.

In July 2023, Johnny launched Overspray — a retail street art shop and
graffiti school, initially on the Pinellas Trail, relocated to 5013 20th Ave
S. in Gulfport. Overspray teaches graffiti, tagging, block and bubble
letters, Wildstyle, and advanced mural classes to groups of up to 20
students. The five-artist teaching team includes John Vitale, Nicole Salgar,
Hinson Thehealer, Digital (LA), and Jujmo. After Hurricane Milton, they
painted free signs for businesses that lost theirs. They host events like
Hot Walls during SHINE.

SHINE participation: The Cool Kids (2015, 2063 Central Ave — faces of the
artists' children), Bait and Switch (2019 — red snapper and sea turtle),
SHINE 2022, SHINE 2024 (mural at Anchor Skate Supply with pro skateboarder
Kris Markovich, inspired by Rays City Connect uniforms; limited-edition
skateboard L'Orange). For SHINE Origins 2025, Johnny painted two solo
walls: 600 1st Ave N (the 600 block where SHINE began) and The Factory at
2606 Fairfield Ave S (collaboration with Sionna Sio Macleish, celebrating
St. Pete history and landmarks).

Studio: 5013 20th Ave S., Gulfport, FL. Website: vitalebrothers.com.
Instagram: @johnvitale (solo), @vitalebros (collective).

muralDescription: |
A massive underwater scene spanning an entire warehouse wall. A giant orange octopus wraps its tentacles around shipwreck debris on the left, while sharks and a spotted eagle ray swim through turquoise water. A vintage deep-sea diver in a copper helmet explores the wreckage on the right. The text "Just Add Water! Voyage Under the Sea" floats across the center in retro lettering. Created in partnership with Sea Walls: Artists for Oceans and NOAA.

## Reda3sb — Feelings (2019)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Gulfport, FL
**Address:** 131 22nd St S
**Building:** Hell's Bells CrossFit
**Instagram:** @reda3sb

### Bio
He started painting graffiti at seventeen — "just for fun and as a hobby."
That was over a decade ago. He has been refining the same craft ever since.

Ricardo Delgado — Reda3sb — is a self-taught urban artist, illustrator, and
designer originally from Mexico, now based in Gulfport. He was already an
established muralist in Mexico before moving to the Tampa Bay area. His
style is hyper-realistic portraiture executed primarily in spray paint —
refining "the narrative quality of images through urban art and
illustration." He tries to leave a little of himself in every mural.

He was introduced to SHINE by the Spanish photorealist graffiti master
Belin, who painted the festival in 2018. Reda3sb and Belin had collaborated
years earlier in Mexico.

For SHINE 2019 he painted "Feelings" at Hell's Bells CrossFit on 22nd
Street South — hyper-realistic female faces and features arranged across
geometric pastel shapes in pink, mint green, and warm peach. The premise is
plain: "We laugh, we cry, we get angry, etcetera. We are all the same.
Regardless of skin color, socioeconomic status, or country of origin, we
all experience the same universal feelings."

searchBio: |
He started painting graffiti at seventeen — "just for fun and as a hobby."
That was over a decade ago. He has been refining the same craft ever since.

Ricardo Delgado — Reda3sb — is a self-taught urban artist, illustrator, and
designer originally from Mexico, now based in Gulfport. He was already an
established muralist in Mexico before moving to the Tampa Bay area. His
style is hyper-realistic portraiture executed primarily in spray paint —
refining "the narrative quality of images through urban art and
illustration." He tries to leave a little of himself in every mural.

He was introduced to SHINE by the Spanish photorealist graffiti master
Belin (Miguel Angel Belinchon Bujes), who painted the festival in 2018.
Reda3sb and Belin had collaborated years earlier in Mexico. Belin is
internationally known for photorealistic portrait murals that fuse realism
with cubist fragmentation — Reda3sb shares that hyper-real portrait
lineage.

For SHINE 2019 he painted "Feelings" at Hell's Bells CrossFit, 131 22nd St
S in St. Petersburg — hyper-realistic female faces and features arranged
across geometric pastel shapes in pink, mint green, and warm peach. The
premise is plain: "We laugh, we cry, we get angry, etcetera. We are all
the same. Regardless of skin color, socioeconomic status, or country of
origin, we all experience the same universal feelings." The piece functions
as a humanist statement about shared emotional experience.

He also runs Delgado's Painting LLC. Instagram: @reda3sb.

muralDescription: |
A panoramic composition of hyper-realistic female faces and features — eyes, lips, a full portrait — arranged across geometric pastel shapes in pink, mint green, and warm peach tones. A blue-toned face gazes out from within a dark inverted triangle at center, flanked by golden circles and spheres. The piece spans the full width of a low-slung commercial building, blending photorealism with abstract geometry.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A panoramic composition of hyper-realistic female faces and features — eyes, lips, a full portrait — arranged across geometric pastel shapes in pink, mint green, and warm peach tones. A blue-toned face gazes out from within a dark inverted triangle at center, flanked by golden circles and spheres. The piece spans the full width of a low-slung commercial building, blending photorealism with abstract geometry.

## Gibbs High School Mural Club — Raising Local Spirits

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 800 31st St S
**Building:** St. Petersburg Distillery
**Instagram:** @stpetedistillery

### Bio
"It's not kids doodling on walls. This is significant, professional work."

The Gibbs High School Mural Club is run by Brian McAllister, Department
Chair of Visual Arts at the Pinellas County Center for the Arts at Gibbs
High — the same magnet program that produced Derek Donnelly and Reid
Jenkins. McAllister earned his BFA in Illustration from Ringling,
freelanced across the US, taught mural painting in Saipan, and came back
to St. Pete to start the club. He donates his time. Donations and
commissions cover paint, lifts, and materials.

Thirty-six students. Two to three murals a year. Thirteen years running.
McAllister estimates comparable professional murals would cost $20,000
each. Notable projects include the Royal Theater (six feet by 133 feet,
with Boys & Girls Club members), the Alden Suites in St. Pete Beach for
the resort's 50th anniversary, and the "Fighting Chance" mural at the
Morean Arts Center commemorating COVID — designed with Leadership St.
Pete Class of 2020 to support the Fighting Chance Fund for affected
businesses.

Their St. Petersburg Distillery mural at 800 31st Street South is
"Raising Local Spirits" / "Voyage Under the Sea" — a sponge diver and a
giant octopus retrieving rum barrels from a sunken ship. It references
the site's rum-running history. Senior club president Anderson Bray, a
four-year member, painted with the octopus team. His take: "It is really
exciting to have it right across the street because I can tell my friends,
'Hey I did this.'"

McAllister's principle: "Once you're in the mural club, you're always in
the mural club."

searchBio: |
"It's not kids doodling on walls. This is significant, professional work."

The Gibbs High School Mural Club is run by Brian McAllister, Department
Chair of Visual Arts at the Pinellas County Center for the Arts (PCCA) at
Gibbs High School — the same magnet program that produced Derek Donnelly
and Reid Jenkins. PCCA was founded in 1984 as Pinellas County's arts
magnet high school. McAllister earned his BFA in Illustration from
Ringling College of Art and Design in Sarasota, freelanced across the US,
taught mural painting in Saipan in the Northern Mariana Islands, and came
back to St. Petersburg to start the club. He donates his time entirely.
Donations and commission revenue cover paint, lifts, and materials.

His own studio is JBM Studios. He works in oil, acrylic, mixed media,
digital painting, and printmaking. He is the official artist for the
Tampa Bay Rays / MLB featured print series. His personal mural projects
include "The Spirit of St. Pete" at Ulele, work at The Schu, the Gulf
Coast Alliance Skyway series, the Tichenor Orthopedic Clinic for Children
in Long Beach CA, and the Fred Held South Pasadena Habitat.

The Mural Club has thirty-six students and produces two to three murals a
year. It has been running for thirteen-plus years. McAllister: "Everything
we do, everything we receive, goes back into the club." And: "Not only are
they learning things they can use in the classroom and in their own
artwork — they are learning about community, learning how to work together
as a team, building relationships."

Notable club projects:
- "Raising Local Spirits" / "Voyage Under the Sea" at the St. Petersburg
  Distillery (800 31st St S) — a 10-day project depicting a sponge diver
  and a giant octopus retrieving rum barrels from a sunken ship,
  referencing the site's rum-running history. Partnership with Matt
  Risley, Director of Brands and Marketing at SPD.
- "Great Futures Start Here" — SHINE 2019, collaboration with Boys &
  Girls Club members.
- Royal Theater mural — historic Royal Theater, six feet tall by 133 feet
  long, with Boys & Girls Club participants.
- Alden Suites, St. Pete Beach — commemorating the resort's 50th
  anniversary (2019).
- "Fighting Chance" mural at the Morean Arts Center, 719 Central Ave —
  designed with Leadership St. Pete Class of 2020 to commemorate COVID-19.
  Features native Florida passion vine (community diversity), masked
  children — one reading with doves emerging from book pages, another
  with a camera documenting history — a pelican against the St. Pete
  skyline, a "+" symbol for healthcare workers, and the MLK quote "What
  are you doing for others?" Proceeds supported the Fighting Chance Fund
  for pandemic-affected businesses.
- Morean Arts Center mural.
- Gulf Coast Arts Alliance mural.
- Northeast Little League.

Senior club president Anderson "Andy" Bray, a four-year member, worked on
the Distillery octopus team. He said: "Usually when we paint, we paint on
a smaller canvas, but this is a huge wall, so we have to use our technique
and skills in different ways." McAllister's recurring line: "Once you're
in the mural club, you're always in the mural club."

Instagram: @gibbs_mural_club. McAllister's site: jbmstudios.com.

muralDescription: |
A massive underwater scene spanning the side of the distillery building along 31st Street. A giant orange octopus wraps its tentacles around the left side, its suckers curling around rum barrels from a sunken ship. Sharks and a spotted eagle ray glide through turquoise water around ghostly gray shipwreck masts and rigging. On the right, a vintage deep-sea diver in a copper helmet explores the wreckage. The title "Voyage Under the Sea" and "Just Add Water!" appear in retro lettering. The concept references St. Pete's rum-running history — sponge divers recovering barrels from sunken sailboats.

## Daniel Barojas — Tlaloc

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** Mexico
**Instagram:** @r3imaging

### Bio
Daniel Barojas is a Mexico-born muralist and visual artist whose work draws
heavily on Mesoamerican mythology and pre-Columbian iconography. His large-scale
pieces feature Aztec deities and symbols rendered in bold geometric patterns
and vibrant primary colors, bridging ancient cultural imagery with contemporary
street art. He works under the name R3 Imaging.

muralDescription: |
A wide mural on the upper half of a two-story commercial building depicting
Tlaloc, the Aztec god of fertility and rain. The central figure is a massive
green-faced deity with spiral eyes, fanged mouth, and a golden crown or
headdress, rendered in sharp geometric patterns. The face is framed by
outstretched wing-like forms filled with interlocking cube patterns in coral,
teal, and brown. Crashing ocean waves in deep blues and teals flank both
sides. A teal hexagonal cube pattern fills the background. Signed
"@R3Imaging" in the lower right. The palette is dominated by teal, coral,
green, gold, and deep blue.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A wide mural on the upper half of a two-story commercial building depicting
Tlaloc, the Aztec god of fertility and rain. The central figure is a massive
green-faced deity with spiral eyes, fanged mouth, and a golden crown or
headdress, rendered in sharp geometric patterns. The face is framed by
outstretched wing-like forms filled with interlocking cube patterns in coral,
teal, and brown. Crashing ocean waves in deep blues and teals flank both
sides. A teal hexagonal cube pattern fills the background. Signed
"@R3Imaging" in the lower right. The palette is dominated by teal, coral,
green, gold, and deep blue.

## Mikael B — Inner Visions (2017)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Copenhagen / Los Angeles
**Instagram:** @mikaelb

### Bio
He started painting graffiti at fifteen in Copenhagen under the name KETS.
He co-founded a design agency, moved to LA, and in 2014 his abstract world
map "Vibrant World" crashed his website with a million Facebook views.

Mikael Brandrup — Mikael B — was born in 1984 in Holbaek, Denmark, raised
on a peninsula west of Copenhagen. He has a non-identical twin brother
Morten who became his manager. He studied graphic and web design at
Copenhagen Technical School, then co-founded design agency Identity
Provided in 2008. He moved to Los Angeles in 2013 to pursue art
full-time.

His style fuses Wildstyle graffiti lettering with graphic design — sharp
geometric crystal forms colliding with fluid organic energy, referencing
everything from Baroque to Cubism through spray paint and acrylic. He
painted at Wynwood Walls in 2022 and his work has shown alongside Kaws,
Banksy, and Jeff Koons at Taglialatella Galleries. Clients include Nike,
American Express, Bentley, Google, and Mercedes-Benz. He founded the
Mikael B Foundation to build schools in Cambodia and is an ambassador for
Red Cross Denmark.

For SHINE 2017 he painted "Inner Visions" — sharp crystalline bursts in
purple, white, and orange emerging from a turbulent blue sea. "I want to
keep dreams alive."

searchBio: |
He started painting graffiti at fifteen in Copenhagen under the name KETS.
He co-founded a design agency, moved to LA, and in 2014 his abstract world
map "Vibrant World" crashed his website with a million Facebook views.

Mikael Brandrup — Mikael B — was born June 5, 1984 in Holbaek, Denmark,
raised on Tuse Naes west of Copenhagen. Non-identical twin brother Morten
Brandrup became his manager. He started graffiti at age 15 (2000) under
the name KETS in Copenhagen's Sydhavnen scene, immersed in hip-hop culture.
He attended Copenhagen Technical School for graphic and web design, left
his internship early to pursue entrepreneurship. In 2008 he co-founded
design agency Identity Provided with Morten and Mickie Storm (clients:
Steen & Stroem, Chiquita). He moved to Los Angeles in 2013; the agency
merged with MeyerBukdahl in 2014. His abstract world map "Vibrant World"
(2014) went viral with approximately one million Facebook views.

His style fuses Wildstyle graffiti lettering with graphic design sensibility
— references from Baroque to Cubism expressed through spray paint and
acrylic. In 2017 he shifted toward pure abstraction and gestural painting.
He paints multiple pieces simultaneously and uses four-meter ladders to
view canvases from above. He describes himself as a painter, muralist,
sculptor, print-master, and designer.

Notable murals: "Vivid Rhythms" (2018, ArtShare Building, Downtown LA,
9,300 sq ft — Certificate of Recognition from City of LA), "Everything
Starts With Nothing" (2022, Wynwood Walls), "Room for Reflection" (2019,
Artscape Festival, Sweden), "Moment of Clarity" (2021, West Hollywood),
and Google headquarters in Santa Monica (with Bumblebee). Solo exhibitions
(most sold out): "Just Be" (Copenhagen, 2016), "Reality Shift" (Gregorio
Escalante Gallery, LA, 2017), "Never Tomorrow" (Copenhagen, 2018), "Flow
State" (Maddox Gallery, West Hollywood, 2021), "The Evolution of
Everything" (Copenhagen, 2022). Represented by Taglialatella Galleries
(New York, Paris, Toronto, Palm Beach) — work shown alongside Kaws,
Banksy, Jeff Koons, and Damien Hirst.

Clients include Nike/Jordan Brand, American Express, Bentley, Google,
LinkedIn, Heineken, Mercedes-Benz, Chase, The Black Eyed Peas, and Topps
(Project 70 baseball cards). He founded the Mikael B Foundation (2017)
to build schools in Cambodia and is an ambassador for Red Cross Denmark.
Published monographs: "Infinite Universes" (2016) and "Never Tomorrow"
(2018). Featured in LA Times, Hi-Fructose, and Cultured Magazine.

For SHINE 2017 he painted "Inner Visions" at 1123 1st Avenue North in
St. Petersburg — spiky triangular bursts of purple, white, and orange
with gold-tipped points emerging from a turbulent blue sea. Quote: "With
Inner Visions I want to keep dreams alive."

Instagram: @mikaelb. Website: mikael-b.com.

muralDescription: |
A long horizontal mural on a dark-toned wall in the Edge District. The
composition features a dynamic collision of sharp, faceted geometric
crystal forms — angular star shapes and prismatic shards in icy whites,
pale blues, warm corals, and deep burgundy — interwoven with sweeping
brushstroke-like curves in dark olive, black, and pink. Misty teal and
aqua clouds billow along the bottom edge, and paint splatters dot the
surface. The overall effect suggests crystals erupting from a turbulent
atmospheric field. The palette moves between cool icy blues on the left
and warm sunset pinks and corals on the right. "PRIVATE PROPERTY" signs
are mounted on the wall surface.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description

## Kosharekart (2020)

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Building:** Coastal Creative
**Instagram:** @kosharekart

### Bio
She spent thirteen years as a stay-at-home mom with four kids before
becoming a full-time artist. Her work is in the Smithsonian.

Jennifer Kosharek — Kosharekart — is a formally trained artist who studied
at the Pinellas County Center for the Arts and holds a BA in English. She
lived in St. Petersburg for over forty years before relocating to Alaska in
2022 to pursue a lifelong dream. She started as a realistic oil portrait
painter, then gave herself "permission to paint unrealistically."

Her signature character Gretchen — a big-eyed whimsical female figure —
was born around 2008, described as "a type of Vivian Girl who fights for
the lives and rights of children and women." She learned spray painting
after meeting street artist Narchy in Calgary and was described as "the
only female in St. Pete doing spray paint street art" at the time. She
owned eve-N-odd Gallery in St. Petersburg from 2011 to 2014, hosting
thirty-plus shows.

Her work is held in the Smithsonian and MOMA collections in New York,
Wales, and Brazil through the mail art community. In December 2024 she
spray-painted "Hope" and her Gretchen character on a beached sailboat at
North Shore Park after Hurricane Helene.

searchBio: |
She spent thirteen years as a stay-at-home mom with four kids before
becoming a full-time artist. Her work is in the Smithsonian.

Jennifer Kosharek — Kosharekart — studied at the Pinellas County Center
for the Arts and holds a BA in English. She is trained in realistic oil
portraiture, intaglio printmaking, and various other media. She lived in
St. Petersburg for 40-plus years before relocating to Alaska in early 2022.
She is a multi-disciplinary artist: painter, spray paint muralist, rag doll
maker, nesting doll painter, quilt maker, illustrator, mail artist, Fluxus
performance artist, fiber artist, and graphic designer. Mother of four.

Her signature character "Gretchen" (born circa 2008) is a big-eyed
whimsical female figure — "a type of Vivian Girl who fights for the lives
and rights of children and women." She writes hidden words, poems, and
sayings onto canvases before covering them with her final creation. She
learned spray painting after meeting street artist Narchy in Calgary, AB.
She was described as "the only female in St. Pete doing spray paint street
art" at that time and helped start the city's mural scene. Her first spray
paint mural was "Gretchen and Spoonman" with Christian Thomas.

She owned and operated eve-N-odd Gallery in St. Petersburg (January 2011 to
January 2014), hosting 30-plus shows. She held a solo exhibition "Woman's
Work" at the Morean Arts Center (October 2020) as part of a SHINE prequel
pop-up. In August 2022 she completed a residency at the Wrangell Mountains
Center in McCarthy, Alaska. In December 2024 she spray-painted "Hope" on a
beached sailboat at North Shore Park after Hurricane Helene (covered by FOX
13). She published "Kosharek Art: Whimsical Art for the Masses" via Blurb.

Her work is held in the Smithsonian (via mail art historian John Held Jr.'s
archive) and MOMA collections in New York, Wales, and Brazil. She has
participated in Free Art Friday for 10-plus years and The Sketchbook
Project.

Instagram: @kosharekart. Website: kosharek.art.

muralDescription: |
A large black-and-white mural spanning the side wall of the Coastal Creative
building. Two stylized female figures anchor the composition — one on the left
with flowing ribbon-like hair and a potted plant atop her head, the other on
the right wearing a braided hairstyle topped with a cactus and leafy branches.
Between them, a toucan perches with its curved beak reaching upward. The
figures are surrounded by densely packed flowers, leaves, birds, and organic
swirling patterns, all rendered in bold black line work against the white
wall. A small "20" date mark is visible near the left figure. The mural wraps
around a centered garage door.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A large black-and-white mural spanning the side wall of the Coastal Creative
building. Two stylized female figures anchor the composition — one on the left
with flowing ribbon-like hair and a potted plant atop her head, the other on
the right wearing a braided hairstyle topped with a cactus and leafy branches.
Between them, a toucan perches with its curved beak reaching upward. The
figures are surrounded by densely packed flowers, leaves, birds, and organic
swirling patterns, all rendered in bold black line work against the white
wall. A small "20" date mark is visible near the left figure. The mural wraps
around a centered garage door.

## Carla Sá Fernandes (2016)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Portugal
**Instagram:** @carlasafernandes

### Bio
She signs her walls "Caratoes." The faces she paints look like they are
dreaming themselves into existence.

Carla Sá Fernandes is a Portuguese artist whose large-scale murals feature
surrealist portraiture in monochromatic palettes — faces dissolving into
flowing organic forms, Art Nouveau curves, and architectural geometry. She
works primarily in grayscale with occasional soft accent colors.

For SHINE 2016, she painted in St. Petersburg.

muralDescription: |
A wide monochromatic mural spanning a concrete block wall. Two surrealist
female faces emerge from flowing organic forms — the larger face on the
right gazes forward with one visible eye, features dissolving into waves,
leaves, and curving lines. The smaller face on the left peers out from
behind swirling abstract shapes. Soft pink circles and a pale pink
swooping curve on the left provide the only color against the otherwise
grayscale palette of grays, whites, and blacks. Geometric elements —
bridge-like structures, dashed road lines — weave through the organic
forms. Signed "CARATOES" in the lower left. The wall shows weathering
and graffiti tags near the door.

## Carrie Jadus — Awakening Bodhisattva

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Building:** Dog Bar
**Instagram:** @jadusfineart

### Bio
She has a degree in electrical engineering. She worked as a
radio-frequency design engineer. Then she quit to paint — as a single
mother.

Carrie Jadus was born in 1976 in Tampa and moved to St. Petersburg at
five. She graduated from the Pinellas County Center for the Arts at Gibbs
High School, traveled to Europe, fell for Impressionism, and then —
detour — earned a BS in Electrical Engineering from USF and designed RF
circuits for several years. She creates 200 to 300 pieces annually,
working primarily in oils at every scale from notecards to fifty-foot
walls.

Her SHINE 2015 mural "Resonance" is a portrait of Nikola Tesla on 22nd
Street South. She also painted "Little Miss Sisyphus" — a girl pushing
an enormous reflective sphere up a mountain — around the corner from the
Tesla. For Grand Central Brewhouse, she painted "Last Call" — six musical
icons chosen because "they're all extremely talented and socially
conscious." She finished the fifty-by-thirty-foot piece in three weeks.

"Awakening Bodhisattva" at Dog Bar is a memorial to Bodhi, the dog of
artist Marianne Wysocki. The title is a double entendre — the dog waking
up, and the Buddhist concept of a being who delays nirvana out of
compassion.

searchBio: |
She has a degree in electrical engineering. She worked as a
radio-frequency design engineer. Then she quit to paint — as a single
mother.

Carrie Jadus was born in 1976 in Tampa, FL. Her family moved to St.
Petersburg when she was five. She comes from an artistic lineage — family
populated with creatives. She attended the Pinellas County Center for the
Arts magnet program at Gibbs High School, training across multiple
mediums. A post-graduation trip to Europe sparked a fascination with
Impressionism. She then earned a BS in Electrical Engineering from the
University of South Florida and worked as a radio-frequency design
engineer for several years before quitting to pursue art full-time as a
single mother.

She works primarily in oils, comfortable at every scale from notecards to
fifty-foot murals. She occupies "the rich and fertile ground between
realism and impressionism" with a "distinctive, soft and subtly
subversive style." Key influences: Winslow Homer (storytelling) and Egon
Schiele (the human figure). She creates 200-300 pieces annually. She has
expanded into large-scale sculpture through collaboration with MGA
Sculpture Studio. Licensed as a professional artist since 2016, she uses
"the business plan of a Toymaker as a model" — multiple product lines to
sustain income.

Her SHINE 2015 murals include "Resonance" — a portrait of Nikola Tesla
at 22nd St S and 5th Ave S, painted with assistant Tara Radosevich over
two weeks — and "Little Miss Sisyphus," a girl pushing an enormous
reflective sphere up a mountain. At Grand Central Brewhouse she painted
"Last Call" (2020), a 50x30 ft mural of six musical icons: Bob Marley,
Bono, Bruce Springsteen, Lady Gaga, Eddie Vedder, and John Legend —
chosen because "they're all extremely talented and socially conscious."
Heads roughly 8 ft tall, completed in three weeks, commissioned by Kevin
Milkey. "Awakening Bodhisattva" at Dog Bar (2020) is a memorial to
Bodhi, dog of artist Marianne Wysocki — a double entendre: the dog
waking up, and the Buddhist concept of a being who delays nirvana out of
compassion.

She maintains a studio at Soft Water Gallery in the Warehouse Arts
District. She illustrates for St. Pete Preservation and designed a Kahwa
Coffee Art Canister (4th installment) benefiting the St. Petersburg Arts
Endowment. Solo series include "Blue Nudes," "Imaginary Friends," and
"Grand Portraits" (Einstein, Nina Simone). Instagram: @jadusfineart.
Website: carriejadus.com.

muralDescription: |
A photorealistic memorial mural covering the side of Dog Bar on Central
Avenue. A large dog — Bodhi Kaiser — lies sleeping against a deep blue
starry sky, paws curled, eyes closed. The rendering is detailed and
luminous, the fur and shadows painted with gallery-level realism. Text at
the bottom reads "Awakening Bodhisattva" with "Bodhi Kaiser 2013-2023"
and the artist's signature.

muralInspiration: "Memorial to Bodhi Kaiser (2013-2023)."

### Mural Description

muralDescription: |
A photorealistic mural on the side of Grand Central Brewhouse depicting
six iconic musicians and performers gathered together as if at a bar,
each holding a glass of beer or wine. From left to right: a figure
resembling Bob Marley in a white jacket, Bono in sunglasses, Keith
Richards in a headband leaning in conspiratorially, Lady Gaga in blonde
hair holding a beer, Bruce Springsteen in a casual shirt, and John Legend
in profile. The background is a deep blue with circular bokeh lights.
"Grand Central" signage is visible at the bottom center.

## Jade Rivera — El Templo - Find the Light (2016)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Lima, Peru
**Address:** 2536 Central Ave
**Instagram:** @jade_rivera_artist

### Bio
He has his own museum, gallery, clothing line, and creative lab on one
street in Lima's Barranco district.

Jonatan "Jade" Rivera is a self-taught Peruvian muralist born in 1983 in
Junin, raised in Lima's Chorrillos district. He first encountered graffiti
at fourteen and has been painting walls since 1998. A visit to the Louvre
helped shape his ambitions, but he never finished formal art school — he
defines himself as self-taught since 1997.

His signature is large-format surrealist photorealism: human figures in
dreamlike stillness surrounded by disproportionately large birds,
butterflies, and candle flames. Birds recur as "messengers between worlds."
The work explores Latin identity, faith, and the importance of believing in
one's own dreams. Beyond murals he works in oil, watercolor, and wood
carving.

He has painted in seventeen-plus countries across South America, North
America, Europe, and the Middle East. Festival credits include Forest for
the Trees (Portland), Constructo (Mexico), and Mirafau (Lima). In 2024 he
painted "La Florista" in Rahway, New Jersey — his daughter Emilia holding
lilies.

For SHINE 2016 he painted "El Templo — Find the Light" at 2536 Central
Avenue — a boy lying across the full width of the wall, a blue ibis
standing on his belly, a monarch butterfly at his neck, and a single candle
flame burning in the darkness.

searchBio: |
He has his own museum, gallery, clothing line, and creative lab on one
street in Lima's Barranco district.

Jonatan "Jade" Rivera is a self-taught Peruvian street artist and muralist
born in 1983 in Junin (Huancayo), Peru. At seven months old he and his
mother moved to Lima, where he grew up in the Chorrillos district. His
interest in art began around age eleven; he first encountered graffiti at
fourteen. At seventeen he enrolled at the National Pre-School of Fine Arts
(Escuela Nacional de Bellas Artes) but gave up applying at the last moment.
He defines himself as self-taught since 1997 and has been making murals
since 1998. A visit to the Louvre Museum in Paris was a significant
formative influence.

His signature is large-format surrealist photorealism: human figures in
dreamlike states surrounded by disproportionately large birds, butterflies,
insects, and candle flames. Birds recur as "messengers between worlds." His
work explores Latin identity, environment, faith originating from oneself,
and believing in one's dreams. His murals are "reflections on life and its
constant eventfulness, portrayed with optimism and wonder." Beyond murals
he works in oil painting, watercolor, and wood carving.

He has painted in seventeen-plus countries: Peru, Colombia, Mexico, Chile,
Argentina, Ecuador, Paraguay, Dominican Republic, Costa Rica, the United
States, Denmark, France, Sweden, Germany, Switzerland, Spain, Italy,
Slovenia, Poland, and the United Arab Emirates. Festival credits include
SHINE 2016 (St. Petersburg), Forest for the Trees (Portland, Oregon),
Constructo (Mexico), Mirafau Festival (Lima), and Barrio Arte (Santiago,
Chile).

Notable murals include "La Florista" in Rahway, New Jersey (November 2024,
a collaboration between Peruvian restaurant QUILLA and the city, depicting
his daughter Emilia holding lilies), "Buscando la verdad" in Santiago de
Chile (2018), and "El Abrazo De Un Deseo."

In Lima's Barranco district he operates Jade Rivera World on Bajada de
Banos street, which includes: Museo Jade Rivera (a museum of his work),
Jade Rivera Lab (workshops and creative encounters), Jade Rivera Gallery
(original pieces, sculptures, prints), and Despierta (his clothing line).
He is represented by Quai 36 (French street art production company) and
listed with the South American Street Art Fund (SASA Fund). He has a
daughter named Emilia.

For SHINE 2016 he painted "El Templo — Find the Light" at 2536 Central
Avenue in St. Petersburg — forty-five feet wide by thirteen feet high. A
boy lies on his back across the full length of the wall against a deep
blue-black background, a blue ibis standing on his belly in a pool of
water, a monarch butterfly at his neck, and a single candle flame burning.

Instagram: @jade_rivera_artist. Website: jaderivera.pe.

muralDescription: |
A surrealist photorealistic mural on a deep blue-black background. A young
man lies on his back with eyes closed, wearing a light-colored shirt. A
monarch butterfly with orange and black wings rests near his neck, and a
blue ibis stands on his torso, bending its long neck downward into a small
pool of water. A single candle flame burns between the boy and the bird.
The composition spans the full width of the wall — forty-five feet of
dreamlike stillness, nature and the human figure merged in silence.

## Sebastian Coolidge & Rif Raf Giraffe (2018)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL / Kansas City, MO
**Instagram:** @sebastiancoolidge

### Bio
Coolidge dropped out of St. Petersburg College his first semester. His
first two years as an artist he dumpster-dived for paint and bartered
murals for food.

Sebastian Coolidge moved from Independence, Missouri to St. Petersburg
around 2008 and biked downtown nightly to paint, carrying supplies on his
back. His 2012 mural at Freshly Squeezed on MLK led to steady commissions.
His style blends classic animation and Lowbrow art — dark and surreal one
wall, light and whimsical the next. He co-painted "A Moment to Reflect"
with Derek Donnelly on the Florida CraftArt building — four stories tall,
finished in ten days, the largest mural in St. Petersburg at the time. He
also runs the clothing brand SC1912.

Jason Harrington — Rif Raf Giraffe — grew up in Kansas City, studied
Painting and Film at the School of the Art Institute of Chicago, and
painted graffiti in Chicago starting in 2002. He returned to KC in 2013
and co-founded the SpraySeeMO mural festival with his wife Ami in 2017 —
the festival has produced roughly a hundred and fifty murals in Kansas City
and expanded to Miami and Los Angeles. His work focuses on futurism and
"future pop" — characters reflecting societal behaviors, ranging from
comedic to political. Clients include Tiffany & Co. and Giphy Arts.

For SHINE 2018 they collaborated on this mural in St. Petersburg. The two
share Kansas City roots and have worked together repeatedly — Wynwood in
2015 (with Oleson Art and Zulu Painter), SpraySeeMO 2018, and KC's
Crossroads district in 2021.

searchBio: |
Coolidge dropped out of St. Petersburg College his first semester. His
first two years as an artist he dumpster-dived for paint and bartered
murals for food.

Sebastian Coolidge (born approximately 1989) moved from Independence,
Missouri to St. Petersburg, Florida around 2008 because his aunt lived
there and he wanted a change of scenery. He enrolled at St. Petersburg
College but dropped out first semester to pursue art full-time. His first
two years were financially difficult — he and friend Elijah Barrett sourced
discarded paint from dumpsters and bartered mural work for food, money, or
clothing. He worked at Vans Store, Starbucks, and a smoke shop
simultaneously while biking downtown nightly to create live paintings.

His 2012 mural at Freshly Squeezed clothing store (MLK & 1st Ave N)
depicting "a kid in a newsboy cap with long outstretched arms squeezing an
orange" led to steady commissions. He has painted approximately forty to
fifty exterior murals. His style blends classic animation and Lowbrow art,
ranging from dark and surreal to light and whimsical. He works in pencils,
pens, spray paint, and oil paint with a spontaneous approach — sometimes no
sketches. Notable murals include "A Moment to Reflect" on the Florida
CraftArt building (co-painted with Derek Donnelly, four stories tall,
completed in ten days, the largest mural in St. Petersburg at the time),
"The Bends" (representing "the irrelevance of time"), and a giant face on
an industrial silo near Woodlawn Park visible from I-275. He painted
"Cherish Yourself" for SpraySeeMO 2024 in Kansas City. He runs the SC1912
clothing brand and participated in the Carmada art car movement. Featured
in Hi-Fructose and Huffington Post.

Jason Harrington — Rif Raf Giraffe — is a Lebanese-Irish American artist
born around 1980 in Kansas City, Missouri. He attended Johnson County
Community College before transferring to the School of the Art Institute of
Chicago (SAIC), where he studied Painting and Film. He lived in Chicago for
a decade, started painting graffiti in 2002, and returned to Kansas City in
2013. He became a full-time artist in 2016. The name came from National
Geographic footage of a giraffe being hunted, then finding a toy giraffe
on his walk home — he added "Rif Raf" because he saw himself as
"working-class trouble."

His work focuses on futurism and "future pop" — characters reflecting
societal behaviors, messages ranging from comedic to political commentary.
He works across mural painting, canvas, digital illustration, animated
GIFs, and film. Clients include Tiffany & Co., Giphy Arts, Emporium at
Area 15 (Las Vegas), and MoDOT. He co-founded SpraySeeMO mural festival
with wife Ami Ayars in 2017 (originally called SolaNoir) — approximately
150 murals in Kansas City, expanded to Miami and Los Angeles. They co-run
Upper Level Gallery in Kansas City. He has painted in London, Barcelona,
Mexico, and Hawaii.

For SHINE 2018 they collaborated on a mural in St. Petersburg. The two
share Kansas City roots and have worked together repeatedly: Wynwood, Miami
(2015, with Oleson Art and Zulu Painter), SpraySeeMO 2018 (514 Southwest
Blvd, KC), American Shaman CBD (2018, with Jake Merten), KC Crossroads
(2021, with Ghost Beard), and SpraySeeMO 2020 at Royal Liquor.

Instagram: @sebastiancoolidge, @rifrafgiraffe. Websites: sc1912.com,
rifrafgiraffe.com.

muralDescription: |
Two surreal, stylized heads side by side against a dark gray background.
On the left, a drooping flesh-toned face with melting features — heavy
lidded eyes, elongated nose, sagging mouth — rendered in pale pinks and
creams. On the right, a robot-like head with fiery orange hair, piano
keys for teeth, three teal circular eyes, and a small mouse or creature
piloting from inside the open skull. The robot wears a mint-green
collared shirt. The style blends lowbrow illustration with surrealist
character design.

## Ashley Cassens — Sweet Disposition (2025)

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** Tampa, FL
**Address:** 2705 54th Ave N
**Building:** 54th Ave Deli Mart
**Instagram:** @ashley.cassens

### Bio
Two of her oil paintings ended up at HomeGoods. She has sixty-two murals
under her belt and a five-star rating across forty reviews on Thumbtack.

Ashley Cassens is a Tampa-based representational figure painter and
muralist. She grew up in Daytona Beach, earned a BFA from Florida Southern
College in 2006, and an MFA from Florida Atlantic University in 2017. She
taught K-12 art for seven years before going full-time as an artist around
2020.

Her studio work is intimate figurative portraiture — layered, chromatic oil
paint on canvas and wood panels, often focused on specific body parts and
details of people she knows. Her "Eye Traps" series celebrates women
through traditional oil painting, patterned fabrics, and fresh color. Her
"Some Place Under Neith" series responds to fundamentalist cult pamphlets,
addressing women's repression through tiny portrait paintings.

"Sweet Disposition" wraps the side of 54th Ave Deli Mart in the Lealman
area — bold botanical imagery flowing from orange blossoms through a
bumblebee in flight to ripe strawberries, commissioned by Creative Pinellas
in partnership with the Lealman CRA. She installed it in May 2025.

searchBio: |
Two of her oil paintings ended up at HomeGoods. She has sixty-two murals
under her belt and a five-star rating across forty reviews on Thumbtack.

Ashley Cassens is a Tampa-based representational figure painter and
muralist, native Floridian from Daytona Beach. She earned a BFA from
Florida Southern College (2006) focused on representational work and
traditional oil painting methods, then an MFA from Florida Atlantic
University (2017), where she received a Graduate Teaching Assistantship,
Provost Fellowship, two Graduate Advisory Board Grants, the Friedland
Grant, Rothenberger Scholarship, Ambassador of the Arts Scholarship, and
the Women in the Visual Arts Scholarship (twice).

She taught K-12 art for seven years and was a teaching assistant at FAU
before becoming a full-time artist around 2020. She runs Painting
Possibilities LLC in Tampa's Northdale area. She is certified in scissor
and boom lift safety, insured, and background checked. She charges $25-30
per square foot for murals with a $1,500 minimum.

Her studio work is intimate figurative portraiture in oil — layered,
chromatic paint on canvas and wood panels, often focused on body parts and
details of loved ones. Notable series include "Eye Traps" (celebrating
women through oil painting and patterned fabrics; two pieces licensed to
HomeGoods; shown at Elevation Coffee Roasters, Tampa), "Projection Series"
(vibrant portraits exploring experiences filtered through screens), and
"Some Place Under Neith" (tiny portraits responding to IBLP cult pamphlets,
addressing women's repression).

Commercial clients include HomeGoods (licensing), Ann Cox Design, Cori Rose
Handmade, Creative Pinellas, and various schools, non-profits, national
corporations, and private residences. She has been featured on Fox 13 Tampa
Bay (January 2023), Creative Loafing Tampa, CanvasRebel Magazine, Artsy
Shark, and Daytime TV on News Channel 8. Thumbtack Top Pro 2022-2025.

"Sweet Disposition" at 2705 54th Ave N, St. Petersburg (54th Ave Deli Mart)
was commissioned by Creative Pinellas in partnership with the Lealman
Community Redevelopment Area, installed May 2025. The mural wraps the side
of the building in vivid oranges, blues, greens, and reds — botanical
imagery blending orange blossoms, a bumblebee, and strawberries in a
contemporary tropical palette. It took approximately two weeks to complete.

Instagram: @ashley.cassens. Website: ashleycassens.com.

muralDescription: |
A wide panoramic mural wrapping the side of a commercial building.
The composition flows left to right through bold botanical imagery:
orange blossoms with white petals and deep green leaves against a royal
blue background on the left, transitioning to a large bumblebee in
flight over teal-green leaf patterns at center, then into ripe red
strawberries with white flowers against warm gold and green tones on the
right. The palette is vivid — deep blues, rich greens, warm golds, and
bright reds. "Ashley Cassens" is signed at the far left. The style
blends botanical realism with graphic design sensibility.

muralInspiration: "Commissioned by Creative Pinellas in partnership with the Lealman CRA to enhance community visibility."

## Justin Bass

**Category:** commercial
**Building:** Tru
**Instagram:** @dropthelove

### Bio
In this mural a man rides a giant pelican carrying a boombox through the
sky. A flamingo flies alongside. That's the vibe.

Justin Bass — DropTheLove — is an artist, designer, and restoration
specialist who has collaborated with Miami scenic artist Ryan Zook on
projects around South Florida. This piece wraps a parking garage wall at
the Tru by Hilton on Central Avenue in a surreal pastel aviation scene —
teal sky, puffy clouds, and Florida birds doing things Florida birds don't
normally do.

searchBio: |
Justin Bass works as an artist, designer, and restoration specialist under
the brand DropTheLove. His LinkedIn title is "Artist / Designer /
Restorations at DropTheLove." He has collaborated with Miami-based scenic
artist and designer Ryan Zook (@zookart, ryanzookart.com) — the pair were
described as "the dynamic duo" by Project In The Paint. He also goes by
"Jb Jb" on Instagram and "jmovement" on Facebook and Flickr.

His mural on the Tru by Hilton parking garage at Central Avenue in St.
Petersburg is an exterior piece distinct from the hotel's interior lobby
art by Mike Traina. The piece is a surreal aviation scene on a teal-green
background — a pink flamingo in flight, a figure riding a giant pelican
carrying a boombox, and a diving pelican among puffy clouds and geometric
shapes.

Instagram: @dropthelove. TikTok: @justinbass511.

muralDescription: |
A wide mural along a parking garage wall on a teal-green background. A
surreal aviation scene features a pink flamingo in flight on the left, a
figure riding atop a giant pelican carrying a boombox through the sky at
center, and a winged pelican diving from the upper right. Puffy blue-gray
clouds and small triangular shapes fill the sky. The style is playful and
illustrative with a pastel palette of teal, pink, lavender, and gray.

searchBio: |

privately commissioned commercial mural with no public attribution found.
It does not appear in SHINE/SPAA records, FindMASA, PixelStix, Visit St
Pete Clearwater, Creative Pinellas, or the city's public art collection.
Note: this building is near but distinct from the Imagine Museum at 1901
Central Ave (which has the Pantonio fish mural on different walls).

muralDescription: |
A monochromatic photorealistic mural covering the side wall of the
Imagine Museum on Central Avenue. A steam locomotive — numbered 611 —
appears to crash through the building wall, bursting through bricks and
debris in dramatic forced perspective. The entire piece is rendered in
shades of gray, black, and white with extraordinary detail — the engine's
headlight, steam valves, and wheel mechanisms are precisely depicted.
Shattered building facade fragments, scattered bricks, and architectural
elements frame the locomotive. The building itself has bright blue trim
along the base, contrasting with the monochrome mural above.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A monochromatic photorealistic mural covering the side wall of the
Imagine Museum on Central Avenue. A steam locomotive — numbered 611 —
appears to crash through the building wall, bursting through bricks and
debris in dramatic forced perspective. The entire piece is rendered in
shades of gray, black, and white with extraordinary detail — the engine's
headlight, steam valves, and wheel mechanisms are precisely depicted.
Shattered building facade fragments, scattered bricks, and architectural
elements frame the locomotive. The building itself has bright blue trim
along the base, contrasting with the monochrome mural above.

## Vitale Bros — Ride 'em Cowboy

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Building:** Ride 'em Cowboy bar
**Instagram:** @vitalebros

### Bio
Their first mural came from a sketch on a paper napkin, handed to a
restaurant owner. That was 1992.

Johnny Vitale and his brother Paul started Vitale Bros in Tampa Bay
hand-lettering signs and painting storefront windows. Their parents are
artists. So were their grandparents. No formal training — just family
tradition and thirty-plus years of practice. Their late aunt Nancy, who had
Down syndrome and attended Parc Center for Disabilities, turned out to be an
artist too, discovered in her final decade.

In the late 1990s they opened a warehouse studio that hosted group art,
music, and fashion shows that helped birth St. Pete's emerging art scene.
They collaborated on set design for Harmony Korine's Spring Breakers. They
have painted for the Tampa Bay Rays, the Buccaneers, L'Oreal, and dozens of
local businesses. They call themselves the "mural police" — restoring damaged
walls around the city for free.

In 2023, Johnny launched Overspray, a street art shop and graffiti school now
based in Gulfport, teaching tagging, block letters, Wildstyle, and advanced
mural technique to classes of up to twenty students. After Hurricane Milton,
they painted free signs for businesses that lost theirs.

They have painted SHINE every year since its inception — "The Cool Kids" in
2015, "Bait and Switch" in 2019, and multiple walls since. For SHINE Origins
2025, Johnny painted two solo murals: one on the 600 block where it all
began, and another at The Factory on Fairfield Ave South with his daughter
Sionna.

searchBio: |
See Vitale Bros entries (ids 11, 12, 38, 73, 142) for full search bio.

muralDescription: |
A vibrant retro-style mural framed by a painted trompe-l'oeil wooden
frame with visible grain and nail heads. On the left, a pin-up cowgirl
with brown curls, a red cowboy hat, and a studded red bustier smiles
confidently. On the right, a cowboy in blue jeans and hat swings a lasso
while riding a green alligator through a tropical Florida landscape —
palm trees, hibiscus flowers, a sandy beach, and a warm sunset sky. "Ride
'em Cowboy" is lettered in bold cursive across the top center. The Vitale
Bros signature is visible at the bottom left. The whole piece has a
classic Americana postcard feel with a distinctly Florida twist.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A vibrant retro-style mural framed by a painted trompe-l'oeil wooden

## PlasticBirdie & Blaine Fontana — The 8th Funky Fish Town (2019)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Portland, OR
**Address:** 756 28th St S
**Building:** Bama Sea Products
**Instagram:** @plasticbirdie

### Bio
Their SHINE wall was the first public art project in the country funded by
NOAA. It took over four hundred cans of spray paint and eight days.

Jeremy Nichols — PlasticBirdie — was born in Tokyo in 1982, grew up in
suburban Columbus, Ohio, and earned a BFA in Printmaking from Ohio State.
Blaine Fontana was born in Seattle, raised on Bainbridge Island, and earned
a BFA in Communication Art/Design from Otis College. Both are based in
Portland, Oregon.

Nichols paints the tension between urban growth and nature — layered
graphic environments that feel familiar and not quite right at the same
time. His clients include the Port of Portland (a mural in the PDX Airport
pedestrian tunnel), Oregon Zoo, and Gerber Gear. Fontana works across
murals, sculpture, fine art, and product design, drawing on Asian
calligraphy, found objects, and graffiti palettes. His clients include Nike,
adidas, Patagonia, TEDx, and VANS.

For SHINE 2019 they collaborated on "The 8th Funky Fish Town" at Bama Sea
Products on 28th Street South — a hundred-spray-paint mural spanning
roughly twenty-five by a hundred and seventy-five feet along the Pinellas
Trail, partnered with PangeaSeed Foundation's Sea Walls program and funded
by NOAA. The piece highlights Florida's small fishery towns and ocean
fragility, inspired by classic school-book infographics. It was the
largest SHINE mural of 2019.

searchBio: |
Their SHINE wall was the first public art project in the country funded by
NOAA. It took over four hundred cans of spray paint and eight days.

Jeremy Nichols — PlasticBirdie — was born in 1982 in Tokyo, Japan, grew up
in the suburbs of Columbus, Ohio, and traveled regularly between upstate
New York and Tokyo as a child. He earned a BFA in Printmaking from Ohio
State University, studying under Charles Massey, Jr. and Philip von Rabbe.
He moved to Portland, Oregon around 2009-2010. His work focuses on "the
energy, movement, balance, and harmony of the chaos around us" — layered
graphic design and nature elements exploring urban growth versus preserving
nature, motivated by childhood displacement across cultures. He has been
featured in Hi-Fructose magazine. Notable murals include "A Place We Call
Home" in the PDX Airport North Pedestrian Tunnel (with Alex Chui, via
Portland Street Art Alliance and Port of Portland), "Hello Bear!" at
Groundwork Coffee in Portland, a mural celebrating historically significant
Black women at the Black United Fund of Oregon (with Mehran Heard, 2014),
and a wall at Plaza Walls in Oklahoma City (2019). Clients include Port of
Portland, Oregon Zoo, Gerber Gear, Lexus, NW Natural, OMSI, and City of
Tigard. Festivals include SHINE 2019, Forest for the Trees (Portland), and
Canvas Miami.

Blaine Fontana was born in Seattle, Washington, raised on Bainbridge Island,
spent ten years in Los Angeles, and has lived in Portland for nearly twenty
years. He earned a BFA in Communication Art/Design from Otis College of Art
and Design (2002), receiving "Best in Show" for his senior thesis and later
named one of seven distinguished Otis alumni (2000-2010). He worked as art
director at Abound LLC, Metro.Pop magazine, and Drifter apparel before
going self-employed in January 2003. He runs Fontana Studios with his wife.
His multidisciplinary practice spans murals, fine art, illustration, graphic
design, sculpture, installations, and furniture, drawing on Asian
calligraphy, found objects, graffiti palettes, and wabi-sabi principles.
Clients include Nike, adidas, Patagonia, TEDx, VANS, Quiksilver, Kid
Robot, Upper Playground, Umpqua Bank, and Toyota. He has exhibited in New
York, LA, Tokyo, London, and many other cities. He published the monograph
"Amalgamate" through ZERO+ Publishing (covering 2002-2006). Festivals
include SHINE 2019, Forest for the Trees (Portland, 2013-2015), and
Colorado Crush.

For SHINE 2019 they collaborated on "The 8th Funky Fish Town" at Bama Sea
Products, 756 28th St S, St. Petersburg — approximately 25 feet tall by
175 feet wide, 100% spray paint, over 400 cans, completed in 8 days. It
was the largest SHINE mural of 2019. The project was a partnership with
PangeaSeed Foundation's Sea Walls: Artists for Oceans program and NOAA —
the first public art project in the US funded by NOAA. The mural highlights
small fishery towns of the Florida region and ocean fragility, inspired by
"iconic infographics found in classic school books." The artists researched
and visited local fisheries before painting. A panel discussion was held
October 26, 2019 at The Factory St. Pete with PangeaSeed founder Tre'
Packard, NOAA's Jeff Pulver, and Ocean Conservancy's Catherine Bruger.

Instagram: @plasticbirdie, @blainefontana. Websites: plasticbirdie.com,
thefontanastudios.com.

muralDescription: |
A massive mural spanning roughly twenty-five by a hundred and seventy-five
feet along the Pinellas Trail at Bama Sea Products. A stylized bald eagle
head dominates the right side — sharp blue eye and curved beak rendered in
warm yellows, oranges, and teal with mosaic-like geometric patterning. The
left side dissolves into abstract geometric triangles in teal, coral, mint,
lavender, and white, creating a crystalline fractured effect that
transitions from the figurative eagle into pure abstraction. The palette
shifts from cool teals and mints to warm golds and corals. The piece
highlights Florida fishery culture and ocean ecology through a graphic,
infographic-inspired style. It was the largest SHINE mural of 2019.

muralInspiration: "First US public art project funded by NOAA, created in partnership with PangeaSeed Sea Walls: Artists for Oceans."

### Mural Description
A massive mural spanning roughly twenty-five by a hundred and seventy-five
feet along the Pinellas Trail at Bama Sea Products. A stylized bald eagle
head dominates the right side — sharp blue eye and curved beak rendered in
warm yellows, oranges, and teal with mosaic-like geometric patterning. The
left side dissolves into abstract geometric triangles in teal, coral, mint,
lavender, and white, creating a crystalline fractured effect that
transitions from the figurative eagle into pure abstraction. The palette
shifts from cool teals and mints to warm golds and corals. The piece
highlights Florida fishery culture and ocean ecology through a graphic,
infographic-inspired style. It was the largest SHINE mural of 2019.

muralInspiration: "First US public art project funded by NOAA, created in partnership with PangeaSeed Sea Walls: Artists for Oceans."
