searchBio: |
Artist unknown. A mural on the side of Planet Retro Records in St.
Petersburg, FL. Yellow line-drawn characters on a dark gray background —
bearded figures wearing headphones, a turntable, and a "RECORDS" sign in
red neon-style lettering. The style is illustrative and music-themed. Not a
SHINE piece — likely a commercial commission for the record store. Note:
Derek Donnelly painted a nearby mural but this is a different piece.

muralDescription: |
Yellow line-drawn characters on a dark gray wall. On the left, a red
"RECORDS" sign and a figure with headphones and a yellow hat. At center, a
yellow vinyl record on a turntable. On the right, two bearded figures with
flowing yellow hair and headphones — their beards and hair rendered as
tangled line work. The entire piece uses only yellow, red, and gray/black,
giving it a graphic, illustrative quality that matches the record store
aesthetic.

## Derek Donnelly — Sonshine

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Instagram:** @derekdonnelly

### Bio
He painted a T-Rex in a Hawaiian shirt going fishing — for AARP. That tells
you most of what you need to know about Derek Donnelly.

A Pinellas County native, Donnelly grew up in Pinellas Park, graduated from
the Center for the Arts at Gibbs High School in 2001, and studied graphic
design and digital illustration at IADT Tampa. He went full-time on murals
in 2010. A year later he opened Saint Paint Arts gallery on the 600 block
of Central Avenue — not as a vanity project, but as a launchpad. Several
artists got their first real exposure there before he closed it in 2014 to
focus on walls.

His style sits at the intersection of illustration and realism — playful
without being childish, bold without being loud. His son Syre shows up in
murals across the city. "Sonshine" is likely one of those portraits — a
monochrome boy in sunglasses reflecting a warm sunset, set against a deep
starfield, near Planet Retro Records.

searchBio: |
Derek Donnelly — see full bio on his SHINE 2025 entries (ids 5, 6) and
SHINE 2018 entry (id 115). Pinellas County native, Gibbs High School
Center for the Arts (2001), IADT Tampa (graphic design / digital
illustration). Full-time muralist since 2010. Founded Saint Paint Arts
gallery (2011-2014), COVE gallery (Pinellas Park), Pinellas Arts Village
district, and Public Art Project Inc. nonprofit. Clients include Fender
Guitars, Tampa Bay Rays, Sirata Beach Resort, and AARP. Multiple Best of
the Bay wins. Inaugural Artmaker 2026 at Ruth Eckerd Hall. SHINE 2025
artist liaison and operations while producing two murals.

"Sonshine" is a commercial mural near Planet Retro Records in St.
Petersburg. A monochrome portrait of a young boy wearing sunglasses that
reflect a warm orange-gold sunset, set against a deep blue-black starfield.
Signed "Donnelly" in the upper right corner. Some paint peeling visible as
of April 2026. The boy may be his son Syre, who appears in murals across
the city.

Instagram: @derekdonnelly.

muralDescription: |
A monochrome portrait of a young boy fills the wall. He wears dark
sunglasses whose lenses reflect a warm orange-gold sunset — the only color
in the piece. His hair, skin, and clothing are rendered in shades of gray
and silver-blue against a deep blue-black starfield background dotted with
tiny white stars. The boy gazes slightly upward with a calm expression.
"Donnelly" is signed in the upper right. Some paint has peeled from the
cheek and forehead, revealing the wall beneath.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description

## Art of Slim — Dreams to Reality (2022)

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Instagram:** @artofslim

### Bio
When he finished his three-wall mural for Air Masters in Seminole Heights,
he started crying and hugged his dog.

Austin "Slim" Gee — Art of Slim — is a St. Petersburg-based spray paint
specialist and muralist. He held an artist-in-residence at Parc at Wesley
Chapel near Tampa for two years (2021-2022), where American Landmark
Apartments gave him free housing and studio space in exchange for community
art programming — his term was extended a second year due to popularity.
Before Tampa Bay he painted anti-litter murals in Columbia, South Carolina
for PalmettoPride.

His largest piece is a three-wall mural spanning the Air Masters warehouse
in Seminole Heights, depicting Tampa's aviation history, wildlife, sports,
and community — it took just over two months and Tampa Mayor Jane Castor
attended the ribbon-cutting in October 2025.

searchBio: |
When he finished his three-wall mural for Air Masters in Seminole Heights,
he started crying and hugged his dog.

Austin "Slim" Gee — Art of Slim — is a St. Petersburg-based spray paint
specialist, graphic designer, acrylic painter, and illustrator. His
Instagram bio reads "Spray Paint Specialist." He is a full-time mural
artist who accepts commissions. He has possible South Carolina roots (803
area code on his website).

He held an artist-in-residence at Parc at Wesley Chapel (American Landmark
Apartments, 5561 Post Oak Blvd, Wesley Chapel, Tampa, FL) for two years
(2021-2022), receiving no-cost housing and studio space in exchange for
community art programming including "Sketch and Sip" events and children's
art classes. His term was extended a second year due to popularity. He
painted a large-scale heron mural at the clubhouse inspired by local
wildlife.

Known murals include "Dreams to Reality" (2022, St. Petersburg — cartoon
kid on skateboard, commercial commission), the Air Masters of Tampa Bay
mural (unveiled October 2025, Seminole Heights, Tampa — spans three
warehouse walls depicting aviation history, wildlife, sports, and
community; took two months; Mayor Jane Castor attended ribbon-cutting),
"Saluda In Bloom" (March 2019, Candi Lane, Columbia, SC — anti-litter
awareness mural for One Columbia and PalmettoPride), and "Love Not Litter
Extension" (2020, same location, with Taylor Renee).

Clients include Air Masters of Tampa Bay, American Landmark Apartments,
One Columbia / PalmettoPride, and Palmetto Outdoor Center. He is a member
of several music festival production teams and arts alliances in Tampa Bay.

Instagram: @artofslim. Website: slimgee.com.

muralDescription: |
A cartoon boy in a blue baseball cap and light blue hoodie rides a
skateboard through a swirling purple vortex, arms outstretched, tongue
out in excitement. The skateboard has blue wheels and a smiley face sticker.
Paint splatters in teal, purple, pink, and green burst across the
background. "DREAMS TO REALITY" is lettered in bold white and purple at
the bottom left. Signed "@ArtOfSlim '22" in the lower right. A "No
Parking Any Time" sign is integrated into the left edge of the composition.
The style is animated and energetic — cartoon character design with graffiti
influences.

## Romero — City of STPETERSRUN

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Instagram:** @r2romero

### Bio
He paints portraits on actual cotton-paper banknotes using acrylic paint.
He has been doing it since 2015.

Arturo Romero — R2ROMERO — is a Mexican American artist originally from
Santa Ana, California, now based in St. Petersburg. He works across murals,
canvases, metal signs, and comic books, mixing fine art, cartoons, and
nature. His signature project "$1000 DOLLAR2" turns real banknotes into
miniature paintings — subjects range from the Sumerians and Enki the water
god to Sid Vicious and Fear and Loathing in Las Vegas.

He has painted in England, Spain, France, the Netherlands, Mexico, and
across the US. In St. Petersburg he participated in the Paint It Pink
project (2024-2025), transforming the former Ventnor Apartments with
twenty-plus local artists.

searchBio: |
He paints portraits on actual cotton-paper banknotes using acrylic paint.
He has been doing it since 2015.

Arturo Romero — R2ROMERO — is a Mexican American artist born and raised in
Santa Ana, California, now based in Saint Petersburg, Florida. He works in
murals, canvases, metal signs, comic books, and his signature banknote art
project "$1000 DOLLAR2" — painting portraits and scenes on actual
cotton-paper banknotes using acrylic paint since 2015. His style blends
fine art, cartoons, and nature with mystical and pop culture themes. Known
banknote subjects include The Sumerians (2023), ENKI the water God,
Chalino Sanchez (2022), Sid Vicious (2022), Fear and Loathing in Las Vegas
(2021), and Hunter S. Thompson & Oscar Zeta Acosta (2021).

He has worked in England, Spain, France, the Netherlands, Mexico, and the
United States. Known murals include "Miss Lure" at Osage Alley & 24th St
in San Francisco (with Goop Massta, 2016). In St. Petersburg he
participated in the Paint It Pink project (2024-2025) on the former Ventnor
Apartments at 4th St S & 4th Ave S. He is listed in the Creative Pinellas
artist directory.

"City of STPETERSRUN" is a surreal folk-art mural near Planet Retro
Records featuring a headless figure covered in symbols including "LORD
ENKI" and "11 33," a gramophone, a crocodile, a life buoy, and a pelican —
themes consistent with his Sumerian and mystical body of work.

Instagram: @r2romero. Website: r2romero.com.

muralDescription: |
A surreal composition on a light blue wall. A headless figure stands at
center, its body covered in hand-drawn symbols — an all-seeing eye, a
heart, palm trees, "LORD ENKI" across the torso, and the number "11 33."
The figure wears red shorts and sneakers and holds a globe with a heart
over its chest. Scattered around the scene: a gramophone playing a record
labeled "RETRO," a green crocodile, a life buoy reading "City of
STPETERSRUN," a pelican, a gloved hand with eye symbols, a "Private
Property" sign, and a boat. Signed "@romero" in the upper left. The style
is folk-art surrealist with a hand-drawn, tattoo-flash quality.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description

## Jujmo — Space Rainbows (2017)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 1701 1st Ave S
**Instagram:** @jujmo

### Bio
She grew up as a military kid moving between bases overseas with limited
access to cartoons. When she finally got to watch anime in the U.S., she
decided to become an artist.

Cheryl Weber — Jujmo — is a Filipina American artist based in Tampa. She
earned her BFA from the University of South Florida and started freelancing
immediately after graduation. Her style is "cute forward" — saturated
colors, no straight lines, happy intertwined worlds of strange creatures
influenced by anime, Japanese folklore, and Filipino folklore. She paints
on walls, helmets, shoes, tote bags, and cars.

She has ten-plus years of mural experience and painted the "B" in the
Black Lives Matter street mural in front of the Dr. Carter G. Woodson
Museum for Juneteenth 2020. She gave a lecture at the Imagine Museum in
2024 and was featured on WEDU Arts Plus (PBS). Her work is her true love:
"I want it to be your time to shine, just as much as mine."

searchBio: |
She grew up as a military kid moving between bases overseas with limited
access to cartoons. When she finally got to watch anime in the U.S., she
decided to become an artist.

Cheryl Weber — Jujmo (pronounced "jooj-moe") — is a Filipina American
artist based in Tampa, FL. She earned her BFA from the University of South
Florida (approximately 2013) and began freelancing immediately via email,
Instagram, and Facebook. Her style is self-described as "cute forward" with
saturated colors and heavy influence from anime, Japanese folklore, and
Filipino folklore. She is characterized by bright colors and the absence of
straight lines — happy intertwined worlds of fanciful creatures. She paints
on walls, helmets, shoes, tote bags, and cars.

SHINE credits: 2017 ("Space Rainbows," 1701 1st Ave S — 48 feet wide by
14 feet high) and 2021 (A-1 Recovery, 2221 5th Ave S, plus a week-long
mural education course for the Shirley Proctor Puller Foundation). She
painted the "B" in the Black Lives Matter street mural in front of the Dr.
Carter G. Woodson African American Museum for Juneteenth 2020. Other murals
include Black Radish Grocery and Love Food Central in St. Petersburg.

She gave a lecture at the Imagine Museum Artist Lecture Series (September
2024) and was featured on WEDU Arts Plus (PBS, Season 14 Episode 8). She
launched a free Instagram livestream drawing show during COVID-19. She
created a limited-edition design for WEDU PBS supporters.

Instagram: @jujmo. Website: jujmo.com.

muralDescription: |
A wide panoramic mural covering a commercial wall, filled edge to edge
with colorful abstract blob creatures and organic shapes. The palette
spans soft pinks, greens, teals, yellows, purples, and deep blues.
Playful characters with simple dot eyes and small mouths peek out from
amorphous cloud-like forms. Some shapes resemble mushrooms, coral, or
amoebas. The composition flows like an underwater garden or alien
landscape — dense, layered, and cheerful. Trees frame the mural on
both sides.

## Morgan Harper Nichols

**Category:** commercial
**Building:** Art Jewelry Boutique
**Instagram:** @morganharpernichols

### Bio
She started a project to write a million poems to strangers in her
lifetime. She has two million followers on Instagram.

Morgan Harper Nichols is a poet, author, and interdisciplinary media artist
born in Los Angeles in 1990, raised in Georgia, now based in Athens where
she is pursuing a PhD in Communication Studies at the University of Georgia.
She earned a BFA in English from Point University and an MFA in
Interdisciplinary Media Arts from Lindenwood University. In 2017 she began
writing personalized poems for strangers who submit their stories through
her website — brief letters of encouragement paired with visual art, sent
free and shared anonymously.

She has published four books with Zondervan/HarperCollins, including the
Wall Street Journal bestseller "All Along You Were Blooming." Clients
include Google, Apple, Adobe, Starbucks, COACH, and Hallmark. She was
diagnosed with autism at thirty-one.

searchBio: |
She started a project to write a million poems to strangers in her
lifetime. She has two million followers on Instagram.

Morgan Harper Nichols (born Morgan Novelate Harper, February 4, 1990, Los
Angeles) is a poet, author, and interdisciplinary media artist. Her family
moved to Georgia in 1993 where her parents started Kingdom City Church in
Stone Mountain. Her younger sister is Christian music artist Jamie Grace.
She was homeschooled growing up. She began attending Point University at
sixteen and earned a BA in English. She earned an MFA in Interdisciplinary
Media Arts from Lindenwood University (2024) and is pursuing a PhD in
Communication Studies at the University of Georgia (began August 2024).

In 2017 she began writing personalized poems for strangers — people submit
stories via her website and she writes brief letters of encouragement
paired with visual art, sent free and shared anonymously on social media.
She creates all words and art on an iPad Pro. Her approach is collage-based,
drawing from worldbuilding, assemblage, and remixing.

Books include "Storyteller: 100 Poem Letters" (2017, self-published), "All
Along You Were Blooming" (2020, Zondervan — Wall Street Journal
bestseller), "How Far You Have Come" (2021, Zondervan), "Peace Is a
Practice" (2021, Zondervan), and "You Are Only Just Beginning" (2023,
Zondervan). She signed a two-book contract with Zondervan in June 2020.

Clients include Google, Meta, Apple, Adobe, Starbucks, KIND Snacks, COACH,
Paul Mitchell, Skillshare, Hallmark, Instagram, Aerie, and Vogue Singapore.
Products found at Target, Barnes & Noble, and Anthropologie. She is Vice
President of the Board of Directors for TWLOHA (To Write Love on Her Arms).
She founded Garden24, an e-commerce platform and physical shop in Phoenix.
She launched the "Storyteller by MHN" app (2019) and "The Morgan Harper
Nichols Show" podcast (2019).

She was diagnosed with autism at thirty-one along with ADHD and sensory
processing disorder. She married Patrick Kekoa Nichols on October 17, 2010.
They have a son, Jacob.

Instagram: @morganharpernichols (approximately 2 million followers).
Website: morganharpernichols.com. Shop: garden24.co.

muralDescription: |
An octopus mural wrapping around the corner of a small commercial building
(Art Jewelry Boutique). The octopus tentacles are rendered in warm earth
tones — browns, rusts, and tans — studded with colorful concentric circles
in teal, pink, purple, yellow, and brown, like suction cups reimagined as
abstract eyeballs. A parchment scroll at center contains a handwritten poem
about inner beauty and strength, signed "Morgan Harper Nichols." The
circles continue onto the sidewalk in front of the building. "Affluent
Creatives" is painted at the base. The background is a muted sage green.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
An octopus mural wrapping around the corner of a small commercial building

circles continue onto the sidewalk in front of the building. "Affluent

## Lyko Creative (2025)

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Instagram:** @lyko.creations

### Bio
Zac "Lyko" Shaffer is a multi-media artist with roots in Detroit and St.
Petersburg. His work fuses street art, graffiti, and impressionism. In
August 2025 he was one of three core artists on the Love Thy Neighbor
Project — painting free inclusivity murals on private property in St.
Petersburg in response to Florida's street mural removals.

This Florida nature wall features a mockingbird, magnolia blossoms, a
golden orange, and zebra longwing butterflies on a sky-blue background.

searchBio: |
Zac "Lyko" Shaffer — Lyko Creations — is a multi-media artist based in St.
Petersburg, FL with connections to Detroit. His Instagram bio reads
"Multi-media artist with art from Detroit to St. Pete." His website tagline
is "Where the fusion of street art, graffiti, and impressionism come to
life." He offers original art, prints, apparel, and murals.

In August 2025 he was one of three core artists (with Jazzy Erickson and
Ceara Rae) on the Love Thy Neighbor Project, a community mural initiative
led by Michelle Sasha / ArtFluent Creatives. The project painted free
inclusivity murals on private properties in St. Petersburg in response to
Florida's street mural removals.

This mural was painted in 2025 — a Florida nature scene with a mockingbird,
magnolia blossoms, a golden orange, and zebra longwing butterflies. Not an
official SHINE Origins 2025 piece.

Instagram: @lyko.creations. Website: lykocreations.com.

muralDescription: |
A panoramic Florida nature scene on a blue cinder block wall. On the left,
a gray-and-white mockingbird perches on a dark branch. At center, green
magnolia leaves and a white magnolia blossom frame a large golden-orange
citrus fruit. On the right, two zebra longwing butterflies with black and
white striped wings flutter near the foliage. The palette is naturalistic —
sky blue background, rich greens, and warm gold — celebrating Florida's
native wildlife and flora.

muralInspiration: ""

white striped wings flutter near the foliage. The palette is naturalistic —
sky blue background, rich greens, and warm gold — celebrating Florida's
native wildlife and flora.

## Chad Mize

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Instagram:** @chadmize

### Bio
See Chad Mize's full bio on his other murals in this app — he has multiple
pieces across St. Petersburg.

This wall is a classic Mize doodle composition — hundreds of hand-drawn
icons packed edge to edge on a teal background: flamingos, octopus, UFOs,
smiley faces, sharks, mushrooms, VW buses, lightning bolts, and everything
in between.

searchBio: |
Chad Mize — see full bio on his other entries (ids 58, 77, 109, 116). This
is a commercial doodle wall in the Warehouse Arts District. Hundreds of
hand-drawn icons packed edge to edge on a teal background in his signature
style — flamingos, octopus, UFOs, smiley faces, sharks, dolphins,
mushrooms, VW buses, lightning bolts, pizza slices, jellyfish, anchors,
suns, moons, and countless other playful icons. Signed "Mize" in the upper
right corner.

muralDescription: |
A teal wall packed edge to edge with hundreds of hand-drawn doodle icons
in pink, blue, white, orange, and navy. The composition is a maximalist
collage of Florida and pop culture imagery: flamingos, octopus, dolphins,
sharks, jellyfish, a UFO, smiley faces, mushrooms, a VW bus, lightning
bolts, pizza slices, anchors, palm trees, suns, moons, an all-seeing eye,
a hot air balloon, starfish, seahorses, and countless other playful
figures. Two windows and a white door are incorporated into the design.
Signed "Mize" in the upper right. Small trees flank the base.

muralInspiration: ""

## Jujmo

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Instagram:** @jujmo

### Bio
She grew up as a military kid moving between bases overseas with limited
access to cartoons. When she finally got to watch anime in the U.S., she
decided to become an artist.

Cheryl Weber — Jujmo — is a Filipina American artist based in Tampa. She
earned her BFA from the University of South Florida and started freelancing
immediately after graduation. Her style is "cute forward" — saturated
colors, no straight lines, happy intertwined worlds of strange creatures
influenced by anime, Japanese folklore, and Filipino folklore.

This wall in the Warehouse Arts District is warm-toned blob creatures with
smiley faces — a different palette than her usual pastels, here in oranges,
teals, browns, and pinks. She has at least three walls across St. Pete.

searchBio: |
Cheryl Weber — Jujmo — see full bio on her SHINE 2017 entry (id 163).
Filipina American artist based in Tampa, BFA from University of South
Florida. "Cute forward" style with anime and Filipino folklore influences.
This is a commercial wall in the Warehouse Arts District — warm-toned blob
creatures with smiley faces in orange, teal, brown, and pink. Signed
"JUJMO" in the lower right. She also has SHINE 2017 ("Space Rainbows")
and SHINE 2021 walls in St. Petersburg.

Instagram: @jujmo. Website: jujmo.com.

muralDescription: |
A panoramic mural of warm-toned abstract blob creatures flowing diagonally
across the wall. The palette is autumnal — orange, teal, brown, pink, and
gold. Smiling blob faces with simple dot eyes and wide grins peek out from
amorphous organic shapes. Small flowers, sparkles, and circular forms dot
the composition. Signed "JUJMO" in the lower right corner. The style is
her signature candy-colored character abstraction, here in warmer tones
than her typical pastel palette.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A panoramic mural of warm-toned abstract blob creatures flowing diagonally

## Carrie Jadus — Are You My Mother?

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL

### Bio
See Carrie Jadus's full bio on her other murals in this app.

"Are You My Mother?" is a monochromatic blue-green rendering of a
hundred-dollar bill, the portrait replaced by hands cradling a newborn
baby. The serial number reads "AXΔ515 22ND ST S.O." — embedding the
address into the currency design. Located in the Warehouse Arts District.

searchBio: |
Carrie Jadus — see full bio on her other entries (ids 149, 150). "Are You
My Mother?" is a monochromatic blue-green mural in the Warehouse Arts
District depicting a hundred-dollar bill. The central portrait is replaced
by hands cradling a newborn baby. The serial number reads "AXΔ515 22ND ST
S.O." The piece reads "FEDERAL RESERVE NOTE," "THE UNITED STATES OF
AMERICA," "THIS IS LEGAL TENDER LIABLE FOR ALL DEBTS PUBLIC AND PRIVATE,"
and "ONE HUNDRED DOLLARS." The signature line reads "Are You My Mother?"
The rendering mimics the intricate engraving style of real US currency.

muralDescription: |
A monochromatic blue-green mural depicting an oversized hundred-dollar
bill. The central oval portrait — where Benjamin Franklin would be — is
replaced by a pair of hands gently cradling a curled-up newborn baby. The
bill's design faithfully mimics real currency: "FEDERAL RESERVE NOTE"
across the top, "THE UNITED STATES OF AMERICA" at right, "100" in all
four corners, and ornate scrollwork borders. The serial number reads
"AXΔ515 22ND ST S.O." The signature line reads "Are You My Mother?" The
entire piece is rendered in shades of teal, blue-green, and white,
matching the color of real US bills.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
A monochromatic blue-green mural depicting an oversized hundred-dollar
bill. The central oval portrait — where Benjamin Franklin would be — is
replaced by a pair of hands gently cradling a curled-up newborn baby. The
bill's design faithfully mimics real currency: "FEDERAL RESERVE NOTE"

"AXΔ515 22ND ST S.O." The signature line reads "Are You My Mother?" The
entire piece is rendered in shades of teal, blue-green, and white,
matching the color of real US bills.

## Dreamweaver

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** St. Petersburg, FL
**Address:** 415 20th St S
**Instagram:** @dream.weavin

### Bio
She painted her first mural at seven, on her aunt's backyard shed in Waco,
Texas. She has not stopped.

Taylor Smith — Dreamweaver — grew up between Portland, Oregon and just
south of Atlanta, born in Dallas. She studied communications and fine art
at Georgia College and State University, with a minor in graphic design,
graduating in 2017. She apprenticed at a tattoo shop at sixteen and spent
time doing live painting at music festivals — Electric Daisy Carnival,
Okeechobee, Electric Forest — before relocating to Florida around 2019.

She works almost entirely in spray paint, blending expressive abstraction
with realism. Nature runs through everything: wildlife, water, birds,
flora. She is left-handed. She has painted an estimated 120 to 150 murals,
15 to 20 in St. Pete alone. She is a resident artist at AER Tampa and a
member of Few and Far, an international women's graffiti crew.

This piece at 415 20th St S wraps the full front of the building in
swirling rainbow aerosol flames — flowing ribbons of orange, magenta,
teal, green, and purple against a dark background.

searchBio: |
Taylor Smith — Dreamweaver / @dream.weavin — born in Dallas, grew up
between Portland and south of Atlanta. BFA in Communications and Fine Art
from Georgia College and State University (2017), minor in graphic design.
Private painting lessons as a kid, apprenticed at a tattoo shop at 16.
Live painting at Electric Daisy Carnival (Vegas), Okeechobee, Electric
Forest. Relocated to Florida around 2019. Works almost entirely in spray
paint — expressive abstraction blended with realism, nature themes. Left-
handed. Estimated 120-150 murals total, 15-20 in St. Pete. Resident artist
at AER Tampa. Member of Few and Far international women's graffiti crew.
Also painted for SHINE 2022 (id 59) and SHINE 2025 (id 7). Other projects
include Clearwater Municipal Parking Garage, sea turtle mural at Coachman
Park for Art Oasis 2024, and "The Garden Within" in downtown Kissimmee for
Osceola Arts.

This commercial mural at 415 20th St S wraps the full front of a building
in swirling rainbow aerosol flames — flowing ribbons of orange, magenta,
teal, electric green, and deep purple against a dark purple-black
background.

Instagram: @dream.weavin.

muralDescription: |
Swirling ribbons of color wrap the entire front facade of a low commercial
building. Flowing aerosol flames in orange, magenta, hot pink, teal,
electric green, and deep purple twist and curl against a dark
purple-black background. The effect is liquid and luminous — like molten
rainbow glass in motion. The piece covers both sides of the building
symmetrically, framing a central window and entrance. Plants sit at the
base. The bold, fluid style is Dreamweaver's signature aerosol work.

muralInspiration: ""

### Mural Description
Swirling ribbons of color wrap the entire front facade of a low commercial
building. Flowing aerosol flames in orange, magenta, hot pink, teal,
electric green, and deep purple twist and curl against a dark
purple-black background. The effect is liquid and luminous — like molten
rainbow glass in motion. The piece covers both sides of the building
symmetrically, framing a central window and entrance. Plants sit at the
base. The bold, fluid style is Dreamweaver's signature aerosol work.

muralInspiration: ""

## Paola Delfín — La Emperatriz

**Category:** commercial
**Based in:** Mexico City, Mexico
**Address:** 1975 3rd Ave S
**Instagram:** @paola_delfin

### Bio
Her monochromatic women have appeared on walls from New Delhi to Wynwood
to Mexico City — always in shades of gray, always tangled in leaves.

Paola Delfín is a Mexican muralist based in Mexico City. She studied
graphic design at Universidad Iberoamericana and has been painting
large-scale monochromatic murals since around 2012. Her signature is
illustrative female figures intertwined with botanical elements — leaves,
flowers, dense foliage — rendered in black-and-white or gray-blue palettes
at monumental scale. The work explores human bonds, intimacy, femininity,
and emotional vulnerability.

She has painted walls across Mexico City, Guadalajara, Playa del Carmen,
New Delhi (for the St+Art India Foundation's Lodhi Art District), Lima,
Wynwood, Berlin, Barcelona, and Buenos Aires. She has been featured on
Colossal and in Juxtapoz.

"La Emperatriz" at 1975 3rd Ave S in St. Petersburg's Warehouse Arts
District is a monochromatic gray-blue mural of two women's faces emerging
from dense tropical foliage.

searchBio: |
Her monochromatic women have appeared on walls from New Delhi to Wynwood
to Mexico City — always in shades of gray, always tangled in leaves.

Paola Delfín is a Mexican muralist and illustrator based in Mexico City.
She studied graphic design at Universidad Iberoamericana in Mexico City
and has been active professionally since approximately 2012-2013. Her
signature is large-scale monochromatic murals — predominantly
black-and-white or gray-blue palettes — centered on the female form
intertwined with botanical elements. Recurring themes include human bonds,
intimacy, femininity, nature, and emotional vulnerability. The style blends
fine-art illustration with street art scale, often described as poetic and
delicate despite monumental size.

She has painted walls in Mexico City (Roma and Condesa neighborhoods),
Guadalajara, Playa del Carmen, New Delhi (Lodhi Art District for St+Art
India Foundation — one of her most internationally recognized pieces),
Lima (Barranco district), Wynwood (Miami), Berlin, Barcelona, and Buenos
Aires. Festival credits include SHINE Mural Festival (St. Petersburg),
St+Art India Foundation, and POW! WOW!. She has been featured on Colossal,
in Juxtapoz, and in Street Art News.

"La Emperatriz" at 1975 3rd Ave S in St. Petersburg's Warehouse Arts
District is a monochromatic gray-blue mural of two women's faces emerging
from dense tropical foliage — a hand reaching up through the plants
holding a flower.

Instagram: @paola_delfin. Website: paoladelfin.com.

muralDescription: |
A monochromatic mural in shades of gray and blue-gray covering the side
of a commercial building. Two women's faces emerge from dense tropical
foliage — the larger figure on the left wears a crown of flowers and
gazes directly at the viewer, while a smaller face appears to the right,
partially obscured. A hand reaches up through the plants on the far left,
holding a flower. The vegetation is lush and detailed — broad leaves,
stems, and blossoms rendered with illustrative precision. The entire piece
is monochromatic, giving it the quality of a pencil drawing or engraving
at massive scale.

muralInspiration: "Themes of unity, community, and the human bond."

### Mural Description
A monochromatic mural in shades of gray and blue-gray covering the side
of a commercial building. Two women's faces emerge from dense tropical
foliage — the larger figure on the left wears a crown of flowers and
gazes directly at the viewer, while a smaller face appears to the right,
partially obscured. A hand reaches up through the plants on the far left,
holding a flower. The vegetation is lush and detailed — broad leaves,
stems, and blossoms rendered with illustrative precision. The entire piece
is monochromatic, giving it the quality of a pencil drawing or engraving
at massive scale.

muralInspiration: "Themes of unity, community, and the human bond."

## Taj Tenfold — Sewing Seeds (2019)

**Category:** shine
**Based in:** Kingston, Jamaica
**Address:** 1720 Central Ave
**Instagram:** @tajfrancis

### Bio
He did the cover art for John Wick 4 and promotional artwork for HBO's
Lovecraft Country. He calls his style "visual reggae."

Taj Francis — Taj Tenfold — was born in 1989 in St. Andrew parish, Jamaica
and studied Illustration at the Edna Manley College of the Visual and
Performing Arts in Kingston (2007-2011). His work explores African ancestry
and human identity through a surreal lens, drawing on Japanese Manga,
hip-hop, Baroque painting, vintage vinyl art, and Jamaican Reggae
aesthetics.

Clients include Marvel Studios, DC Comics, HBO (Lovecraft Country),
Lionsgate (John Wick 4), Adobe, Harper Collins, and Audible. He is
represented by Bernstein & Andriulli for illustration and murals. He
shows at The Olympia Gallery in Kingston and held his debut solo exhibition
"The Night Sees" in February 2024.

For SHINE 2019 he was a lead artist, painting "Sewing Seeds" at 1720
Central Avenue — ninety feet long by fifteen feet tall. A woman's face
rises from a surging sea, her purple and magenta hair coiled in a rippling
orange scarf, the thread made of water repairing damage to the ocean.

searchBio: |
He did the cover art for John Wick 4 and promotional artwork for HBO's
Lovecraft Country. He calls his style "visual reggae."

Taj Francis — Taj Tenfold — was born in 1989 in the parish of St. Andrew,
Jamaica. He studied Illustration at the Edna Manley College of the Visual
and Performing Arts in Kingston (2007-2011). He is based in Kingston. His
work explores nuances of human behavior and identity relating to African
ancestry through a surreal lens. Influences include Japanese Manga, hip-hop,
Baroque paintings, vintage vinyl and poster art, Pop Art, and Jamaican
Reggae aesthetics. He works in paintings, digital art, murals, video art,
animation, illustration, design, and creative direction.

Commercial clients include HBO (Lovecraft Country), Marvel Studios, DC
Comics (cover artworks), Lionsgate (John Wick 4 cover), Adobe (Design
Creator Showcase), Audible ("SUNDOWN"), Harper Collins ("War Widow" cover),
Rocket League (Parallel Skin), and PURE Chocolate Jamaica. He is
represented by Bernstein & Andriulli (B&A) for illustration, painting,
murals, and commercial projects in the USA, UK, and Europe. He shows at
The Olympia Gallery in Kingston. His debut solo exhibition "The Night Sees"
opened February 17, 2024 at 10A, Kingston.

Festival credits include SHINE 2019 (St. Petersburg, lead artist), POW!
WOW! DC 2018 (double portrait mural at M St. NE & 3rd, Washington DC),
Paint Jamaica 2014 (two murals at 41 Fleet Street, Downtown Kingston), and
MANIFESTO Festival (Toronto, 2015).

For SHINE 2019 he painted "Sewing Seeds" at 1720 Central Avenue in St.
Petersburg — ninety feet long by fifteen feet tall. A woman's face and
shoulders rise from a surging sea, undulating curves of blue across her
face, purple and magenta hair coiled in a rippling orange scarf. The title
plays on "sewing" (cloth) — the thread is made of water, repairing damage
to the ocean and addressing sea rise.

Instagram: @tajfrancis (approximately 41K followers). Website:
tajtenfold.com.

muralDescription: |
A vibrant two-story mural spanning ninety feet. A woman's face dominates
the right side — rendered in shades of blue with bold features and a
direct gaze, her purple and magenta hair coiled in a rippling orange
scarf. To the left, a blue-toned figure appears to pull thread made of
water through surging waves. Swirling ribbons of pink, coral, blue, and
yellow flow across the composition like ocean currents. The palette is
jewel-toned Caribbean color — crimson, orange, purple, aqua, and royal
blue against a light background.

muralInspiration: "The thread is made of water, repairing damage to the ocean — addressing sea rise through the artist's Jamaican heritage."

### Mural Description
A vibrant two-story mural spanning ninety feet. A woman's face dominates
the right side — rendered in shades of blue with bold features and a
direct gaze, her purple and magenta hair coiled in a rippling orange
scarf. To the left, a blue-toned figure appears to pull thread made of
water through surging waves. Swirling ribbons of pink, coral, blue, and
yellow flow across the composition like ocean currents. The palette is
jewel-toned Caribbean color — crimson, orange, purple, aqua, and royal
blue against a light background.

muralInspiration: "The thread is made of water, repairing damage to the ocean — addressing sea rise through the artist's Jamaican heritage."
